Volati · VOLO-SE · the new Memo conveyor's card of 25 October 2026

What the Investment Analyst wrote behind each of the eleven ratings: Volati

The Investment Analyst's answers to the 101 questions and its 13 group judgements, 50,637 words, set out under the eleven ratings: each rating, then each of its lettered sub-items, then the answers beneath.

Word for word. Every answer is the Investment Analyst's text exactly as written; a script checked that every word is on this page, in order. What is changed is layout only: answers are grouped by rating rather than by layer; each answer is titled with the question as it was set (the Investment Analyst's own heading appears beneath only where it adds words); an answer that belongs under more than one sub-item is shown in full once, at its first place, and as a one-line link everywhere else.

Which answer sits where comes from the same table the ratings map uses. Each answer carries its layer and group, because "positive" means something different in each layer. Each layer's own material (what it was asked, what its scores mean, the Investment Analyst's notes and appendices) is at the end, so nothing is left out.

The ratings alone: the ratings map.

How to read the scores and letters

The Investment Analyst ended each answer with a score from 1 to 100: 100 is the most positive for an investment case, 1 the most negative, and 50 neither. What "positive" refers to differs by layer, so each layer states its own meaning (listed just below).

Where the Rater has scored this stock (every memo from 3 November 2026), the number in each pill is the Rater's, set after the memo was written from the Investment Analyst's judgements against a key per group of questions; the Investment Analyst's own score is kept in the pill's tooltip. The letter beside each score is read from the one canonical source for ratings, the same file the eleven ratings page and the memo scorecards read; nothing is graded on this page. The letter follows the item's rule: for most items this stock's rank among the stocks scored on the same scale on that same question or group judgement, re-cut each night (Richard's ruling of 4-Nov-26: every letter by rank; in the file's words: A above 90%; B+ above 82.5% to 90%; B above 75% to 82.5%; C+ above 66.67% to 75%; C above 58.33% to 66.67%; C- above 50% to 58.33%; D above 25% to 50%; F up to 25%); on the External change forces and Internal change forces items the number carries a sign (50 = neither positive nor negative) and the letter is the rank, so read the two together. “37th of 56” is the stock's place among them; a dashed outline marks a provisional grade (fewer than 30 stocks scored).

What the scores mean in each layer

Layer 1

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

Layer 2

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

Layer 3 and Layer 4

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

Inputs

1

External headwinds/tailwinds

–not yet rated

Memo topic 1, : the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

External change forces

Layer 1 · External change forces

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group. The rating above stands for it, so it carries no number of its own.

The external forces are net negative and not yet turning.

The centre of gravity is a cyclical trough in Swedish construction and European farm investment. Those end markets sit behind S:t Eriks and Tornum, which earned roughly 40% to 50% of RemainCo's EBITA in normal years and close to nothing in H1 2026. The rate cycle has just turned up, which postpones the recovery and raises Volati's financing cost at the same time. Grain-storage pricing is in a capacity-driven price war. Input costs (energy, steel, cement) are outrunning pass-through where pricing power is weakest.

Against this, Ettiketto (about 55% of platform EBITA in 2025) and Communication face steady to improving demand. Comparisons become easy from Q1 2027.

For the model, take these points forward:

  • Do not assume any end-market-driven recovery at S:t Eriks and Tornum before H2 2027.
  • Treat H1 2027 year-on-year improvement as mostly mechanical: easy comparisons, cost run-rates, production realignment.
  • Recognise that part of Ettiketto's 2026 Swedish volume is likely flattered by the food VAT cut.

The external picture improves decisively only if Swedish rates fall and housing starts rise. Neither is visible on a six-month horizon.

1a

External headwinds/threats

6 answers
C-0232C-0535C-0725C-0940C-3558C-3940
C-02

To what extent, and in which direction, is competitive intensity changing?

F3218th of 19

Layer 1 · External change forces

Judgement. Competitive intensity is rising, and it is rising precisely where Volati is weakest: grain storage and standard concrete products. Labels stay benign. The market for acquiring founder-owned businesses, the input to Volati's model, is also getting more crowded.

Reasoning.

  • Grain handling. A deep capital-spending freeze has pushed suppliers into aggressive discounting. Tornum's Q2 2026 EBITA margin fell from 7% to 2%. The global leader in grain storage, Kepler Weber, reported H1 2026 revenue down 25.4% and gross margin down 3.7 points to 16.1%, and blamed excess silo capacity built during the 2020 to 2023 commodity boom. This is overcapacity that has to exit before pricing recovers; it is not a short-term bidding skirmish. Tornum's response (project selectivity, walking away from low-margin bids, in-house silo manufacturing through SIMEZA) protects price integrity but gives up volume.
  • Concrete. Customers are pushing back on high-volume standard products, and S:t Eriks is shifting to specialised, lower-capital lines. The cost is volume loss and under-absorption. Distance and specifications limit foreign entry, so pressure comes from local rivals (Benders, Starka), not imports.
  • Labels. Pricing is rational and cost pass-through works: CCL Industries reported a record Q2 2026 on pass-through pricing. Ettiketto's margin fell from 19% to 15% in Q2 because of the lower-margin mix it acquired with Interket, not because it lost pricing.
  • Market for acquisition targets. New acquirers (Momentum Group, Röko, Tången, Auroora, InstallatørGruppen) and established ones expanding into Germany (Lifco, Sdiptech, Indus) are crowding the field. Experts cite entry multiples rising from 6x to 8x EBITA to 8x to 10x EBITDA.

Next six months. Grain-storage pricing stays poor until capacity leaves the market. Volati's balance sheet is the weakest in that contest.

C-05

How are input costs, input availability and supplier concentration changing, and is the net effect a headwind or a tailwind?

F3518th of 19

Layer 1 · External change forces

Judgement. A moderate net headwind for the next two to three quarters. The cost of energy, steel, cement and paper is rising faster than Volati can reprice in two of its five platforms. Availability is broadly restored after the Q2 2026 label-material shortage.

Reasoning.

  • Paper and adhesives (Ettiketto). Middle East shipping disruption caused acute shortages in early Q2 2026, which restricted deliveries until late June. Price increases were implemented and take "full effect" in H2 2026, so the margin recovery is lagged by one to two quarters. The upstream paper mills are concentrated and powerful: the 2022 UPM strike cost Ettiketto SEK 25m of sales.
  • Cement and aggregates (S:t Eriks). Cement is the single most costly and carbon-intensive input, with about 52,000 tonnes used in 2025. Supply is concentrated (Cementa, Heidelberg Materials) and exposed to quarry-permit risk (the Slite precedent) and ETS pass-through.
  • Steel (Tornum, Communication). Costs are up on EU safeguards. Tornum's in-house SIMEZA silos reduce dependence on outside suppliers but not on steel prices.
  • Energy and freight. Brent at about USD 111 and Middle East routing raised oil and shipping costs. The provisional Q3 report says these costs "prevented any meaningful margin expansion at S:t Eriks and Tornum". Freight had been normalised through 2025.
  • Wages. Swedish and Central European wage growth of about 3% to 4% (up to 7.9% in some surveys) is sticky against falling volumes.
  • Currency. The pre-spin analysis (a net USD purchase deficit, no forward contracts at end-2025) no longer describes RemainCo. Much of the NOK and DKK exposure left with Salix, while Interket (UK, Netherlands), Clever (Germany), Tramex (Ireland, United States) and SIMEZA (Spain) add EUR, GBP and USD. The exposure needs re-estimating, but it is a second-order line.

Net effect. A headwind. Pass-through works at Ettiketto with a lag; in a downturn it barely works at S:t Eriks and Tornum.

C-07

To what extent is a macro force acting on the company, on its sector and on its industry, and at which level does it bite hardest?

F2519th of 19

Layer 1 · External change forces

Judgement. The rate cycle is the dominant macro force and it bites hardest at the company level. Volati combines three exposures to rates that compound one another: rate-sensitive end demand (Swedish housing, farm investment), floating-rate debt plus expensive fixed preference capital, and an equity worth only about 43% of enterprise value, so any change in multiple is magnified. At sector level, Swedish serial-acquirer multiples are "hostage" to the gap between their earnings yield and swap rates (DNB Carnegie, October 2026). At industry level (capital goods), the regime is risk-off but the Industry tilt is Neutral.

Reasoning.

  • Rates. US 10-year yield near 5.10%, a Fed hike priced for December, European swap rates up 70bp over August and September, the ECB priced for 72bp more tightening to September 2027 (Bernstein), and a Riksbank decision in November 2026 with a hiking bias after holding at 1.75%. Each 100bp on Volati's floating net debt of about SEK 1.4bn costs about SEK 14m pre-tax (company disclosure, pre-spin: SEK 17m post-tax on the larger pre-spin debt).
  • Energy. Brent at USD 111 keeps inflation and central banks firm and lifts input costs (C-05).
  • Growth. Swedish GDP was strong in Q2 (+1.4% quarter on quarter), German machinery is weak, and European manufacturing PMIs are recovering (Eurozone 52.9, Sweden 58.1 in September). The short-cycle industrial recovery helps peers with automation exposure far more than Volati, whose cycles are late (construction, agriculture).
  • Regime. Risk-off is confirmed and broadening. The Fitness for Fighting tilts are Fragility Double Underweight and Momentum Underweight. Volati is small, leveraged, multi-part and in a downtrend, and so fights both.
C-09

What other material external dynamic, positive or negative, is at work?

–40

Layer 1 · External change forces

Judgement. Three further dynamics matter: a negative post-spin shareholder rotation and liquidity effect, an unhelpful comparison with Salix, and a modest positive in the broadening of sell-side coverage.

Reasoning.

  • Post-spin rotation (negative). RemainCo's market value is now only about SEK 1.9bn, with average daily trading of USD 0.3m to 0.4m. Holders who owned Volati for Salix or for the compounder story have reason to sell the smaller, more cyclical remainder; index weights also fall. The share is down about 15% to 18% since it first traded post-spin, consistent with this classic spin-off pattern. Low liquidity magnifies moves both ways.
  • Comparison with Salix (negative for perception). Salix reported agriculture EBITA up 29% and home and fittings EBITA up 21% in Q2 2026. The sector and industry notes read the separation as "crystallising underperformance at Volati, which handed out its best business", and that framing will stick until RemainCo shows growth.
  • Coverage (positive). Nordea Equity Research appears to have initiated with a Buy and a SEK 39 target on 2 October 2026, so coverage is no longer a single, conflicted broker. DNB Carnegie had withdrawn in May 2026 because it advised on the Salix listing, then reinstated at Hold.
  • Weather (random). Autumn and winter flooding decides Corroventa's H2. Winter frost affects S:t Eriks' Q1 deliveries.
  • Geopolitics. The Middle East conflict disrupts Tornum's export order signing and label-material shipping; Ukraine reconstruction is a deferred positive for Tornum.
C-35

To what extent are new entrants (from adjacent markets; standard entrants; orthogonal or innovative entrants) entering or threatening this market, and how much of a threat is each type?

C587th of 19

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement. The threat from new entrants is low to moderate overall. Low-cost adjacent hardware in grain storage is the only real one. Standard entrants are blocked by qualification, freight and service barriers. Orthogonal entrants are absent.

Reasoning.

  • Adjacent. Lower-cost silo and equipment producers (Latin American and Asian capacity from the boom) add to the grain price war. In labels, direct-to-container printing technology providers could become adjacent entrants over a decade.
  • Standard. Low: certification, municipal specifications and local plant networks block foreign concrete entrants (Sweco expert). Label qualification and machine integration block new converters. Corroventa's fleet scale is hard to copy.
  • Orthogonal or innovative. None found. Low-Earth-orbit satellites do not threaten masts in dense networks. AI does not threaten these businesses.
C-39

Where relevant or material, how are suppliers behaving: a support to the company, or a source of risk?

F4015th of 19

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement. Suppliers are a source of moderate risk, not support. They are concentrated (paper mills, cement producers), they pass on regulatory and energy costs, and they have caused supply shocks (the 2022 UPM strike, the 2026 Middle East shortages). Decentralised purchasing and multi-sourcing of standard metals and components mitigate the risk partly.

Reasoning.

  • Major paper mills and multinational cement suppliers "hold substantial pricing leverage" over regional converters and precast producers.
  • Cement depends on quarry permits (the Cementa Slite precedent).
  • Steel prices are pushed up by EU safeguards.
  • Mitigants: subsidiaries keep multiple suppliers for standard metals and components, build inventory when early shortage signals appear, and Tornum has brought silo manufacturing in-house.
  • Decentralised procurement "limits group-level purchasing power" against larger peers such as Indutrade or Lifco.
  • Freight, normalised in 2025, is rising again with oil.
1b

External tailwinds

7 answers
C-0128C-0355C-0440C-0835C-3235C-3740C-4355
C-01

To what extent is end-market demand changing, structurally or cyclically, and with how much visibility? (A cyclical swing counts when it has two-plus years of visibility.)

F2819th of 19

Layer 1 · External change forces

Judgement. Demand is in a deep cyclical trough, partly structural, in the end markets behind roughly half of RemainCo's normal-year EBITA: Swedish construction for S:t Eriks, European farm capital spending for Tornum, and weather-driven restoration for Corroventa. Demand in the other half (Ettiketto, Communication) is steady to improving. There is no two-year visibility of a recovery. The last six months were worse than the prior 12 to 18 at Tornum, no better at S:t Eriks, better at Communication and normal at Ettiketto. The next six months are more likely flat to weaker than better. Rates have turned up (a Riksbank hiking bias, European Central Bank rises priced into 2027, European swap rates up 70bp over August and September 2026), and that delays the one recovery the case most needs, Swedish construction.

Reasoning by platform.

  • S:t Eriks (cyclical, with a structural backlog of unmet housing need). Swedish building is in its deepest slump since the early 1990s, with volumes 25% to 30% below peak. Housing starts are a fraction of normal, though the sources differ on the numbers: about 8,000 a year against 18,000 in 2021 in one, about 25,000 against a long-term need of 60,000 in another. Infrastructure has been the buffer: national transport plans and rail and electrification investment. But water and wastewater demand began slowing in Q2 2026 as municipalities deferred pipe replacement. Management itself told DNB that tough conditions would persist through the rest of 2026. The unmet housing need is a reservoir for a later recovery, not a visible one.
  • Tornum (cyclical, with a structural policy drag). Low grain and pulp prices, high diesel and fertiliser costs and delayed EU grant payments have frozen farm investment. Sales fell 37% in Q1 2026, 8% in Q2 and, on the provisional Q3 figure, 17% to 20%. The post-2027 Common Agricultural Policy proposals (a potential 30% real cut in funding) and the Nordic nitrogen rules from 2027 are slow structural headwinds. Ukraine reconstruction is a long-dated tailwind with no date attached. The Lantmännen project (SEK 500m, over half of Tornum's annual sales) has rolled off and has not been replaced.
  • Corroventa (random, with a mild structural tailwind). 2025 was one of the driest years; Q2 2026 sales fell 14%. Climate change raises flood frequency over time, but no single year can be forecast.
  • Ettiketto (stable, partly flattered). Swedish fast-moving consumer goods and nicotine-pouch label demand returned to "good levels". But Swedish food retail volumes rose 5.3% in September 2026 after a value-added tax cut in April 2026 (Danske Bank), and food inflation is expected back by year-end. Part of 2026's Swedish label demand is therefore likely a tax-driven one-off, and the base effect turns against Ettiketto in 2027.
  • Communication (lumpy, with improving niches). Sales fell 9% in Q1 2026 and rose 25% in Q2 on Asian project deliveries, defence, rail and airport demand and US base-station orders. US 5G rollout stays slow. Demand is real but driven by projects, so it does not set a reliable run-rate.

Visibility. Low. Backlogs are short. Tornum's order book being "slightly stronger than last year" coexisted with a further fall in Q3 sales.

C-03

To what extent is a disruption force strengthening or being disproved, and in which direction does it act on the company?

D5511th of 19

Layer 1 · External change forces

Judgement. The disruption forces at work are mostly gradual technology shifts that, on balance, mildly favour Volati's platforms. None is a material driver of the next 18 months.

Reasoning.

  • Digital and hybrid label presses, shorter runs (strengthening, positive). Smaller flexographic converters cannot fund the move to digital, which speeds consolidation towards scaled buyers such as Ettiketto, whose "Ettiketto Way of Working" upgrades the presses of the converters it acquires.
  • Direct-to-container printing and RFID smart labels (strengthening slowly, a long-term substitution risk). These could erode pressure-sensitive label volumes over a decade. They also create high-margin niches for early adopters.
  • Low-carbon cement (strengthening, mildly positive). S:t Eriks uses evoZero (up to 80% lower carbon impact) and is partnering with Cemvision on a cement alternative (up to 95% lower). EU ETS free-allowance phase-out is expected to make 25% to 30% of European clinker capacity unviable by 2030. That favours early movers in precast but raises cement costs.
  • Internet-of-things remote drying (Corroventa SuperVision 2.0) points towards a recurring, software-enabled service model. It is positive but small.
  • Low-Earth-orbit satellite internet as a substitute for masts is largely disproved for dense urban data traffic.
  • Artificial intelligence is neutral for these physical niche businesses.
C-04

To what extent is political or regulatory change under way, and how does it bear on the company?

F4017th of 19

Layer 1 · External change forces

Judgement. A great deal of policy is changing. Its net effect over 6 to 18 months is mildly negative, concentrated at Tornum, with partial offsets at S:t Eriks (infrastructure) and Ettiketto (packaging regulation that favours scaled converters).

Reasoning.

  • Negative.
  • EU agricultural policy after 2027: a potential 30% real cut in funding, rural-development funds removed, and Danish and Nordic nitrogen rules from 2027. These prolong Tornum's customers' investment freeze, on top of the delayed grant payments already hurting.
  • EU steel safeguards from 1 July 2026 (low-cost imports cut by about 50%) and CBAM raise steel input costs for Tornum and Communication.
  • EU ETS pass-through raises cement costs for S:t Eriks.
  • US tariffs (Section 232: 25% on general engineering components, up to 50% on certain metal fabrications) cloud Communication's and Tornum's exports.
  • Mixed. The EU Packaging and Packaging Waste Regulation has applied directly since 12 August 2026 (technical documentation, declarations of conformity, PFAS restrictions on food-contact packaging). It raises compliance costs at Ettiketto's European plants, but it pushes brand owners to re-engineer labels and penalises small converters that cannot comply. Recyclability-graded producer-responsibility fees arrive later.
  • Positive. Sweden's national transport plan (SEK 1,171bn, Trafikverket) underpins S:t Eriks' infrastructure products. The Swedish ROT renovation deduction gives minor support. The April 2026 food VAT cut temporarily lifts Swedish food volumes and so Ettiketto's food labels (see C-01). Volati's bank loans carry sustainability-linked terms tied to S:t Eriks' concrete carbon targets: a small incentive, and a small risk.
C-08

To what extent is a key driver at the industry or sector level accelerating the company's demand?

F3519th of 19

Layer 1 · External change forces

Judgement. Very little. The drivers lifting the industry and sector (the short-cycle industrial upturn, electrification and data centres, defence, the "scarce box" capacity stories, repaired acquirers buying again) mostly bypass Volati's end markets. The partial exceptions are defence, rail and airport communications at Communication, infrastructure spending at S:t Eriks, and packaging regulation for Ettiketto.

Reasoning.

  • The Industry Digest's six live stories (data-centre power, conversion of order books, the China line, subtraction, law-dated demand, the short-cycle turn) place Volati only in "subtraction", and on the wrong side: it handed out its best business.
  • The Sector Digest's reset story (repaired balance sheets buying again produce unpriced upgrades for 12 to 24 months) names Volati as "the control case" where leverage stopped the engine.
  • Communication benefits from defence and critical-infrastructure demand and some US base-station orders. Its Q2 2026 sales rose 25% and DNB raised its 2026e EBITA by 14% to SEK 83m. But Communication is about a sixth of group sales and its demand is lumpy.
  • S:t Eriks' infrastructure products benefit from multi-year transport plans, but this is a buffer, not an accelerator.
C-32

How is customer demand behaving: to what extent does it support the thesis, and how fragile is that support?

F3518th of 19

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement. Customer demand does not yet support a recovery thesis. It supports only an Ettiketto-plus-self-help thesis, and even that support is moderately fragile. Demand in construction and agriculture is still falling or flat. Ettiketto's demand is steady but partly flattered in 2026. Communication's demand comes in project lumps.

Reasoning. The recovery thesis needs construction and farm customers to resume investment. Tornum's customers keep deferring (provisional Q3 sales down 17% to 20%; the Middle East conflict pausing export contract signings; EU grant delays). S:t Eriks' private builders are delaying starts, and municipalities have begun delaying water and wastewater upgrades. Corroventa's restoration contractors need floods. Ettiketto's fast-moving consumer goods and nicotine-pouch customers show high retention, and no customer exceeds 10% of group sales. But the Swedish food VAT boost will fade in 2027, and Interket's mix is more price-sensitive. Customer concentration is low at group level and high at Tornum (Lantmännen was over 50% of Tornum's sales in 2025).

C-37

What are regulators and political actors doing, does it help or hurt the company, and how fragile is that position?

F4018th of 19

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement. On balance they hurt modestly (see C-04). The single most material regulatory threat is EU agricultural policy from 2028 for Tornum's customers. The most material support is Swedish infrastructure investment for S:t Eriks. The position is fragile for Tornum and robust elsewhere.

Reasoning. CAP proposals cut real farm support by up to 30% and remove rural-development funds. Nitrogen rules restrict farm acreage from 2027. Grant payments have been slow. Together these extend Tornum's trough into 2027 and 2028. Sweden's national transport plan and security-of-supply investment support S:t Eriks' infrastructure volume. Packaging regulation burdens and favours Ettiketto at once. Swedish ROT renovation subsidies are a minor cushion. ESG-linked financing terms put mild pressure on S:t Eriks' decarbonisation. The UK's environmental land-management schemes (funding rising from £800m to £2.0bn by 2028) are a small plus for any UK farm exposure.

C-43

How large is the realistic total addressable market relative to the company, and what rate of growth does it allow for over the mid term?

D5512th of 19

Layer 1 · Company - Competitive advantage

Judgement. The addressable markets are large relative to Volati and do not constrain growth. Realistic mid-term organic growth across the portfolio is about 2% to 4% a year in normal markets (higher in Ettiketto and Communication, lower in Tornum). Acquired growth of 4% to 8% a year is possible if capital allows. The binding constraint is capital, not market size.

Reasoning.

  • Organic. Volati's own annual report claims addressable demand supporting organic volume growth of 3% to 5% above Nordic GDP. That is optimistic against its 2023 to 2025 record of negative organic growth, and against agricultural and construction markets growing at about GDP or below over a cycle (technical installation markets are forecast at about 2% a year to 2030 in the Indus work).
  • Acquired. European label converting is fragmented, with consolidation runway in Germany, the UK and the Netherlands. The Nordics have about 15,000 targets; the former Salix CFO estimated the addressable acquisition market at about USD 5bn across Northern Europe and the Baltics. DNB models 6% acquired growth a year for 2027 to 2032, against 8% to 10% for the premier acquirers.
  • Platform ceilings. Corroventa's restoration market is narrow; Tramex widens it to moisture measurement worldwide. Tornum's European market of EUR 1.3bn to 2.0bn is mature.
1c

Overall external picture

2 answers
C-0638C-1027
C-06

Where is the company's own revenue and demand cycle, and to what extent is it turning?

F3817th of 19

Layer 1 · External change forces

Judgement. Group EBITA is at or near its trough. LTM EBITA is SEK 262m at 5.9%, against SEK 472m at 10.5% in 2023. H1 2026 was probably the margin low. Turning has started only in parts, not group-wide: Ettiketto and Communication are growing, and S:t Eriks has finished its inventory clearance. Tornum is still falling and Corroventa depends on autumn weather.

Reasoning.

  • Revenue. Organic growth of about -5% (2023, legacy perimeter), about -7% (2024), -4% (2025, RemainCo), -6% (Q1 2026) and +2% (Q2 2026). The Q2 turn came from Communication's project deliveries and easier comparisons, not from the cyclical platforms. The provisional Q3 Tornum figure of -17% to -20% says the agricultural trough is not over.
  • Margin. S:t Eriks ran its plants below demand in H1 2026 to release inventory, which created under-absorption (Q1 EBITA SEK -16m; Q2 margin 5% against 12%). Production was to be realigned with orders in H2 2026, and restructuring savings reach full run-rate in H2. These are mechanical, not demand-driven, sources of recovery. The provisional Q3 statement that cost inflation prevented meaningful margin expansion at S:t Eriks and Tornum suggests the mechanical recovery is being partly absorbed.
  • Comparisons. Easy from Q1 2027 (Tornum -37%, S:t Eriks EBITA SEK -16m, Corroventa weak in Q1 2026), so year-on-year optics will improve in H1 2027 even without end-market recovery.
C-10

Taking each sector and industry thematic driver in turn, and then all of the above together: how strong are the external headwinds and how strong are the tailwinds at value-chain, industry and sector level, and what is the overall external change picture?

F2718th of 19

Layer 1 · External change forces

Judgement. The headwinds are clearly stronger than the tailwinds at all three levels. The overall external picture is a late-cycle trough in which the rate cycle has turned against the recovery. The tailwinds (Ettiketto's consolidation and pass-through, Communication's niches, easy comparisons from Q1 2027) are real but cannot carry a group in which two platforms are in cyclical troughs and the balance sheet magnifies everything.

Sector thematic drivers (Diversified industrial groups), taken one at a time.

  1. "A break-up re-rates the parent only when what leaves is worse than what stays." Volati is the counter-example: a headwind for its narrative.
  2. "Consensus models only businesses already owned, so a repaired balance sheet buying again is a stream of unpriced upgrades." Volati's balance sheet is not repaired; leverage is at the top of its range, so this is not available until 2027 at the earliest. A headwind.
  3. "A serial acquirer's multiple is the market's guess at how long the engine runs, and organic growth is its evidence." Organic growth was -4% in 2025 and -2% in H1 2026, with the engine constrained. A headwind.
  4. "Rates are the warning sign for every compounder." Rates are rising. A headwind.

Industry thematic drivers (Industrials and capital goods).

  1. "In a short-cycle turn the cheap, geared names with costs cut and the newly lit acquirers win." This is the one potential tailwind: Volati is cheap on normalised earnings, geared and has cut costs. But its cycles are late, not short.
  2. "A fence or a specification gives price; a mandate gives tendered volume." Packaging regulation and Sweden's transport plan help a little; agricultural policy hurts.
  3. "Outside the fence only the service network defends profit." This partly fits Corroventa and Ettiketto's machinery service.

Value chain. Strong headwinds in demand (S:t Eriks, Tornum, Corroventa), competition (grain, standard concrete) and inputs (energy, steel, cement). Moderate tailwinds at Ettiketto and Communication.

Environment. Risk-off, defensives leading, Fragility Double Underweight and Momentum Underweight. Volati fights the environment on every axis.

Internal change forces/risks

–not yet rated

Memo topic 2, : the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

Internal change forces

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group. The rating above stands for it, so it carries no number of its own.

Volati is mid-way through a large internal change, but the change is mostly subtraction and repair, not addition.

The separation removed the best business. What remains is being reorganised into five transparent platforms with new, more operational leaders, cost programmes and an integration push at Ettiketto. These self-help levers are worth perhaps SEK 60m to 100m of EBITA by 2027, and they are the main reason 2027 earnings should rise even without a market recovery.

But the changes are thinly resourced. Leverage is at the top of the range, preference capital costs SEK 64m a year, the dividend is about to be cut, and the latest acquisitions were bought on full multiples and will dilute margins until integration succeeds. The founders and the unchanged CEO keep the capital-allocation approach as it was, and they control the votes.

For the model, take these points forward:

  • Model the self-help (cost run-rates in H2 2026, Interket and Clever margin progress through 2027 to 2028, the full-year effect of Tramex, central costs) as the main driver of the 2026 to 2027 EBITA step-up.
  • Keep acquisitions close to zero until the leverage on the company's definition is clearly below 2.5x.
  • Treat a further disposal as optionality, not a base case.
2a

Internal risks

6 answers
Judgement20C-1448C-8628C-5945C-8125D-0345
Group judgement

Risks

F2019th of 19

Layer 1 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The risk profile is dominated by one fact. A leveraged balance sheet, with preference capital that behaves like debt, sits on top of cyclical earnings that are still falling in two platforms, inside a market regime that punishes exactly this profile.

The core growth engine has decelerated on both organic and acquired measures. The input forces have not turned. Sudden operating shocks (integration failure, impairment, a project loss, a control lapse) are individually low to medium in probability, but each would push leverage towards the 3.5x maximum, which is where the high-magnitude outcomes (dilution, a fire-sale disposal) begin. Sentiment risk is high: rates, sector short-sellers, illiquidity and post-spin selling.

A fair trough is about SEK 13 to 15, roughly 37% to 45% below the current price. The second set should do two things:

  • Run its model with an explicit leverage path and covenant-headroom check under a bear case of no recovery until 2028.
  • Treat the downside as fatter-tailed than the operating sensitivities alone suggest.
C-14

To what extent are standards improving in execution, operational measures, governance or cost discipline? (choices and actions)

F4819th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. Standards are improving in cost discipline, working capital and disclosure, alongside visible evidence of past control weakness. On balance they are modestly improving from a low base.

Reasoning.

  • Improving.
  • Cost programmes at S:t Eriks and Tornum began in Q1 2026, with restructuring charges taken in Q2 and full run-rate due in H2 2026 (management guided six to nine months to take effect).
  • Inventory release lifted Q2 operating cash flow to SEK 163m (from SEK 105m).
  • Segment disclosure went from three to five areas, a new CFO arrived from a larger industrial group, and KPMG replaced EY as auditor.
  • Margin-first commercial behaviour at Tornum and S:t Eriks.
  • Warrant programmes are priced at market, with total dilution of 1.34%.
  • Weak or deteriorating.
  • A raw-material inventory valuation error at S:t Eriks had built up since 2016 and needed a SEK 19m inventory reduction (SEK 15m off retained earnings). It was self-identified, but it shows a decade-long control gap in a decentralised model with no internal audit function.
  • Workplace accidents of 24.3 per million hours (2024) are more than four times Lagercrantz's 5.8, and absenteeism was 5.5%.
  • The hedging policy is pro-cyclical and decentralised.
  • The two 2026 quarters missed DNB's EBITA estimates by 35% and 18%, which suggests operational visibility at the centre is limited.
C-86

What negative corporate change developments are present/plausible?

F2819th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. The negative corporate developments are numerous and correlated. They all point to a thinner margin of safety at exactly the point where execution matters most.

Reasoning.

  • Present.
  • The best business (Salix) and its leader left.
  • Expensive preference capital stayed with the weaker remainder.
  • Leverage is at or above the top of the target range.
  • Three platform-CEO changes and a new CFO in under seven months.
  • A co-founder and 39% holder left the board amid a personal tax investigation.
  • An inventory restatement at S:t Eriks shows a long-running control gap.
  • There is no internal audit function, and only three of five directors are independent.
  • Two consecutive quarterly EBITA misses (Q1 by 35%, Q2 by 18% against DNB).
  • Interket was bought at about 22x trailing EBITA.
  • A dividend cut is likely.
  • DNB, the main broker, advised on the separation.
  • Plausible.
  • An impairment of goodwill at S:t Eriks or Tornum. Goodwill is large against book equity of only about SEK 0.8bn (consensus 2026e shareholders' equity SEK 802m). Goodwill is tested at 10.1% to 12.8% post-tax discount rates; a material impairment would sharply cut book equity and could tighten any equity-based covenant terms, which are not disclosed in the files.
  • Leverage breaching 3.5x if EBITDA falls a further 12% to 17%, forcing asset sales at trough prices or an equity raise (Volati has raised equity only twice in 20 years).
  • A further integration miss at Clever or Interket.
  • Another platform-CEO departure.
  • A cyber incident across decentralised IT systems.
  • Disposal of the founder's stake.
C-59

How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system (as at Lifco, Avanza or Ryanair)? Such that the goals, strategy, culture, execution, decisions are congruent, holistic and symbiotic?

D4514th of 19

Layer 1 · Company - Management

The Investment Analyst's heading How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system?

Judgement. The culture is coherent at the operating level and incongruent at the system level. Decentralised, entrepreneurial and owner-aligned subsidiaries (co-ownership stakes, internal promotion, a long-term-owner reputation) sit inside a portfolio and capital structure that contradict the compounder ideal. Cyclical platforms, high leverage, expensive preference capital and a 15% target the portfolio cannot deliver in this cycle do not fit together. Volati is not a Lifco-style system.

Reasoning.

  • Congruent elements. Decentralised profit and loss responsibility; platform co-ownership (managers buy stakes, as with Salix Hibernia's set-off issue); training programmes; zero-bonus, market-priced warrants for the CEO; a long-term horizon ("We do not build Volati for each quarter"). Ex-executives praise the balance between autonomy and financial oversight.
  • Incongruent elements. The goal (15% per-share compounding) does not match the means (leverage-constrained M&A, cyclical assets). The incentive design has no operational targets on warrants and no clawback disclosure. Safety and absence metrics are poor (24.3 accidents per million hours; 5.5% absenteeism). Turnover was 11.9% in 2024.
C-81

What fundamental risks are there that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability, and how plausible and how large is each?

F2518th of 19

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. The largest sudden risk is a balance-sheet event, because leverage leaves little room for any of the operating shocks below. Each is low to medium in probability. Several are correlated through the same cyclical trough, so the joint probability of at least one material event over 24 months is meaningful, roughly one in three to one in two.

The risks.

  1. Leverage breaching the 3.5x maximum. Triggered by a further 12% to 17% fall in EBITDA, or by acquisitions without EBITDA recovery. It would force a disposal at trough prices or an equity raise; Volati has raised equity only twice in 20 years. Plausibility: low to medium over 18 months. Size: very large. A rights issue at a depressed price could dilute 30% to 50%, and a fire-sale disposal would crystallise low multiples.
  2. Goodwill impairment at S:t Eriks or Tornum. Goodwill is large against book equity of about SEK 0.8bn. It is tested at post-tax discount rates of 10.1% to 12.8%, and Tornum is loss-making. Plausibility: medium at the year-end 2026 test. Size: non-cash, but it could cut book equity sharply, hurt sentiment and, if any covenant is equity-based (not disclosed in the files), tighten financing.
  3. Integration failure at Interket or Clever. Margins stay near 5% beyond their 24-month programmes, or restructuring write-downs follow; the Risks report names a two-year recovery with "meaningful near-term costs". Plausibility: medium. Size: the margin uplift the model relies on (SEK 50m to 75m of EBITA) fails to arrive, and Ettiketto's margin stays at about 15%.
  4. A control or accounting failure in the decentralised structure. There is no internal audit function, and the S:t Eriks inventory error ran for a decade. Plausibility: low. Size: moderate, mainly to reputation and the multiple.
  5. A shock at Ettiketto. A paper-mill strike (the 2022 UPM strike cost SEK 25m of sales) or loss of a large fast-moving consumer goods or nicotine-pouch customer group. A regulatory restriction on nicotine pouches in Sweden or the EU is not covered in the files but is a plausible conjecture for an important growth category. Plausibility: low. Size: large relative to group EBITA, because Ettiketto is about 55% to 60% of platform EBITA.
  6. Loss of a large project, or a cost overrun, at Tornum or Communication. Percentage-of-completion accounting and lumpy projects carry this risk. Plausibility: medium. Size: SEK 10m to 30m.
  7. Disposal of the founder's stake. Perlhagen's personal tax case could lead him to sell his 39% holding. Plausibility: low. Size: a price overhang, and a change of control dynamics.
  8. A cyber incident across decentralised IT. Plausibility: low. Size: moderate.
D-03

In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

D455th of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The drivers are diffuse where the forecast improves and concentrated where it can go wrong, and that asymmetry is the most useful single fact about this case's predictability. The improvement in 2027 (EBITA +SEK 80m, +25%) comes from four sources of similar size, three of them within management's control, so no single failure costs more than about 5% of 2027 EBITA. But the historical forecast errors, and so the likely future ones, are concentrated in two platforms that now contribute about 2% of platform EBITA (S:t Eriks and Tornum caused the 35% and 18% misses in Q1 and Q2 2026 through production rates and pricing, not sales), in one random variable (Corroventa's weather, worth SEK 10m to 20m of EBITA a year either way), and in one balance-sheet mechanism (fixed claims of about SEK 150m a year that turn a 10% EBITA error into a 17% to 20% EPS error). From 2028 the drivers of the improvement themselves concentrate: about 60% of the SEK 200m of growth from 2027 to 2030 depends on Nordic interest rates through S:t Eriks, Tornum and the pace of acquisitions. Against the last four to six years, the difference is not a new driver but the removal of one: Salix's steady earnings used to average out the cyclical platforms' swings and they now hit the group number unbuffered, with Ettiketto (about two-thirds of platform EBITA) the only remaining ballast.

Reasoning.

1. The history, 2021 to 2025, and what drove it. Continuing EBITA was SEK 472m in 2023, about SEK 390m in 2024 and SEK 332m adjusted in 2025 (reported 318), with LTM at Q3 2026 near SEK 270m adjusted. The decline of about SEK 200m from 2023 came almost entirely from S:t Eriks (margin 9.1% in 2022 to 4.2% in 2025 and about 2% in 2026) and Tornum (9.1% in 2023 to 0.2% in 2024 and a loss in 2026), with Corroventa's dry 2025 adding SEK 10m to 20m. Ettiketto and Communication grew. The sales decline was only about 4%; the EBITA decline was 40%. The history therefore says: two platforms, with high fixed costs and no pricing power, generate almost all the variance.

2. The forecast improvement in 2027 is diffuse (second set's table): Ettiketto +26, Corroventa +16, S:t Eriks +16, Tornum +16, other +6. Three independent management actions (co-location, two cost programmes), one annualisation (Tramex) and one random draw (weather). The correlation between them is low; a failure at any one costs SEK 10m to 26m, or 3% to 7% of 2027 EBITA. That is diffuse by any standard, and it is why 2027 is more predictable than Volati's history suggests.

3. The forecast error is concentrated. The T-D2 report's evidence and the first set's C-69 agree: in every year since 2023 the miss against DNB came from S:t Eriks and Tornum, and in 2026 from production decisions (S:t Eriks' EBITA fell from SEK 47m to 20m on sales up 1%) and price (Tornum's Q2 decremental above 60%). The two platforms' combined 2027 EBITA in the base case is SEK 33m; their plausible range is SEK -25m to +60m. So two platforms with 2% of the base-case EBITA carry about 60% of the plausible error. Add Corroventa's weather (plus or minus SEK 15m) and the concentration is three units, two of which management cannot steer. Peers show what diffuse error looks like: proven Swedish compounders missed Q4 2024 EBITA by 2% on average and the less proven by 4% (DNB, 1 April 2025); Volati missed by 35% and 18% in the two quarters after that.

4. The fixed-claims amplifier concentrates the error again below EBITA. Net financial items of about SEK 73m, the preference dividend of SEK 64m and minorities of SEK 10m are fixed; amortisation of about SEK 108m is fixed for reported earnings. On 2027 figures, EBITA plus or minus 10% (SEK 40m) is adjusted EPS plus or minus 17% to 20% and reported EPS plus or minus 35% to 40%. The interest line adds a second, external concentration: each 100bp on floating debt is about SEK 12m to 13m, and the Riksbank is expected to add 65bp by Q3 2027.

5. The 2028 to 2030 improvement concentrates on one macro variable. Of the SEK 200m of growth from 2027 to 2030: S:t Eriks and Tornum recovery about SEK 65m (rate-dependent), new acquisitions about SEK 55m (dependent on leverage, which depends on EBITDA, which depends on the cycle) and Ettiketto about SEK 55m (integration). The first two are correlated through Nordic rates; the third through European consumer demand and the Swedish VAT cliff of December 2027. That is a concentration on two external variables for about three-quarters of the growth.

6. Ettiketto as the single-platform concentration. At about two-thirds of platform EBITA, Ettiketto is now the group. Its drivers are better than the cyclical platforms' but not diffuse: Swedish food volumes (partly VAT-driven, with a cliff in December 2027 and a fall in consumer confidence in October 2026), nicotine-pouch labels (volumes up 45% in Q2 2026 but eight member states lobbying for an EU flavour ban), and two integrations run from Sweden. A shock here would be larger than any at S:t Eriks or Tornum, with lower probability.

7. Interplay and the structural change against history. Before the spin, Salix (half of group EBITA, 10% margin, steady) was the diversifier. Its removal did not add a driver; it removed the one that cancelled the others. The group number is now a sum of five low-correlation drivers plus a fixed charge, with one dominant positive (Ettiketto) and two dominant negatives (S:t Eriks, Tornum). The predictability consequence: direction is predictable (the self-help is arithmetic), level is not (two platforms and the weather set the error), and the EPS level is less predictable still (fixed claims).

Internal corporate change forces

C-11

To what extent have the chief executive, or other key leaders (e.g. finance director, operations director, board, division heads, etc.) or other key actors (e.g. activists, major shareholders) changed, and what does the change signal?

F4818th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. Almost every layer below the CEO has changed in twelve months, and the founder-shareholder layer has partly stepped back. The CEO has not changed. The signal is twofold. The operating layer is being professionalised with stronger operational and commercial hires, which is positive over 12 to 24 months. But there is heavy simultaneous churn during a leveraged trough (near-term execution risk), and the capital-allocation philosophy is unchanged because the CEO and chairman are unchanged.

Reasoning.

  • Changes.
  • CFO: Martin Aronsson resigned in May 2025, Lars Ingman was interim, and Charlotta Nyberg (formerly Group CFO at Permobil, with Telia and Ericsson behind her) started on 20 April 2026.
  • Ettiketto: Klara Svedberg became CEO on 1 June 2026 (Alfa Laval, Tetra Pak, Ball). Rikard Ahlin moved to Deputy CEO, dedicated to M&A, and left the group executive team.

  • Board. Co-founder Karl Perlhagen (about 39% to 39.5% of votes) resigned on 19 November 2025 because of a tax investigation into his private share transaction; the company says it has no connection to Volati's operations. Christina Tillman joined the Salix board. The board shrank from seven to five, and only three of the five are independent. Patrik Wahlén (23.4% of votes) remains chairman.
  • What it signals. 1. RemainCo lost its best operator (Hansson) and best business together. 2. The new platform heads are external and operational, which fits a turnaround mandate. 3. Three platform-CEO changes in five months, plus a new CFO, mean the 2027 delivery rests on people with no record inside Volati. 4. The largest shareholder is now off the board and under a personal tax investigation. That is a latent overhang: what the 39% holder does next is unknown.
C-12

To what extent have the strategy or the strategic priorities changed, and how well is the change articulated and resourced? (choices)

F4219th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. Strategy has changed meaningfully in structure, but not in kind. Volati moved from three business areas to five platforms, separated Salix, now grows only within existing platforms (no new platforms), pushes bolt-on M&A down to every platform, and runs a margin-first stance at S:t Eriks and Tornum. The change is clearly articulated but poorly resourced. The one decisive strategic question is left unaddressed: whether five unrelated platforms, three of them deeply cyclical, belong under one leveraged balance sheet.

Reasoning.

  • Articulation.
  • Ettiketto pursues European label consolidation, with operations led by Svedberg and M&A by Ahlin, targeting a 20% long-term margin on acquired businesses through the "Ettiketto Way of Working".
  • Corroventa received an M&A mandate (Tramex) to reduce weather dependence and enter North America and Australia.
  • S:t Eriks is shifting from high-volume standard products to specialised, lower-capital lines.
  • Tornum is applying project selectivity and in-house silo production (SIMEZA).
  • Communication is targeting international projects.
  • Segment reporting improved, the executive team now includes platform CEOs, and management says all five platforms are expected to make bolt-on acquisitions within twelve months (Q1 2026 call).
  • Resourcing.
  • Leverage is at or slightly above the top of the 2.0x to 3.0x range (2.9x at Q2, reportedly 3.1x at Q3).
  • Free cash flow after preference and ordinary dividends is small (C-50, C-52).
  • Historically, capital "almost exclusively" went to Salix and Ettiketto, so the industrial platforms were under-invested.
  • The central team is compact.
  • "All five platforms making bolt-ons" is therefore an aspiration that the balance sheet cannot fund at scale in 2026 to 2027.
  • The unaddressed question. The Business Foundations and Change Forces work and the August memo all flag the structural oddity of housing Ettiketto (about 15% to 18% margins) and S:t Eriks or Tornum (2% to 5%) together. The Wisdom Library test ("Linked businesses are not a conglomerate") finds no operating linkage between the platforms beyond the head office and the balance sheet.
C-13

To what extent has financial guidance been raised, or have mid-term targets or capital-markets-day ambitions been set or raised? (choices)

F3519th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. Nothing has been raised. Volati gives no annual or quarterly guidance. Its long-term targets are unchanged despite misses in three of the last four years. The only new ambitions are qualitative and untimed. There has been no capital markets day for the new RemainCo.

Reasoning.

  • Targets unchanged.
  • Average annual EBITA per ordinary share growth of at least 15% over a cycle ("doubling every five years").
  • Return on adjusted equity of 20%.
  • Net debt to adjusted EBITDA of 2.0x to 3.0x, not above 3.5x.
  • Dividend of 10% to 30% of net profit, plus the SEK 40 preference dividend.
  • New qualitative statements (Q1 2026 call). In normal markets the remaining platforms should operate "at 2023 earnings levels or higher" (SEK 472m of EBITA on the 2023 perimeter). The group "expects to grow above its financial targets once market conditions normalise". All platforms are expected to make bolt-ons within twelve months.
  • The misapplied figure. The SEK 1.1bn to 1.5bn of EBITA by 2027 belonged to the pre-spin group (Q3 2025 call). Management has not restated a RemainCo equivalent.
  • Why this matters for the model. The "2023 level in normal markets" statement is the most useful anchor for normalised earnings. Adjusted for acquisitions since 2023 (Clever about SEK 290m of sales, Interket SEK 450m, Tramex SEK 90m, NicEtikett SEK 35m), it implies normalised EBITA of about SEK 550m to 600m once the acquired businesses reach target margins. That is far above 2027 consensus of about SEK 400m. But it carries no date and no building blocks.

C-15

To what extent is the company reducing its perimeter or composition, through divestments, simplification or exits from low-return units? (actions)

F4219th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. The perimeter shrank dramatically, but in the wrong direction for a RemainCo shareholder. The Salix separation removed the highest-quality, most stable business (about half of 2025 group EBITA) and kept the low-return cyclical units. Within platforms there is pruning (S:t Eriks exiting low-margin volume products, Tornum declining low-margin bids). There has been no exit from a low-return unit.

Reasoning.

  • The separation. Salix was built from a SEK 500m purchase in 2015 into a SEK 5.5bn listed company (11.6x return on invested capital, 25.4% IRR). As a value-crystallisation event for shareholders who kept both shares it was excellent. For the remainder it raised earnings volatility, concentrated cyclicality and left the preference shares (and their SEK 64m dividend) with RemainCo.
  • The sector pattern. "A break-up re-rates the parent only when what leaves is worse than what stays." Volati inverted it.
  • Volati's own history. In 2021 it divested its Consumer businesses (the Bokusgruppen listing, the Besikta sale) and leverage fell to 0.8x. The playbook exists. The question is whether a disposal of a weaker platform follows. Management says capital will now go exclusively to the five platforms, and disposals at a cyclical trough would fetch low prices. No such step is signalled.
C-16

To what extent is the company entering new geographic or product markets, and how sensibly? (actions)

F5216th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. Volati is entering new geographies sensibly in strategic logic, but the main entries dilute margins and have been bought at full prices on current earnings. The one clearly accretive entry is Tramex.

Reasoning.

  • Ettiketto into Germany, the UK and the Netherlands. Clever Etiketten (early 2025, SEK 290m of sales, about 5% margin, a two-year integration). Interket (January 2026, SEK 450m of sales, about 5% initial margin, a 24-month improvement programme; total consideration SEK 494m per the Q3 report fragment). The logic, scaling a proven Nordic consolidation model and gaining scale against CCL and Multi-Color, is sound. The risk is execution far from home, and in Q2 2026 Ettiketto's margin fell from 19% to 15%. The Risks report names failure to lift Clever's margin from 5% within twelve months as an operational trigger.
  • Corroventa into North America and Australia (Tramex, Ireland, July 2026). SEK 90m of sales at an adjusted EBITA margin of about 20%. It is accretive, diversifies away from European flood seasonality and adds a professional-meter niche.
  • Tornum into Spain (SIMEZA, 2023, EUR 10m of sales). Vertical integration of silos, sensible for margin capture but not a demand fix.
  • Communication is taking on international projects (Asia, solar mounting); these are opportunistic.
C-17

To what extent is the company raising prices in real terms (above cost inflation), and are the rises real, merely nominal, or so far only latent or plausible and increasingly noted by management? (actions)

F3518th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. Nominal and lagged, not real. Ettiketto recovers cost inflation with a one-to-two-quarter lag. Communication's margin gains come from volume and mix. S:t Eriks and Tornum are giving price, not taking it. Real pricing power is latent at best.

Reasoning.

  • Ettiketto. Price increases were implemented in Q2 2026 to offset Middle East-driven material costs and take full effect in H2 2026. Framework agreements carry index mechanisms, and management describes "strong pricing power" (EBITA up 18% in Q1). But the purpose is cost recovery; nothing in the files shows prices rising faster than costs over a cycle.
  • Communication. The Q2 margin rose 3 points to 14% on higher shipments over fixed costs; this is operating leverage, not pricing.
  • S:t Eriks. Faces customer resistance on standard products and deliberately accepts lower volume to protect mix.
  • Tornum. Faces competitor discounting; it declines low-margin bids, defending price integrity rather than raising price.
  • Corroventa. Its pricing leverage appears only in flood events.
  • Volati's 2021 to 2023 record. Subsidiaries "successfully raised product prices to offset high material and freight inflation". That shows pass-through capacity in an inflationary upturn, not pricing above inflation.
C-18

To what extent is the company stepping up mergers and acquisitions, and how strategically sensible, disciplined and well integrated are they? (actions)

F3819th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. M&A was stepped up in 2026, against the "frozen engine" narrative. Three deals added SEK 575m of annual sales, plus Clever in 2025. Strategically the deals are sensible. On current earnings they are expensive, they are mostly dilutive in the near term, and they were funded at the expense of leverage. The step-up is now largely exhausted until EBITDA recovers.

Reasoning.

  • Interket (January 2026). Total consideration SEK 494m for SEK 450m of sales at about a 5% initial margin, which is about SEK 22m of EBITA. That is roughly 1.1x sales and about 22x trailing EBITA. It is about 7x only if the 24-month improvement programme lifts the margin to about 15%. This is far from the "disciplined ~7x EBITA" record cited for Volati's historical bolt-ons. It is an integration bet taken with leverage near 3x.
  • The integration evidence so far. In Q2 2026 Ettiketto's EBITA rose only 8% (SEK 60m to SEK 65m) on 37% sales growth. Implied arithmetic: legacy Ettiketto, including Clever already in the base, made about SEK 60m on about SEK 331m of sales (about 4% organic growth, flat EBITA, hit by material shortages and lagged pricing), and Interket added about SEK 5m on about SEK 105m. Ettiketto's headline growth is mostly acquired. Its 2026 to 2028 margin path depends on integration, not on organic momentum. Clever's two-year programme (from early 2025) should show results by early 2027.
  • Tramex (July 2026). SEK 90m of sales at about 20% adjusted EBITA margin. Accretive, diversifying and sensible.
  • NicEtikett (July 2026). A SEK 35m-sales asset deal; a local bolt-on with immediate cost synergies.
  • Funding. FactSet shows SEK 599m of acquisition cash out in H1 2026 alone. Leverage reportedly rose to 3.1x at Q3 "following recent acquisitions".
  • Discipline and governance. The model is decentralised: platforms identify, execute and integrate, with central approval and capital. The Change Forces report flags the risk that inexperienced platform teams overpay or under-integrate without central oversight. Sourcing competition is rising (C-02).
C-19

To what extent is the company making more use of its balance sheet (for investment, acquisitions or returns), or stepping up shareholder returns (a buyback starting or increasing, a special or progressive dividend), or is management increasingly mooting either? (actions)

F2819th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. The balance sheet is already fully used. Leverage is at the top of the range, and expensive preference capital ranks ahead of the ordinary shares. Shareholder returns are about to fall: the ordinary dividend is likely to be cut from SEK 2.00 (paid for 2025 on the pre-spin group) to about SEK 0.5 to 0.7. There is no buyback, and none is mooted.

Reasoning.

  • Leverage.
  • Company basis: 2.9x at Q2 2026 (from 3.0x a year earlier), reportedly 3.1x at Q3. Policy is 2.0x to 3.0x, not above 3.5x.
  • DNB, including all preference shares and leases: 4.1x for 2026e, and 4.5x to 4.8x in other DNB notes.
  • The CEO says operating near 3.0x is a "deliberate choice to support acquisitions". The headroom to the 3.5x maximum is only about 0.4x to 0.6x of EBITDA: roughly SEK 200m to 280m of extra net debt, or a 12% to 17% fall in LTM EBITDA.
  • Preference shares. SEK 40 a share, SEK 64m a year, valued at about SEK 1.0bn in FactSet's enterprise value. About 6.4% on value, and not tax-deductible: expensive capital compared with bank debt at about 3.5% to 4.5%. Sdiptech redeemed its preference shares (SEK 184m) in 2026, a precedent. Volati cannot afford to redeem SEK 1bn without re-leveraging.
  • Dividends. The ordinary dividend of SEK 2.00 for 2025 (about SEK 159m) was held flat "to prioritise financial flexibility". Consensus dividend per share is SEK 0.56 (2026) and SEK 0.675 (2027), within the 10% to 30% payout policy. The cut frees about SEK 100m a year from 2027. That is a mechanical help to deleveraging, but negative for holders who wanted income.
  • Buybacks. None. Management is mooting acquisitions (all platforms to do bolt-ons), not returns.
C-20

What other material positive corporate change forces are present/plausible?

–52

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. Several modest positives exist and are mostly mechanical or self-help. Together they could add perhaps SEK 60m to 100m of EBITA by 2027 against an LTM of SEK 262m, independent of end markets. That is meaningful but not transformational.

Reasoning.

  1. Cost programmes at S:t Eriks and Tornum reach full run-rate in H2 2026. The size is not disclosed; it includes headcount reductions.
  2. S:t Eriks production realignment after the inventory clearance removes the H1 2026 under-absorption that management caused on purpose.
  3. Interket and Clever margin programmes (24 months and two years). A lift from about 5% towards 12% to 15% on SEK 740m of combined sales is worth about SEK 50m to 75m of EBITA if fully delivered, phased over 2026 to 2028.
  4. Tramex adds about SEK 18m of EBITA a year from July 2026.
  5. Central-cost rightsizing. A parent built for an SEK 8.4bn group now serves SEK 4.8bn. Q2 implied central costs and eliminations of about SEK 30m, including separation costs that should not recur.
  6. The dividend cut frees cash (C-19).
  7. Corroventa normalisation. After two exceptionally dry years, average weather alone would restore part of its earlier 15% to 22% margins.
  8. Plausible, not signalled. A further portfolio action (the 2021 and 2026 precedents), refinancing or redemption of the preference shares once leverage allows, and a capital markets day setting RemainCo targets.
C-87

To what extent have key controlling actors (e.g. activists, major shareholders) indicated scope for change in strategy/approach?

F4518th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. They have indicated none explicitly. Their record shows a latent willingness to separate businesses to crystallise value. But no activist is present, and the control structure blocks outside pressure.

Reasoning.

  • Control. The founders control 62.9% of votes (Perlhagen 39.5%, Wahlén 23.4%). Ordinary shares carry one vote and preference shares 0.1. No activist is documented. Board voting power has been concentrated by the contraction to five members.
  • Revealed preference. The founders and board listed Bokusgruppen (2021), sold Besikta (2021) and separated Salix (2026). They are willing to realise value through separations rather than hold forever, unlike Lifco's "never sell" doctrine.
  • Stated position after the spin. Capital will go exclusively to the five platforms and grow within them. No disposal is flagged.
  • Unknowns. Perlhagen's intentions now that he is off the board and under investigation. And whether the founders, who also hold large Salix stakes, will allocate attention and capital to RemainCo or to Salix. The chairman's 18.6m ordinary shares (about SEK 440m at SEK 23.75) give strong alignment. Insiders' near-total absence of open-market buying gives no conviction signal.
C-21

Taken together, how strong, broad and well-resourced are the positive corporate changes under way?

F3819th of 19

Layer 1 · Internal change forces

Judgement. The positive corporate changes are moderately broad: cost programmes, integration programmes, production realignment, new operating leaders, an accretive bolt-on and better disclosure. They are weakly resourced and narrowly concentrated. Most of the upside rests on two integration programmes at Ettiketto and two cost and realignment programmes at S:t Eriks and Tornum, run by leaders new to Volati. They are funded by a balance sheet with almost no slack, and partly offset by a long list of negatives (C-86).

Reasoning. The self-help sum identified in C-20 (perhaps SEK 60m to 100m of EBITA by 2027) is real and largely independent of the cycle. That is why 2027 consensus EBITA of about SEK 400m is achievable without a market recovery (see C-67). But nothing in the corporate changes creates a new growth engine. M&A is constrained, there is no price-led margin expansion, and there is no new mid-term plan. The changes repair a trough; they do not build a compounder.

C-22

What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

F3818th of 19

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

Judgement. The staircase is short and its steps are mostly low in novelty: they are mechanical repair rather than new stories. The best-founded steps fall in months 6 to 18 (H1 2027): easy comparisons, cost run-rates, integration progress, a smaller dividend and falling leverage. The novel steps (an acquisition restart, a RemainCo capital markets day, a portfolio action, a construction recovery) are either unsignalled or depend on the cycle.

Next 6 months (to April 2027).

  • The Q3 2026 detail (published 23 October). Novelty low; it is a risk, not a step, until the segment figures are known.
  • Christian Engvall's first months at Corroventa (from 19 October 2026), plus whether the autumn floods arrive. Novelty low; poorly founded, because weather is random.
  • The Riksbank decision in November 2026. Probably negative (a hike).
  • The FY2026 report in February 2027. This brings the year-end goodwill test, the first full post-spin year, and the dividend proposal (likely about SEK 0.4 to 0.7, freeing cash). Novelty moderate; well-founded as a deleveraging signal.
  • Cost programmes at full run-rate in H2 2026, visible in Q4. Well-founded; small.
  • One or two small bolt-ons. Management expects bolt-ons at all platforms, but capacity is limited. Moderately founded.

Months 6 to 18 (April 2027 to April 2028).

  • Q1 and Q2 2027 against very easy comparisons: Tornum (Q1 2026 sales -37%), S:t Eriks (Q1 EBITA SEK -16m) and Corroventa. Year-on-year EBITA growth could be large. Well-founded mechanically. Novelty: optics.
  • Interket and Clever margin progress. Clever's two-year mark is early 2027; Interket reaches 18 months in mid-2027. Moderately founded.
  • Leverage on the company's definition falling below 2.5x through EBITDA growth. This would re-open acquisition capacity and so the sector's "repaired balance sheet buying again" story. Moderately founded on base-case EBITDA. A potential re-rating trigger.
  • A capital markets day or new RemainCo targets. Unsignalled. Novelty high if it happens.
  • A possible refinancing or partial redemption of the preference shares once leverage allows (the Sdiptech precedent). Unsignalled.

Months 18 to 36 (2028 to 2029).

  • A Swedish construction recovery if rates ease (S:t Eriks back towards 6% to 9%). Poorly founded on today's rate path.
  • The European farm investment cycle. Hampered by post-2027 agricultural policy; Ukraine reconstruction a possible tailwind.
  • Ettiketto's continued European roll-up under a dedicated M&A deputy, if capital allows.
  • A further portfolio separation or disposal, given the founders' 2021 and 2026 precedents. Novelty high; unsignalled.
