Why I trust the 2027 part and haircut the 2028 part.

  • The 2027 savings are either already visible (S:t Eriks and Tornum margins in Q3 2026) or physically committed (the co-location, for which the restructuring cost has been booked). Decentralised peers show that local cost cuts are fast: Sdiptech held margins in Q2 2020 through flexible cost reductions (Redeye, 23 July 2020). Volati's six-to-nine-month promise is so far being met.
  • The 2028 part rests on integration programmes run by an Ettiketto CEO who joined on 1 June 2026, in German, Dutch and British businesses whose customer mix is more standard and price-sensitive. Ettiketto's playbook lifted earlier acquired converters towards its group margin, but over years, not quarters, and Clever is already behind (still well below 10% about 20 months into a two-year programme). I therefore model Interket and Clever at 11% to 12% by 2028, not at the 15% the first set allowed, and 13% by 2030, still well below the 20% Ettiketto target.

Why the cost base cannot be cut much further without shrinking revenue. Raw materials are the largest line (about 60% of consolidated sales in 2025) and procurement is decentralised by design. Storskogen's pooled purchasing (SEK 20m a year from freight, an electricity framework with Vattenfall) shows what pooling can do, but Volati has no such programme and the culture resists it (a former Dover executive: getting local leaders to share budgets "is extremely difficult"). Plant leases and depreciation are rigid. Plant closures would remove fixed cost but also the spare capacity that gives S:t Eriks its recovery leverage, a trade-off management has so far declined.

What reaches the line items, in sum. Adjusted EBITA: about +SEK 45m to 50m in 2027 (S:t Eriks 16, Tornum 10 to 12, Ettiketto 18 to 20, head office 0 to 3) and a further +SEK 20m to 25m in 2028 (almost all Ettiketto). Reported EBITA: about +SEK 85m to 90m in 2027 (adding the non-recurring 2026 items). Cash: the restructuring costs were spent in 2026; savings flow into free cash flow from 2027, after tax. EPS: about +SEK 0.45 in 2027 and +SEK 0.20 in 2028 on the adjusted basis.

5e

Reinforcing feedback loops

2 answers
C-4750P-0547
C-47To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?D5014th of 19full answer under 4a Business quality
P-05

After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, such that dynamics at play today create additional scope for further positive/negative dynamics in the near/mid-term (reinforcing feedback loops), or the opposite (balancing loops), how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

F4712th of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

The Investment Analyst's heading After assessing in greater detail whether reinforcing or balancing feedback loops are at work in the business model, how are they likely to shape the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years?

Judgement. Six loops are at work. Today the balancing loops dominate. The key change ahead is that the leverage-and-acquisition loop flips from balancing to reinforcing around mid-2027, when the company's leverage falls below about 2.5x mainly through EBITDA recovery and the dividend cut. Once flipped, it compounds: lower leverage allows bolt-ons, bolt-ons add EBITDA, and EBITDA lowers leverage further. Consensus does not model acquisitions, so this loop is the main source of unpriced upgrades for 2028 to 2030. But two balancing loops keep running through the forecast period and cap the outcome: the grain-storage overcapacity loop (Tornum stays near breakeven into 2028) and the valuation loop (equity cannot be issued, and the preference shares cannot be redeemed before about 2029). The net effect on the line items: a lower EBITA path than the self-help alone would imply in 2027, and a progressively higher one from 2028, because acquisitions restart.

The loops, their direction now and their effect on the model.

Loop Type now When it changes Effect on the forecast
1. Leverage and acquisitions Balancing (3.1x, deals paused after SEK 575m of acquired sales in 2026) Flips to reinforcing around Q2 to Q3 2027 at below 2.5x Acquisitions of SEK 50m (2027), SEK 150m (2028), SEK 200m (2029 and 2030); acquired sales of SEK 120m (2028) rising to SEK 475m (2030); EBITA +SEK 14m (2028) to +SEK 57m (2030)
2. Under-absorption at S:t Eriks Was reinforcing (lower production released cash and crushed margin); now reversing Reversed in Q3 2026 (production raised) +SEK 10m of EBITA in H1 2027; the cash it released reverses (working capital rebuild about SEK 20m to 30m in 2027)
3. Grain-storage overcapacity Balancing (discounting, deferred projects) Not before 2028; capacity leaves slowly, through acquisitions rather than bankruptcies Tornum margin -1% (2027), 1% (2028), 3% (2030); volume recovery only from 2028
4. Label consolidation Reinforcing (regulation and digital-press costs push small converters to sell; each deal adds procurement scale) Running now, at small scale, self-funded by Ettiketto Ettiketto is the first platform to buy again; most of the new bolt-ons in my base case are at Ettiketto
5. Valuation and cost of capital Balancing (about 11x 2027 EV/EBITA; equity worth about 43% of EV; swap rates up) Only after a re-rating, which needs organic growth No equity issuance; no buybacks; the preference shares stay until about 2029
6. Retaining the cyclical platforms Balancing (S:t Eriks return on capital employed excluding goodwill 3%, Tornum -14%, against Ettiketto 39% and Communication 67%; group 17%, or 8% including goodwill) Only through recovery or a disposal, neither in the base case Caps group returns and the multiple; makes group EBITA a sum of uncorrelated drivers

Reasoning on the loops that matter most.

Loop 1: why the flip is likely, and why it is the swing factor for 2028 to 2030. The precedents differ in speed. Storskogen took about three years from its 2022 leverage peak (4.35x) to restart, mainly through cash flow and nine divestments. AddLife went from 3.5x to 2.2x through organic cash flow and working capital. Sdiptech went from 3.31x to 2.81x in nine months through ten divestments above 6x EBITA. Volati's path most resembles AddLife's, organic and EBITDA-led, not Storskogen's (which needed disposals) or Sdiptech's (which sold good assets at good prices, impossible for S:t Eriks or Tornum at a trough). It is faster than Storskogen's because Volati's leverage problem is mostly a denominator problem: EBITDA fell, debt did not balloon. The sector digest's rule is that "consensus models only businesses already owned, so a repaired balance sheet buying again is a stream of unpriced upgrades for 12 to 24 months". On my base case that window opens in H2 2027 and runs through 2029.

Loop 4: can it run while the group is constrained? Yes, at small scale. Ettiketto has done eight acquisitions and sources its own deals (DNB Carnegie, 11 September 2026), and NicEtikett (SEK 35m of sales) was bought in July 2026 with leverage at 2.9x to 3.1x. But larger deals (Interket size) need the parent's balance sheet, and private-equity roll-ups (Triton-backed All4Labels) compete for targets. The EU Packaging and Packaging Waste Regulation (applying since 12 August 2026) and digital presses (up to USD 1m each) feed the pipeline. This loop is real but slow, and it carries an integration cost: each new converter starts at 5% to 10% margins and dilutes Ettiketto for 18 to 24 months, as Interket shows.

Loop 3: why Tornum stays near breakeven longer than consensus assumes. The PETKUS expert (10 February 2026) says excess capacity in grain drying leaves mainly through acquisitions, and that local players survive on service proximity, so weaker producers are slow to exit. AGI's US plant consolidation and low-priced standard bin push prices down further before volume returns. The post-2027 Common Agricultural Policy proposals and Nordic nitrogen rules prolong the demand freeze. AGI reported that farm order intake picked up in late 2026 (30 September 2026), the first faint positive, but North America is not Tornum's market.

The interplay between loops. Loops 1 and 5 interact: as leverage falls, the valuation loop eases only if organic growth returns (the sector's rule: organic growth is the evidence for the multiple). If the company buys growth before organic growth turns, the market may read it as more of the "false friend" pattern (acquired growth masking organic decline) that the first set flagged, and the multiple does not move. Loops 2 and 1 interact through cash: the inventory release that helped leverage in 2026 reverses in 2027, which slows the deleveraging slightly. Loop 6 interacts with all of them: as long as S:t Eriks and Tornum are retained, a quarter of group revenue earns below the cost of capital, which keeps the cost of equity high and the acquisition currency weak.

There is no formal guidance to test.

The informal guidance points two ways: cautious for the rest of 2026 and optimistic for the medium term. Management's record shows honesty about misses but optimism about recovery timing. No building blocks are given.

The informational vacuum (no guidance, no RemainCo capital markets day, divergent broker bases, two consecutive large misses) is itself a risk factor. It explains part of the discount, and its removal (a capital markets day with credible building blocks) is one of the few cheap catalysts management controls.

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

The provisional Q3 fragments (Tornum sales down 17% to 20%; cost inflation preventing meaningful margin expansion at S:t Eriks and Tornum; a 4% fall in the share on the day) suggest 2026 consensus sits above what management's tone and Q3 support.

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

Adjusted for weaker input forces, the 15% target should be read as a long-run aspiration that the second set should not use as a forecast anchor.

6c

Case simplicity

7 answers
Judgement44C-7930D-0250D-0345D-0435D-0545D-0640
Group judgement

Risks

F447th of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts for the next 18 months are predictable in direction and only moderately predictable in level, and the predictability decays through the window rather than improving.

What makes the direction predictable is that the first and second sets, working independently, built the same case from the same evidence (D-01), and that most of the 2027 improvement is the arithmetic of actions already taken and visible in the Q3 2026 run-rate: the end of self-inflicted under-absorption at S:t Eriks, two cost programmes whose effects have begun to show, a co-location whose restructuring cost has been booked, and a full year of Tramex (D-02). Four sources of similar size, three within management's control, mean no single failure costs more than about 5% of 2027 EBITA (D-03).

What limits the predictability of the level is the concentration of the error rather than of the drivers. Two platforms with about 2% of forecast EBITA (S:t Eriks and Tornum) have caused every miss since 2023 and carry about 60% of the plausible 2027 error, through production and pricing decisions the outside cannot see (D-03, D-06). Weather adds a random SEK 15m. Fixed claims of about SEK 150m a year turn a 10% EBITA error into a 17% to 20% adjusted EPS error and a 35% to 40% reported EPS error. The cone on 2027 EBITA is about -17% to +13% with a downside skew, because every bear ingredient has already happened once in the last eight quarters; the cone on the share price is wider and skews up, because the multiple starts at a trough band and the equity is a leveraged claim on the enterprise (D-04).

What makes the predictability decay is time. To April 2027 the two prints are set by actions and comparisons; from July 2027 the case depends on the leverage loop flipping, on Nordic rates, on the VAT cliff and on an agricultural cycle whose bottom is a forecast, not a fact. The disclosure allows the memo to test the case at each print at the platform level but gives no early warning of a backlog or weather shock (D-05). The multiple is company-driven, so the triggers that re-rate it are identifiable, but no sector tailwind will help (D-07).

The one risk that overrides the others is sequence. The second set's deleveraging path is credible, but on the Q3 2026 balance sheet an 11% fall in adjusted EBITDA with flat net debt would breach the 3.5x maximum; the headroom becomes comfortable only after the Q4 2026 cash release, and management has shown it will spend before then if a deal is available. The next four months are the fragile period.

For the memo: treat the 2027 self-help case as the base, with a 55% chance of landing within plus or minus 10% of SEK 398m of adjusted EBITA and the shortfall more likely than the excess; treat February and April 2027 as the two tests that decide whether the case moves from "bottoming" to "turning up"; and treat 2028 as a cyclical and capital-allocation call, not a forecast.

C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

F3019th of 19

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. The perimeter is narrower than before (five platforms, about 70 operating companies; 19 countries was the pre-spin figure). Predictability has fallen, not risen, because the stable distribution business left and four of the five remaining platforms are cyclical, lumpy or weather-driven, each with its own driver.

Reasoning. The five platforms have five unrelated demand drivers: consumer staples labels, telecom and defence capital spending, flood events, Swedish construction and European farm investment. The five-segment disclosure helps outsiders model each one. But the group number is a sum of uncorrelated or mixed-sign drivers plus leverage, and the Q1 and Q2 2026 misses against DNB (by 35% and 18%) show how poor short-term visibility is. The case-simplicity guardrails fail on driver count (about six named drivers, nested inside one another) and on headwind count (three of five platforms). The geographic footprint is widening (UK, Netherlands, Germany, Ireland, North America, Spain) as acquisitions add complexity.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

F508th of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. For 2027 the expected change rests mostly on things Volati has demonstrably done: local cost-cutting (shown in Q3 2026 at S:t Eriks, where the margin held at 5% on sales down 9%, and at Tornum, where the decremental margin fell to about 13%), integrating acquired label converters in the Nordics (Ettiketto's EBITA grew from SEK 52m in 2019 to SEK 215m in 2025 and its margin returned above 20% after each acquisition pause), closing and annualising acquisitions (Tramex, NicEtikett), and deleveraging through cash flow (93% conversion over twelve months; the 2021 precedent). About 60% to 65% of the 2027 step-up of SEK 80m is of that kind. The rest of the thesis, and almost all of 2028 to 2030, relies on strengths Volati has not demonstrated: lifting acquired converters' margins outside the Nordics (Clever's programme is already delayed by the CEO's own admission in July 2026; Interket sits at about 5% to 6%), earning a margin at S:t Eriks while its sole cement supplier consolidates clinker at Slite and buys 106 aggregate sites, surviving a price war in grain storage (Tornum's margin went from 9.1% in 2023 to 0.2% in 2024 without any demonstrated floor), and doing all this with a CFO and three platform CEOs who joined in 2026. The record of the people who will deliver 2027 is therefore mostly a record of the people who have left or moved. Plausibility is good for the direction and size of the 2027 change, moderate for its timing by quarter, and weak beyond 2027.

Reasoning.

1. The expected change, split by what has been demonstrated.

Component of the 2027 change (SEK m, second set) Demonstrated by Volati? Evidence Verdict
S:t Eriks: no repeat of under-absorption, cost programme, +16 Yes, in Q3 2026 Production raised in Q3; margin 5% on sales -9%; DNB "cost actions are starting to take effect" Plausible; timing within a quarter or two
Tornum: cost programme, easy H1 comparison, +16 Partly Q3 decremental only about 13% against 25% to 60% earlier in 2026; but EBITA still SEK -9m and sales -17% Plausible for the cost half; the volume half needs orders that have not been announced
Ettiketto: co-location savings from Q1 2027, integration, organic growth, +26 Partly Nordic playbook proven (eight acquisitions, margin back above 20%); SEK 19m restructuring booked and the German consolidation "completed" (Q3 call); but Clever delayed and the group has never integrated a UK, Dutch or German converter to target Plausible for the co-location saving; the margin lift to 9% (2027) is an extrapolation of a Nordic record to new markets
Corroventa: Tramex full year +10, weather +6 Yes for Tramex (known 20% margin, SEK 90m sales); no for weather Q3 margin 17.9% with Tramex at 32% of sales; organic still negative Plausible for Tramex; weather is a coin toss
Head office, bolt-ons, Communication, +6 Mixed Head office flat; Communication held 11% Small

2. The precedents on timing, which are the weak point. Grain-storage margins took two to three years to repair in every precedent (Kepler Weber 2016 to 2018 through a structural reorganisation; AGCO's grain and protein 2019 to 2021 through plant consolidation; AGI's farm segment two years after 2017). Heavyside building materials took multi-year horizons: eight of ten companies had lower margins in 2022 than 2019 and nine recovered only by 2024 (Morgan Stanley, 28 October 2026). Nordic acquirers' balance-sheet repairs took one to three years (AddLife 3.2x to 2.2x over about two years through cash flow; Sdiptech nine months but through ten divestments; Storskogen three years with twenty divestments). Volati's path most resembles AddLife's, organic and EBITDA-led, which is the slowest of the three but the only one open to it, since its weak platforms cannot be sold at a trough. Management's own six-to-nine-month promise on cost programmes has so far been met, which is the strongest piece of present delivery; its record on calling market recoveries (construction "during 2025 to 2026") is poor.

3. What the business would need to acquire.

  • A European integration capability. Ettiketto's record is Nordic, with co-located plants and framework customers. Interket's customers are "standard, price-sensitive"; German retail partners show "extreme price pressure" (FrieslandCampina, September 2026). The hard-integration model Volati runs (DNB, February 2026, comparing it to AQ Group and KB Components) has worked at KB Components within twelve to eighteen months, so it is not unprecedented in the sector, but it is unprecedented at Ettiketto abroad and it is led by a CEO who started on 1 June 2026.
  • Pricing power against a consolidated upstream. S:t Eriks' input prices (aggregates +7.9%) are rising faster than its output prices (ready-mix -0.3%); Heidelberg Materials will own the only Swedish cement and the largest aggregate network. No Volati platform has shown real pricing above cost. The 2028 to 2030 S:t Eriks margins (4.0% to 5.5%) assume at least a neutral price-cost spread, which has not been demonstrated.
  • A floor under Tornum. The 2024 collapse to 0.2% and the Q3 2026 loss show the business has no demonstrated downside protection; the forecast's floor (SEK -8m in 2027) is an assumption that cost cuts hold while AGI prices "more aggressively" and Kepler Weber discounts to defend share.
  • Management continuity. Three platform CEOs and the CFO are new in 2026; the executive team grew from three to eight. The track record that makes the self-help plausible (cost programmes, Ettiketto's playbook) was built by people who are no longer in the roles.

4. The present delivery that argues for plausibility. Underlying EBITA grew 15% in Q3 2026; cash conversion 93%; the S:t Eriks margin held; Communication held 11% on 7% growth; Corroventa beat DNB by 28% on EBITA; the Salix listing, the refinancing and three acquisitions closed on schedule. Operational promises with a date have been kept in 2026. Financial promises without a date (the 15% target) have not been kept since 2022.

5. Net. The 2027 change is plausible because most of it is the arithmetic of actions already taken; the probability that the base case of SEK 398m is reached within plus or minus 10% is in my view about 55%, with the shortfall more likely than the excess. The change from 2028 is not yet plausible on demonstrated strengths; it is a bet on the cycle and on a new team repeating an old playbook in new countries.

D-03In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?D455th of 8full answer under 2a Internal risks
D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

F357th of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The cone on 2027 adjusted EBITA is about SEK 330m to 450m around a base of SEK 398m (-17% to +13%), on adjusted EPS about SEK 1.5 to 2.8 around SEK 2.24 (-32% to +23%), and on reported ordinary EPS about SEK 0.4 to 1.7 around SEK 1.13. It widens sharply in the last six months of the 18-month window (H1 2028), where the 2028 cone is SEK 370m to 540m. The earnings cone is skewed to the downside: the bear ingredients (a dry season, a Tornum loss, an integration stuck near 6%, a rate rise) have each already happened within the last eight quarters, while the bull needs a Swedish construction upturn that rising rates make unlikely before 2028; a probability-weighted 2027 EBITA is about SEK 385m to 390m, below base. The share-price cone is wider and, unusually, skews the other way: because the multiple starts at the 3rd percentile of its five-year range (9.7x two-year-forward on consensus; about 9.0x on our EPS) and ordinary equity is only about 43% of enterprise value, the plausible end-2027 price range is roughly SEK 15 to 48 around a base of about SEK 30 to 32 (-40% to +95%), with a probability-weighted value near SEK 30. So: the direction of 2027 earnings is predictable, the level is moderately predictable (the cone on EBITA is about plus or minus 15%, far wider than peers' plus or minus 2% to 4% but narrower than Volati's own 2026 misses of 18% to 35%), the EPS level is poorly predictable, and the share price is unpredictable in the sense that its cone is set by the multiple and the leverage rather than by the operations.

Reasoning.

1. The earnings cone, built from the drivers.

2027 adjusted EBITA (SEK m) Bear (30%) Base (50%) Bull (20%) What has to happen
Ettiketto 250 280 300 Interket and Clever stuck near 6% against 9%; or co-location savings plus Swedish volumes
Communication 75 83 90 Telecom freeze deepens; or defence and grid projects
Corroventa 45 59 72 Dry winter and spring; or floods
S:t Eriks 24 41 60 2% margin on falling volume and aggregate costs; or 5% on civil engineering growth and realignment
Tornum -25 -8 +15 Price war and no orders; or AGI-style order pick-up
Head office and bolt-ons -60 -57 -55
Group about 310 to 330 398 about 450 to 480

The bear is not a stress test: each ingredient has occurred in 2025 or 2026. The bull requires at least two of three external turns (wet weather, construction, grain) that have not occurred since 2023. That is the skew.

2. Why the cone is wider than peers' and narrower than Volati's recent record. Peers with diversified, non-cyclical portfolios miss by 2% to 4% (DNB, April 2025). Volati's cone is wider because two platforms with high fixed costs and no pricing power can swing SEK 30m to 40m each, because weather adds SEK 15m, and because the group is small enough (base EBITA SEK 398m) for SEK 10m items to be 2.5% each. It is narrower than the 2026 misses because 2026's largest errors were self-inflicted and one-off (the deliberate S:t Eriks production cut, SEK 25m to 30m in Q2; separation costs), and because the comparison base for H1 2027 is so low that year-on-year growth is almost assured even in the bear.

3. The EPS cone and the reported-EPS trap. Adjusted EPS: bear about SEK 1.53, base 2.24, bull 2.76. Reported ordinary EPS (after about SEK 108m of amortisation and SEK 5m of items): bear about SEK 0.45, base 1.13, bull 1.65. The FY2026 reported ordinary EPS will be close to zero (nine months SEK 0.02). The memo should expect the February 2027 report to show a 2026 ordinary EPS that a casual reader will take as a collapse; it is the capital structure and amortisation, not the operations, but it widens the perceived cone.

4. The price cone. Using end-2027 as the horizon and two-year-forward adjusted EPS (FY2029 in the base; roughly FY2028 bear and bull as proxies): bear SEK 1.9 at 8x (the Storskogen bear multiple) is about SEK 15; base SEK 3.57 at the 11.21x guideline is about SEK 40 at the model's horizon, or SEK 2.89 (FY2028) at 10x to 11x is about SEK 29 to 32 on a more conservative forward basis; bull SEK 3.7 at 13x (the Selector's "conservative" band) is about SEK 48. Weighted at 30/50/20 the expected value is about SEK 30 to 34 against SEK 23.75 to 24.75. The price cone skews up for two reasons the earnings cone does not share: the starting multiple is already at a trough band (so the bear's multiple compression is limited to about 1x to 2x while the bull's expansion is 2x to 4x), and the enterprise-value leverage means a 15% rise in enterprise value is a 35% rise in the ordinary equity. The first set's trough of SEK 13 to 15 remains the right bear marker.

5. Where the cone widens inside the 18 months. To April 2027 (Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 reports) the cone is narrow: both quarters are set mostly by actions taken and by comparisons (Q1 2026 adjusted EBITA SEK 41m). From July 2027 the cone widens: Q2 2026 was Communication's exceptional quarter (a hard comparison), the loop flip depends on leverage below 2.5x, and H1 2028 depends on the Riksbank's 2027 path, the VAT cliff of December 2027 and the CAP adoption. Predictability therefore decays through the window rather than improving with evidence.

6. What narrows or widens it from here. Narrowing: a Q4 2026 print with net debt near SEK 1.2bn, Tornum's loss under SEK 10m and Ettiketto's margin at 14% or above, and a Q1 2027 print showing the co-location saving. Widening: a Riksbank path above 2.4%, a Tornum goodwill impairment (SEK 139m against book equity of about SEK 0.8bn), an ordinary dividend of nil, a Nordea downgrade, or a further acquisition before leverage falls.

7. The skew in words. The downside is more probable and smaller in price terms (the multiple is already low); the upside is less probable and larger. That is the classic profile of a leveraged trough, and it is why the case is unpredictable without being unattractive: an investor is paid for accepting a moderately predictable earnings path and an unpredictable multiple, provided the balance sheet survives the next four months.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

D456th of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. Helpful at the platform level and unhelpful at the driver level. Since 21 April 2026 Volati reports five platforms quarterly with net sales, organic and acquired growth, EBITA before items affecting comparability, the margin, return on capital employed excluding goodwill, cash conversion and leverage on its own definition, and it names the items affecting comparability by platform. That is enough to test the second set's milestones in February and April 2027 (Q4 2026 adjusted EBITA about SEK 82m to 85m, leverage about 2.7x, a dividend proposal, no Tornum impairment; Q1 2027 adjusted EBITA about SEK 66m, S:t Eriks about SEK -6m, Tornum about SEK -9m, first co-location savings). What it does not give is anything that would let the memo see those prints coming: no order intake or backlog at S:t Eriks or Tornum, no rental utilisation or day rates at Corroventa, no size for the cost programmes or the co-location savings, no separation cost figure, no price-cost commentary by platform, no guidance, and no capital markets day. Monitoring over six months is therefore a matter of reading the two prints when they arrive and of tracking external proxies in between; the proxies exist and are reasonably good for four of the five platforms, which is why the disclosure is not disqualifying.

Reasoning.

1. What the company gives, and its quality. The five-segment table is complete and consistent quarter to quarter; the organic and acquired split is given; the Q3 report attributed Tornum's fall to the Lantmännen roll-off, S:t Eriks' to a deliberate exit from low-margin volume and to weaker water and wastewater projects, Corroventa's to dry weather and Ettiketto's to Interket's mix and un-offset material costs. The CEO's statements have dates (cost measures within six to nine months; co-location savings from Q1 2027; seasonally strong Q4 cash flow). Items affecting comparability were itemised (SEK 19m co-location, SEK 8m transaction costs). Leverage, cash conversion and the platform returns on capital are published each quarter. The 2025 annual report gives goodwill by platform and the discount rates for the impairment test (10.0% Tornum to 12.0% S:t Eriks). This is above-average disclosure for a Swedish small cap.

2. What it withholds, and why each gap matters for the next six months.

  • No order intake or backlog for S:t Eriks or Tornum. Tornum's Q3 fall was a backlog event (Lantmännen complete, not replaced) that the report disclosed only after the fact; the same can happen in Q4 (seasonally Tornum's largest quarter) with no warning. DNB had to build the Lantmännen bridge from the Q2 2025 call.
  • No quantification of the cost programmes or the co-location savings. The second set's +SEK 45m to 50m of 2027 savings is its own estimate; management has said "savings from Q1 2027" without a number, so a Q1 2027 Ettiketto margin of 14% or 16% cannot be judged against a target.
  • No rental utilisation at Corroventa. The only test is the quarterly margin; the segment's Q4 depends on rainfall in November to December, which is observable (SMHI) but not disclosed in relation to fleet use.
  • No price-cost disclosure at S:t Eriks. The platform's margin depends on aggregates (+7.9%) and cement (+3.8%) against tender prices; the report says only that infrastructure was stable and water and wastewater weaker.
  • Leverage definition in the annual report only. The 3.1x cannot be reconstructed from the interim report alone (adjusted EBITDA of SEK 426m is pro forma for acquisitions and after lease costs).
  • No guidance and no capital markets day. The first set called this the single cheapest catalyst management controls; nothing in the Q3 presentation or call addressed it.
  • Reported EPS after the preference dividend without an adjusted EPS line; the February 2027 report will show FY2026 ordinary EPS near zero with no company-provided bridge.

3. The external proxies that fill the gaps, platform by platform, for the six months to April 2027.

  • Ettiketto: Swedish grocery volumes (Statistics Sweden monthly retail; Bernstein and DNB on Axfood, ICA), Haypp's nicotine-pouch volumes (quarterly), European label-stock and freight prices (Jefferies' monthly paper and packaging tracker), the EU TPD3 draft (late 2026).
  • Communication: Nordic telecom capital spending commentary (Telia, Tele2 results), Swedish defence and grid procurement.
  • Corroventa: SMHI and Met Office rainfall and flood warnings for October to March; Nordic insurers' weather-claims commentary (Sampo, Topdanmark).
  • S:t Eriks: Byggfakta's construction-starts indicator, SCB housing starts, Trafikverket tenders, Veidekke's spring update, Peab's and NCC's Q4 reports (January to February), Morgan Stanley's monthly Swedish cement and aggregates price tracker.
  • Tornum: AGI's Q4 report and order commentary, Kepler Weber's Q4, the CEMA business barometer (monthly), CAP adoption in Q1 2027, Lantmännen's tertial report, Swedish grant disbursement news.
  • Group: the Riksbank decisions (November 2026, early 2027), DNB's mid-quarter compounder updates, and the preference share price as a market read on dividend safety.

4. The practical monitoring plan that the disclosure supports. Two hard checks (the second set's milestone table for February and April 2027) and a soft dashboard of the proxies above. The checks are good: they are specific, numerical and at the platform level the company reports. The dashboard is adequate for direction and useless for magnitude. The six-month monitoring question therefore resolves to "can the memo tell by April 2027 whether 2027 is on track?" and the answer is yes, with a one-quarter lag and no early warning of a backlog or weather shock.

5. Against peers. Lifco and Indutrade disclose less (no platform returns on capital) but need less, because their errors are small. Sdiptech gives segment organic growth and a stated acquisition pace. Storskogen publishes a margin drop-through. Volati's disclosure is better than the first and worse than the last two where it matters most (drop-through, backlog).

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

F408th of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The company's statements trace onto lines but not onto a path. Its platform margin targets (Ettiketto 20%, Corroventa 22%, Communication 14%, S:t Eriks and Tornum 10%) name the line (platform EBITA margin) and the end-state but no timing, no building blocks and no intermediate milestones; its only group-level promise ("2023 earnings levels or higher in normal markets") names a number (SEK 472m on the 2023 perimeter) with no date; its 15% EBITA-per-share target names the output with no inputs. The dated operational statements of 2026 (cost measures in six to nine months, production realignment in H2 2026, co-location savings from Q1 2027, seasonal Q4 cash) do trace onto specific lines (S:t Eriks and Tornum margins, Ettiketto margin, net debt), and they are the only part of management's communication that can be tested quarter by quarter. The transmission to EPS is clear in mechanics and opaque in presentation: the company reports EPS after the preference dividend with no adjusted line, so the market's EPS figures sit on three bases and the sell-side's bridge from EBITA to EPS is the only one that exists. The external transmission is clear in direction for every platform and unclear in magnitude for the two that matter, because at S:t Eriks and Tornum the lever between the end market and the margin is a production or pricing decision taken inside the plant, which the outside cannot see.

Reasoning.

1. Tracing the company's statements.

Statement Operational driver Line Milestone or measure Trace to EPS
15% EBITA per share growth over a cycle none stated group EBITA, share count none; missed 2023 to 2025 arithmetic only
"2023 level or higher in normal markets" end-market normalisation group EBITA SEK 472m plus acquisitions no date about SEK 1.4 of adjusted EPS above 2026
Platform margin targets mix, integration, volume platform EBITA margins no timing S:t Eriks and Tornum at 10% alone would be worth about SEK 2.0 of EPS
Cost programmes effective in 6 to 9 months (29 April 2026) headcount, plant costs S:t Eriks and Tornum margins Q3 2026 margin hold at S:t Eriks (met) about SEK 0.25 to 0.30 of EPS in 2027
Production realigned in H2 2026 absorption S:t Eriks margin Q3 2026 (met) about SEK 0.10
Co-location savings from Q1 2027 plant consolidation in Germany Ettiketto margin Q1 2027 report about SEK 0.15 to 0.20
Seasonally strong Q4 cash, lower leverage working capital net debt, leverage FY2026 report interest line, and the acquisition restart
Bolt-ons at all five platforms within 12 months (April 2026) capital allocation acquired growth three deals done, all Ettiketto and Corroventa small
Dividend 10% to 30% of net profit payout ordinary dividend AGM April 2027 cash, not EPS

The dated operational statements trace well and have mostly been met. The ambitions do not trace at all: there is no bridge from SEK 318m to SEK 472m or to 10% at S:t Eriks. The second set had to build the bridge (SEK 80m in 2027: 26 + 16 + 16 + 16 + 6), and that bridge is the memo's, not the company's.

2. The internal transmission to EPS. The mechanics are simple and the second set has written them out: ordinary EPS = (adjusted EBITA - amortisation - items - net financial items) x (1 - tax) - minorities - SEK 64m, over 79.4m shares. The problem is presentation. The company reports only reported EPS after the preference dividend (SEK -0.07 in Q3, SEK 0.02 for nine months), and no adjusted figure; DNB adds back amortisation and deducts the preference dividend; Nordea's 2026 figure is after items; FactSet averages them. A reader cannot get from the company's own numbers to the EPS the market quotes without a broker note, and the first set found DNB's own 2026 figure internally inconsistent. The February 2027 report will make this worse before the memo is read: a FY2026 ordinary EPS of about SEK 0.05 against a FactSet "consensus" of SEK 1.30.

3. The external transmission, platform by platform.

  • Ettiketto (clear direction, lagged magnitude): Swedish food volumes and nicotine-pouch volumes to label volumes (one to two quarters); label-stock, freight and energy prices to margin (three to six months under index clauses); EU packaging regulation to the acquisition pipeline (years). Clear enough to monitor; the VAT cliff of December 2027 is a known future shock.
  • Communication (lumpy): telecom and defence project timing to quarterly sales; steel safeguard to margin without indexation. Direction clear, timing not.
  • Corroventa (random): rainfall to rental utilisation to margin, within a quarter; Tramex damps it. Direction clear, magnitude unquantified by the company.
  • S:t Eriks (opaque in the middle): housing starts and civil engineering budgets to volume (lag of two to four quarters), cement and aggregate prices to cost (one to two quarters), but the margin in any quarter is set by the plant's production rate against sales, as Q2 2026 showed (EBITA -57% on sales +1%). The outside sees the end market and the input prices; it does not see the production decision. Heidelberg's consolidation adds a structural price-cost variable nobody has quantified.
  • Tornum (opaque at both ends): grain prices, CAP and grants to farm investment (lag of two to four quarters), competitor discounting to price (project by project), the cooperative order book to deliveries (lumpy; one project was half a year's sales). Neither the backlog nor the price is disclosed.

4. The transmission to the multiple. Clearer than the transmission to EPS: the sector's rule (the multiple is the market's guess at how long the engine runs; organic growth is the evidence) and DNB's rule (compounder multiples track the gap between earnings yield and swap rates) both apply, and both currently point down (organic -3%, rates rising). The lever the company controls, leverage below 2.5x and a restart of bolt-ons, is identifiable and dated by the second set (Q2 to Q3 2027).

5. Net. An analyst can monitor this case; an investor reading the company's own communication cannot. The clarity gap is between management's dated operational promises (good) and its undated ambitions (useless), and between the mechanics of EPS (simple) and the presentation of EPS (confusing). A capital markets day with platform margin paths and a group EPS bridge would move this score by 15 to 20 points; none is announced.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

F1519th of 19

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. Sentiment risks are high and largely outside Volati's control, and the capital structure magnifies them. Volati is exactly the profile the current regime sells first: small, illiquid, leveraged, cyclical, multi-part and in a downtrend.

Reasoning.

  1. Rates and the serial-acquirer de-rating. Swedish compounder multiples track the gap between earnings yield and swap rates (DNB). A Riksbank hike in November 2026, or ECB rises, could compress the multiple for the whole cohort. With equity at about 43% of EV, a 10% fall in EV is about a 23% fall in equity.
  2. A sector short-seller or accounting event. Lifco fell 16% on The Analyst's report of January 2026. A new campaign against any Nordic acquirer, or against Volati's own earn-out or goodwill accounting, would hit hard.
  3. Peer disappointments. Storskogen's construction-exposed Services unit, Nordic builders and installers, and European agricultural-equipment makers (John Deere and Kepler Weber readings) all transmit read-across.
  4. Post-spin selling and liquidity. Average daily value traded is USD 0.3m to 0.4m. Index reweighting, or exits by holders who owned the stock for Salix, can move the price far more than fundamentals.
  5. The regime. Risk-off, defensives leading, a Fragility Double Underweight and a Momentum Underweight. Small-cap growth "cracks before large caps", and in a falling market "the cleanest case in the sector wins". Volati is the least clean case in its cohort.
  6. Coverage. A downgrade from Nordea's new Buy, or a further cut from DNB, would move a two-broker consensus sharply.

C-61How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?F3519th of 19full answer under 4b Management quality
C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

F3019th of 19

Layer 1 · Guidance

Judgement. There is no record of upgrading guidance, because none is given. The behaviour on targets is fixed: the 15% target has never been re-based through four years of misses. The behaviour on outlook has been early and optimistic about recovery. This is an "honest tracker" on admitting misses, not a "sandbagger" who under-promises and then raises.

Reasoning. The CEO refuses short-term guidance ("Volati is best evaluated over time") and admits misses openly. He expected Swedish construction to return to organic growth "during 2025 to 2026" (it did not). He framed Q2 2026 as "the cyclical trough". In Q1 2026 he stated an elevated ambition to "grow above financial targets once markets normalise". The pre-spin "SEK 1.1bn to 1.5bn by 2027" was an arithmetical catch-up to the target, not an operating plan. Compare Indus, which raised 2026 targets by 45% (classic sandbagging), and Lifco, which refuses guidance but has delivered about 20% compounding.

C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

F1519th of 19

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. The record is poor and consistent. Volati has missed sell-side EBITA estimates in each of the last three full years and in each of the last three reported quarters before Q3 2026. It has missed its own growth target in three of the last four years. The misses cluster in comparable conditions (construction and agricultural downturns, dry weather), which is exactly the environment ahead.

Reasoning.

  • Against DNB EBITA estimates. FY2023 -2%; FY2024 -17%; FY2025 -7%; Q4 2025 a miss (postponed S:t Eriks deliveries; target cut from SEK 132 to SEK 120); Q1 2026 -35% (SEK 41m against about SEK 63m); Q2 2026 -18% (SEK 91m against SEK 111m). Q2 sales beat by about 3%, so the miss is in margin, not revenue.
  • Against the 15% EBITA-per-share target. 2022 +23% (beat); 2023 +13%; 2024 -17%; 2025 -18%; LTM -31%.
  • Over five years. The last clear beat cycle was 2021 to 2022, in a buoyant environment.
  • Q3 2026. Not in the files; the share fell 4% on the day.
P-06

After assessing the track record of delivery in greater detail, how much weight should the forecasts for the next 18 months to four years carry, and in which direction should they be leaned?

F3815th of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The weight should differ by line, because the record is not uniformly poor. Volati's misses are concentrated in one place: the margins of the two cyclical platforms, S:t Eriks and Tornum, and the timing of their recovery. Its record on structural actions (the Salix listing, the refinancing, cost programmes, acquisitions), on Ettiketto and Communication, and on cash is good. So: high weight on Q4 2026 and on the self-help in 2027 (about ±5% on EBITA); medium weight on 2027 as a whole (about ±15%); low weight on 2028 to 2030 (about ±20% to 25%). Lean revenue down against consensus in every year, lean S:t Eriks and Tornum margins and their recovery timing down by one to two quarters, and lean neither way on Ettiketto, Corroventa and cash. My base case already carries these leans. The probability distribution around it is skewed down: a probability-weighted 2027 adjusted EBITA is about SEK 388m against a base case of SEK 398m.

Reasoning.

1. The record, by what was missed.

Period EBITA against DNB Carnegie's estimate Where the miss came from
FY2023 -2% Construction and agriculture
FY2024 -17% Tornum (margin 0.2% from 9.1%), S:t Eriks
FY2025 -7% Corroventa (dry year), S:t Eriks (postponed infrastructure deliveries in Q4)
Q1 2026 -35% (SEK 41m against about SEK 63m) S:t Eriks (SEK -16m), Tornum (SEK -15m)
Q2 2026 -18% (SEK 91m against SEK 111m) S:t Eriks (SEK 20m against SEK 53m expected), Tornum (SEK 6m against SEK 21m), Corroventa
Q3 2026 Underlying EBITA +15%; roughly in line with, or slightly below, what DNB's full-year SEK 321m implies; share -4% on the day Tornum (SEK -9m), S:t Eriks flat

In Q2 2026, sales beat by about 3% while EBITA missed by 18%. Ettiketto (EBITA up 18% in Q1, 22% in Q3) and Communication (Q2 EBITA +56%) beat or met. The pattern is consistent: the brokers' errors are in the cyclical platforms' margins, which depend on volume, production decisions and price, and which management's own commentary repeatedly called too early (construction "would return to organic growth during 2025 to 2026").

2. Management's record on its own statements. The 15% EBITA-per-share target has been missed in 2023, 2024 and 2025. But the shorter-dated operational promises of 2026 are being kept: the six-to-nine-month cost programmes show in Q3 margins; S:t Eriks raised production in H2 as said; the Salix listing came on 15 June as planned; Tramex and NicEtikett closed in July. The lesson is to trust what management controls and discount what it forecasts about markets.

3. How the leans are built into my base case (so the next analyst does not haircut twice).

  • Revenue: 2% to 4% below consensus in each year (organic growth of about 2% in 2027 against about 4% in consensus).
  • Tornum: SEK -8m in 2027 against DNB's SEK +5m; 1% margin in 2028.
  • S:t Eriks: 3.4% in 2027 and 4.0% in 2028, close to DNB's 3% and 3.9%; no recovery before 2028.
  • Ettiketto integration: Interket and Clever at 11% to 12% by 2028, not the 12% to 15% the first set allowed.
  • Head office: no saving.
  • Not leaned: Corroventa (Tramex is a known quantity; weather is random in both directions), Communication, cash and the dividend cut.

4. The distribution around the base case.

2027 adjusted EBITA Bear (30%) Base (50%) Bull (20%)
SEK m about 330 398 about 450
Adjusted EPS (SEK) about 1.53 2.24 about 2.76
Leverage at end-2027 (company definition) about 2.5x 2.1x about 1.8x

The bear case is more likely than the bull because three of its four ingredients have already happened at least once in the last eight quarters (a dry year at Corroventa, a Tornum loss, an integration margin stuck near 5% to 6%), while the bull needs a Swedish construction upturn that rising rates make unlikely in 2027. The skew is wider in 2028 (bear about SEK 370m, bull about SEK 540m against a base of SEK 462m) because the cyclical platforms carry more of the growth.

5. What would change the weighting. Two successive quarters in which S:t Eriks and Tornum meet expectations (Q4 2026, reported in February 2027, and Q1 2027, reported in April 2027) would raise the weight on 2027 to high. A third consecutive Tornum loss in Q4 2026 above SEK 10m, or Ettiketto's Q1 2027 margin below 14% after the co-location, would lower it.

Forecasts with good growth rates and higher growth/margins than past

Growth rate

Group judgement

Financial forecasts

D5010th of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The forecasts point to a strong, mostly mechanical earnings recovery from a trough, financed by a balance sheet that heals faster than the first set expected, but they do not yet describe a compounder.

  1. 2027 is the most predictable year in the forecast, and the recovery in it is real. About 60% to 65% of the +SEK 80m of EBITA is mechanical or self-help already visible in the Q3 2026 run-rate: the end of S:t Eriks' self-inflicted under-absorption, the cost programmes at S:t Eriks and Tornum, Ettiketto's co-location, and Tramex for a full year. Financial leverage turns +25% EBITA into about +50% adjusted EPS.
  2. The 2026 numbers in the market are not what they seem. Adjusted EBITA for 2026 is roughly where DNB has it (about SEK 318m against SEK 321m). But DNB's SEK 1.98 EPS is about SEK 0.50 above what its own bridge supports, Nordea's SEK 0.62 is on a heavier basis (after items affecting comparability), and the FactSet average of SEK 1.30 means nothing. Reported continuing EPS for 2026 will be close to zero.
  3. Revenue is the line most likely to disappoint consensus, by 2% to 4% a year, because organic growth stays near 2% to 3%, while margins come in close to DNB.
  4. The balance sheet is the hidden positive. The company's leverage falls from 3.1x to about 2.1x by end-2027, through EBITDA, the dividend cut and Q4 seasonality. The leverage-and-acquisition loop flips to reinforcing in H2 2027, and acquisitions, which consensus does not model, become the main source of upgrades for 2028 to 2030.
  5. The cone widens sharply after 2027, because growth then depends on Nordic rates through S:t Eriks, Tornum and, indirectly, the pace of acquisitions. The skew is to the downside: three of the four bear-case ingredients have already occurred in the last eight quarters.
  6. Pricing power does not help. Price recovers costs at Ettiketto and is negative at S:t Eriks and Tornum, so the margin recovery has to be bought through cost and integration, not price.

For the third set: test the 2027 self-help against the Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 prints, and treat 2028 to 2030 as a cyclical and capital-allocation call. For the fourth set: the market's revenue numbers are too high, its margin numbers are about right for 2027, its 2026 EPS numbers are on inconsistent bases, and its 2028 to 2030 numbers leave out the acquisition restart.

P-08

After assessing below-operating-profit cost items (e.g. tax, interest, one-offs, minority investments, etc.) and share buybacks or issuance in greater detail, how is it likely to shape earnings per share, net income and free cash flow over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

F4214th of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. Below operating profit, every line is either fixed or slowly rising, so below-the-line items magnify the operating recovery rather than absorb it. The decisive line is the SEK 64m preference dividend: it takes about 20% of 2026 adjusted EBITA (against about 10% of pre-spin group EBITA), and it is why reported ordinary EPS is close to zero in 2026 (about SEK 0.05) before rising to about SEK 1.13 in 2027 and SEK 3.0 by 2030. The gap between adjusted and reported EPS is large and persistent (about SEK 1.1 to 1.4 a share), almost all of it amortisation of acquired intangibles, which rises with each acquisition. There will be no buybacks and no material issuance. Free cash flow after the preference dividend roughly doubles from about SEK 85m in 2026 to about SEK 167m in 2027, helped as much by the absence of 2026's one-off costs and the dividend cut as by EBITDA.

Reasoning.

1. The EPS bridge, line by line (continuing operations).

2. Each line, its behaviour and what would move it.

  • Items affecting comparability. 2026 carries about SEK 47m: about SEK 14m in Q2 (separation and restructuring), SEK 29m in Q3 (SEK 8m transaction costs for Tramex and NicEtikett, SEK 19m Ettiketto co-location, about SEK 2m other) and a few million in Q4. I allow SEK 5m a year afterwards, because a serial acquirer integrating converters will keep booking some. The Salix capital gain of SEK 4,044m sits in discontinued operations and must be excluded.
  • Amortisation of acquired intangibles. SEK 46m in H1 2026, about SEK 27m a quarter after Tramex and NicEtikett. Acquisitions in January to September 2026 added SEK 106m of intangible assets and SEK 215m of goodwill (preliminary allocations; final in the 2026 annual report). Each SEK 150m to 200m of new bolt-ons adds about SEK 4m to 6m a year. Non-cash, and it reverses deferred tax, so I add it back after tax at the full rate.
  • Net financial items. About SEK 18m to 19m a quarter from Q3 2026 on net debt of about SEK 1.3bn at about 4.5%, plus lease interest of about SEK 12m a year and SEK 5m of other items. It falls slowly as net debt falls, and rises again from 2029 as acquisitions resume. Pre-spin consolidated net interest was SEK 150m to 173m a year (2023 to 2025) on about twice the debt, so the absolute charge has halved, but against an EBITA that has also halved.
  • Tax. DNB uses 21.4% (2026) and 22.0%; 2025 was 21.8%. I use 22.0% and then 22.5%, because the share of profit earned in Germany, the UK, the Netherlands and Ireland rises with Interket, Clever and Tramex. Pillar Two produced no top-up tax (2025 annual report). The preference dividend is not deductible.
  • Minorities. About SEK 10m to 13m. Put-option liabilities fell to SEK 48m from SEK 287m because SEK 239m left with Salix; remeasurements go through equity, not the income statement.
  • Shares. 79,406,571 ordinary shares, unchanged. All warrant programmes total 1.34% dilution if exercised, but their strikes were recalculated after the Salix distribution and they are bought at market, so dilution is immaterial. No buybacks: leverage, not the share price, sets capital allocation, and management prioritises acquisitions.
  • Preference shares. Unchanged in the base case (P-02). A partial redemption from 2028 is the main upside to the bridge (a third of the shares, about SEK 330m, would add about SEK 0.12 a share).
  • Goodwill. Ettiketto SEK 237m, S:t Eriks SEK 274m, Scanmast SEK 140m, Tornum SEK 139m and Corroventa SEK 84m at end-2025; a one-point rise in the discount rate gave no impairment in 2025. Tornum is the exposure at the year-end 2026 test. An impairment would be non-cash and outside adjusted EPS.

Margins/growth vs. past

C-53How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?F2519th of 19full answer under 4c Financial profile
C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

F5517th of 19

Layer 1 · Company - Economic strength

Judgement. The mid-term scope is large relative to current earnings. Normalised EBITA of about SEK 550m to 600m against an LTM of SEK 262m is consistent with management's "2023 level or higher in normal markets" once acquisitions are included. But it comes from a few big, mostly cyclical areas, and peer precedents show this kind of repair takes two to three years. Concentration in a few areas makes the outcome binary in timing. The scope is congruent with the stated strategy.

Areas of scope, approximate EBITA uplift from 2026e.

  1. S:t Eriks. From about 2% to a 6% to 9% margin on about SEK 1.3bn of sales: +SEK 50m to 90m. Needs Swedish construction recovery and the mix shift.
  2. Tornum. From a loss to a 5% to 8% margin on about SEK 0.9bn: +SEK 70m to 100m. Needs farm investment to return and grain overcapacity to clear.
  3. Ettiketto integration. Interket and Clever from about 5% to 12% to 15% on about SEK 740m of combined sales: +SEK 50m to 75m. Within management's control, over 2026 to 2028.
  4. Corroventa. Normal weather plus Tramex: +SEK 20m to 35m.
  5. Central costs and separation costs not recurring: +SEK 10m to 30m.

Items 1 and 2 are cyclical and make up about half the total. Items 3 to 5 are self-help.

Peer precedents.

  • Storskogen's pruning lifted its pro-forma margin 70bp, and it took two to three years to return to EBITA growth.
  • Sdiptech's divestments lifted core margin by about 150bp to 200bp within a year.
  • Indus's exit from automotive lifted long-term ROCE above 10%.
  • Lifco's Dental mix shift added 240bp.
  • These show self-help is achievable. Cyclical recovery is outside management's control.

Congruence. Yes: margin-first commercial policy, integration programmes, platform focus.

C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

F2019th of 19

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. Yes, on both counts. The organic engine has been negative for three years and was -2% in H1 2026. The acquisition engine was run hard in 2026 but is now constrained by leverage. The input forces (Swedish construction, European farm investment, flood frequency) are not yet turning, and the rate cycle is moving against them.

Reasoning.

  • Organic growth (RemainCo or legacy basis): about -5% (2023), about -7% (2024), -4% (2025), -6% (Q1 2026), +2% (Q2 2026), and provisionally a further fall at Tornum in Q3 (-17% to -20%). Volati's own normal is organic growth above GDP (3% to 5% above Nordic GDP is claimed), with a historical 50/50 split of organic and acquired EBITA growth since 2017.
  • EBITA growth per share: -31% LTM, against a 15% target.
  • Acquired growth: about 9 points of Q2 sales growth. It cannot continue at the 2026 pace without EBITDA recovery (C-18, C-50).
  • Input forces: housing starts at a fraction of normal; farm capital spending frozen, with EU support cuts ahead; two dry years for restoration; rates turning up. The only accelerating input forces sit at Communication (defence, Asian projects) and in Ettiketto's demand, partly flattered by a tax change.
C-67

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

F4217th of 19

Layer 1 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. Sell-side forecasts are not heroic by history. Their 2026 to 2028 EBITA margins (about 6.8%, 8.0% and 8.7%) stay below 2024 (9.1%) and 2023 (10.5%). The recovery they model is mostly self-help plus Tornum moving from loss to breakeven, not a cyclical recovery. The credibility of the gap therefore depends on timing:

  • 2026 is at risk of a 5% to 10% cut, because its H2 hockey stick has no visible support after Q3.
  • 2027 is credible as a central case with balanced to slightly negative risk.
  • 2028 has upside only if the cycle turns.

Revenue. 2026e SEK 4.80bn (+11.5%: about 10 points from Interket, NicEtikett and Tramex, organic about 0% to 2%); 2027e SEK 5.14bn (+7%: about 2 to 3 points from annualisation, about 4% organic); 2028e SEK 5.33bn (+3.6%). That compares with 2023 to 2025 RemainCo revenue of about SEK 4.5bn, 4.3bn and 4.3bn and negative organic growth. The 2027 organic assumption of about 4% is above anything since 2022 and depends on Tornum's comparisons.

EBITA. DNB 2026e SEK 326m (+2.5% on 2025) and 2027e SEK 408m (+25%). Reconstructed from DNB's platform assumptions, the 2027 increase of about SEK 82m breaks down as:

  • Tornum about +SEK 32m (from about -SEK 27m to +SEK 5m, mostly cost cuts and lapping the Lantmännen roll-off);
  • Ettiketto about +SEK 27m (integration and growth, to a margin of about 15.8%);
  • S:t Eriks about +SEK 16m (from 1.8% to about 3%);
  • Corroventa about +SEK 13m (Tramex for a full year, normal weather);
  • Communication about flat.

About three-quarters of this is self-help or comparison-driven, which makes it credible.

Below EBITA. Consensus EBIT of SEK 221m, 329m and 384m implies amortisation of about SEK 85m to 105m. Pre-tax profit of SEK 134m and 260m implies net financial items of about SEK 87m and 69m. DNB's net profit for 2026e is SEK 140m. The basis issues are set out in the Preface. Dividend per share: SEK 0.56 and 0.675, against SEK 2.00 paid. Free cash flow: SEK 55m, 171m and 213m. Capex: SEK 108m to 122m (about 2.3% of sales, above the 1.4% to 1.6% historical rate).

Credibility. 2026 H2 needs about SEK 193m of EBITA against SEK 129m in H2 2025. The Q3 fragments point the other way, and DNB cut its 2026 EPS 3% before the report. 2027 is reachable without a recovery if the integration delivers and Tornum breaks even. The analyst's own central range for 2027 EBITA is SEK 370m to 400m, with a bear case of about SEK 320m to 350m and a bull case of about SEK 450m to 480m.

P-04After assessing the pricing power in greater detail, how is price likely to contribute to revenue and margin over the next 18 months to four years?F3814th of 16full answer under 5a Pricing power
P-09

After assessing the other questions/items in the set in greater detail, what is the net likely impact to the main financial line items over the next 18 months to four years, versus the last four to six years?

D529th of 16

Layer 2 · Financial forecasts

Judgement. The net impact is a reversal of the 2023 to 2026 decline, driven first by self-help and then by the cycle and acquisitions, but not a return to compounding until 2029 at the earliest. Revenue grows about 6.5% a year from 2025 to 2030 (about 2.5% organic, about 4% acquired), against a decline of about 2% a year from 2023 to 2025. The EBITA margin recovers from 6.7% in 2026 to 8.1% in 2027 and about 10% by 2030, back to 2023's level only at the end of the period. Adjusted EPS rises about 50% in 2027 and about 30% a year on average from 2026 to 2030, but from a trough, and mainly because fixed claims shrink as a share of EBITA. The drivers are diffuse in 2027 and concentrated afterwards, which is the single most important fact for the third set.

Reasoning.

1. The main line items against history.

Leverage, company definition (end of period) about 2.9x to 3.0x 2.1x 1.4x

2. The concentration of the drivers changes over time.

  • 2027 is diffuse. The +SEK 80m comes from four sources of similar size (Ettiketto +26, Corroventa +16, S:t Eriks +16, Tornum +16), three of them mechanical or within management's control. No single driver failing would cut 2027 EBITA by more than about 5%.
  • 2028 to 2030 is concentrated. The +SEK 200m comes from three blocks: S:t Eriks and Tornum recovery (about SEK 65m, cyclical, rate-dependent), new acquisitions (about SEK 55m, dependent on leverage and the loop in P-05) and Ettiketto integration plus growth (about SEK 55m). The first two are correlated: if Swedish rates stay high, construction stays weak and EBITDA growth slows, so leverage falls more slowly and acquisitions are delayed. About 60% of the 2028 to 2030 growth therefore rests on one macro variable: Nordic rates.

3. The returns profile. Return on capital employed including goodwill was 8% over the last twelve months to Q3 2026 (17% excluding goodwill). On my base case it reaches about 12% in 2028 and about 14% in 2030: above the cost of capital, but still below the 13% to 18% of proven Nordic acquirers, because a quarter of revenue (S:t Eriks and Tornum) earns low returns throughout.

4. What has changed structurally against the last six years, and what has not. Changed: Salix's stable earnings and cash are gone; the preference dividend's weight has doubled; the interest charge has halved; the ordinary dividend has fallen by about 85%; Ettiketto is now about two-thirds of platform EBITA (about 55% in 2025) and carries two integration programmes. Not changed: dependence on Swedish construction and European farm investment for about a third of normal-year EBITA; decentralised purchasing; management's preference for acquisitions over buybacks.

Forecasts and upgrades

8

Probably upgrades this financial year to

–not yet rated

Memo topic 7, : the Rater's score

Behind the rating as a whole

Group judgement

The fourth set as a whole

C+503rd of 8

Layer 4 · The fourth set as a whole

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The market's position on Volati can be stated in one sentence: one broker rates it Hold at SEK 25 on the view that leverage blocks the engine and the end markets have not turned, no investor says anything about it in public, and the dashboard reads F, F and A. Against that, our assessment is: the 2027 recovery is mostly self-help already in the run-rate, in line with the broker's own 2027 number; the balance sheet repairs faster than the broker's text implies and about as fast as its model implies; and the things that would change the narrative are dated and testable (February and April 2027), with a low bar.

Guidance (I-01) offers no favourable gap because there is none to raise; the company's dated operational statements are being met and its undated ambitions are above us and ignored by the market. Sell-side estimates (I-02) offer a modest favourable gap over 18 months: our 2027 and 2028 sit with DNB's, DNB's 2027 for the cyclical platforms is now below ours, its 2028 has no recovery in it, and no 2029 exists; the gap is masked for the first six months by a composite that will keep reading as cuts for mechanical reasons (Nordea's stale basis, the dividend reset, the reported EPS of zero). The narrative (I-03) offers the largest favourable gap: the positives that our assessment rests on (easy comparisons, the co-location saving, leverage below 2.5x, a bolt-on restart, the preference-share option) are either unarticulated or framed as constraints, and there is no investor narrative to have priced them.

The qualifications are three. The unarticulated negatives (Heidelberg's upstream consolidation, the grain-storage price war, the VAT cliff) will surface in the same window and the memo should name them before the market does. The gap is favourable only if the sequence holds: the Q4 2026 cash release before any acquisition, then two prints. And with no buyer base, a closing of the gap would be slow; the reward for being right is a re-rating from the "crash" band towards the "conservative" band over 12 to 18 months, not a step change on a print.

For the memo's topics #7 and #8: scope for upgrades over the coming 6 to 12 months is moderate and specific (2027 by 0% to 6% from April 2027; 2028 by 3% to 8% from October 2027); current momentum in guidance, estimates and narrative is negative and will remain so on the screens until mid-2027, while the peers' momentum is positive, which is the mirror image of where the fundamental gap sits.

I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

F427th of 8

Layer 4 · Guidance

Judgement. Volati gives no numerical guidance, so the question becomes: will its dated qualitative statements be met, and will its standing targets and ambitions be re-based? My answer for the six earnings events in the window (Q4 2026 in February 2027, Q1 2027 in April, Q2 in July, Q3 in October, Q4 2027 in February 2028, Q1 2028 in April 2028) is that the dated operational statements are more likely to be met than not at each of the first three events (about 60% to 65% each), that "guidance" in the sense of the tone of the outlook is more likely to be softened than firmed at the first event (February 2027) and more likely to be firmed than softened from the second (April 2027), and that nothing will be formally raised. The standing targets will be formally missed for 2026 (adjusted EBITA per share about -4%), formally met in 2027 and 2028 (+25% and +16%, from the trough) and neither raised nor re-based unless a capital markets day is called, which I put at a 30% to 40% probability within 18 months and which would, if it happens, most likely set 2028 to 2030 platform margin paths below the current targets (a re-basing down in substance, presented as an increase in specificity). For 18 months to three years out, the "2023 level or higher in normal markets" ambition (SEK 472m on the 2023 perimeter; SEK 550m to 600m with acquisitions) will not be met on our base case before 2028 to 2029 on the enlarged perimeter and not before 2030 on the existing one; it is more likely to be quietly dropped or re-dated than raised. Our assessment therefore sits in line with management's near-term tone and below its medium-term ambition, which is an unfavourable gap in the sense that the ambition will be the thing that disappoints, and a neutral one in the sense that the market gives the ambition no weight.

Reasoning.

1. What there is to be met, event by event.

Event Dated statement to test Likely outcome What drives "met" What drives "reduced" (softer tone, pushed-out timing)
Q4 2026 (Feb 2027) Seasonally strong Q4 cash flow; leverage down from 3.1x; cost programmes delivering; German co-location complete Met on cash and leverage (70%): working-capital release of SEK 60m to 100m and no new deal would give about 2.7x to 2.8x; met on margins at S:t Eriks (easy Q4 2025 comparison after postponed deliveries); risk at Tornum (seasonally its largest quarter; Lantmännen roll-off) and Corroventa (DNB expects SEK 21m of EBITA at 20.5%, which needs a wet quarter) A dry quarter and a Tornum loss above SEK 10m; a goodwill impairment at Tornum (SEK 139m) at the year-end test; a dividend proposal at or near zero under the 10% to 30% policy; the first disclosure of FY2026 ordinary EPS near zero
Q1 2027 (Apr) Co-location savings from Q1 2027; comparisons against Q1 2026 adjusted EBITA of SEK 41m Met (65%): adjusted EBITA SEK 60m to 70m is mostly comparison arithmetic; Ettiketto margin 14% or above would confirm the saving Ettiketto below 14% after the co-location; a Riksbank hike in the spring; Nordea cutting on its first post-Q3 update
Q2 2027 (Jul) Leverage below 2.5x; first bolt-on Met on leverage (60%); the bolt-on is likely (management's revealed preference) but may come before 2.5x Communication's hard comparison (Q2 2026 +25% sales on Asian deliveries); a second acquisition pushing leverage back towards 3x
Q3 2027 (Oct) Interket near 9%; Tornum order intake Even (50%) The VAT cliff beginning to show in Swedish food volumes; CAP rules reducing farm investment
Q4 2027 (Feb 2028) and Q1 2028 (Apr 2028) 2027 at or above DNB's SEK 2.32 of adjusted EPS; 15% target "met" from the trough Met (55%) on our base; the first year management can claim the target Tornum still loss-making; Swedish construction still at 2026 levels if rates stay above 2%

2. Why the near-term tone is more likely softened than firmed at the first event. Three items that are not operational will dominate the February 2027 report's reception: a FY2026 ordinary EPS of about SEK 0.05 against a FactSet consensus of SEK 1.30 (a presentation artefact, but a headline); an ordinary dividend proposal that under the policy would be near nil (SEK 2.00 was paid for 2025; FactSet has SEK 0.56); and the year-end goodwill test at Tornum (return on capital employed excluding goodwill -14%; post-tax discount rate 10.0%). Management's outlook language in Q3 ("do not anticipate a dramatic shift" in construction; Tornum "no immediate market recovery") will likely be repeated. None of this changes our base case; all of it reads as "reduced" to a market with one active broker.

3. Why the tone is more likely firmed from April 2027. The comparison base is the lowest quarter of the cycle (Q1 2026: S:t Eriks SEK -16m, Tornum SEK -15m, group SEK 41m). The co-location saving arrives. Cash conversion stays high because Q1 is a working-capital build but the dividend outflow is small (SEK 24m against SEK 159m in 2026). Management will be able to say the cost programmes delivered on the six-to-nine-month promise, which it has already begun to say. The probability of a firmer tone at the April and July events is about 55% to 60%.

4. The standing targets and the ambitions, 18 months to three years out.

  • 15% EBITA per share growth over a cycle. Not re-based through four years of misses; LTM -31% at Q3 2026. On our base it reads +25% (2027), +16% (2028), +15% (2029): "met" from the trough, missed over any cycle measured from 2023. Probability of being formally raised: under 5%. Probability of being re-based (replaced by platform-level targets, a return-on-capital target, or a leverage-first framework) at a capital markets day: 30% to 40% within 18 months. If a capital markets day comes, the memo should expect lower platform margin targets for S:t Eriks and Tornum (10% is unreachable on any visible path; DNB has 3.3% and 0.7% for 2028) and an explicit acquisition budget: more specificity, less ambition.
  • "2023 level or higher in normal markets." Our base reaches SEK 462m in 2028 and SEK 530m in 2029 including bolt-ons not yet made; on the existing perimeter about SEK 540m only in 2030. "Normal markets" has no date, so the statement cannot be missed; it can only be dropped. Probability of being raised: under 5%; of being repeated unchanged: 60%; of being re-dated or replaced: 35%.
  • Platform margin targets. Our 2030 margins are below all five. Ettiketto's 20% is the one most likely to be reaffirmed with a date (DNB expects the German synergies to restore it by 2028; we have 15.9% in 2028 and 16.7% in 2030, because Clever and Interket sit at 11% to 13%). Corroventa's 22% needs a flood year. Communication's 14% is within reach in a good project year. S:t Eriks' and Tornum's 10% are the ones a capital markets day would re-base.
  • Leverage 2.0x to 3.0x, maximum 3.5x. Will be met from end-2026 on our base (2.7x) and comfortably from mid-2027 (2.1x by year-end). Management may lower the target range once the preference shares are addressed; not within 18 months.
  • Dividend policy 10% to 30% of net profit. Will be "met" trivially because net profit is near zero; the number that matters (SEK 0 to 0.50 for 2026) will disappoint income holders and help deleveraging.

5. The record that conditions these probabilities. Operational promises with dates have been kept in 2026 (cost programmes, realignment, the listing, the refinancing, three acquisitions). Market calls have been early (construction recovery "during 2025 to 2026"; Q1 2025 "positive signs in Nordic building"). The company has never raised a target and never re-based one. The structure of its communication ("Volati is best evaluated over time"; "our quarters are hard to predict") is designed to avoid the question this item asks, and that is itself the finding: there is no guidance to be raised, and so no cheap positive catalyst from guidance, only from a capital markets day the company has not announced.

6. The gap between our assessment and guidance, stated plainly. Near term (to April 2027): in line; our Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 markers are what management's dated statements imply, and the risks are presentational. Twelve to 18 months: slightly above management's tone, because our 2027 includes self-help that management has stated but not quantified and an acquisition restart it has signalled but the broker frames as blocked. 18 months to three years: below management's ambitions (we reach 2023 levels only with acquisitions and only by 2029). An investment case prefers guidance to be below the analyst's view; here the near term is neutral and the medium term is the wrong way round, but with the mitigation that the market has stopped pricing the ambitions.
