I-02

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

A521st of 8

Layer 4 · Sell-side estimates

Judgement. Consensus here is one active broker (DNB Carnegie: Hold, SEK 25, adjusted EPS SEK 1.92, 2.32 and 2.88 for 2026 to 2028 after the Q3 review) plus one stale one (Nordea: Buy, SEK 39, EPS SEK 0.62, 2.02 and 2.70 from 2 October, on a different basis and not updated for Q3). Our base case is in line with DNB on 2027 and 2028 adjusted EBITA and EPS (SEK 398m and 2.24 against roughly SEK 395m to 400m and 2.32; SEK 462m and 2.89 against SEK 454m to 460m and 2.88), 2% to 4% below both brokers on revenue in every year, and between the two on 2026. The most likely sequence is: at the February 2027 event, 2026 is finalised near DNB's SEK 1.92 (our like-for-like SEK 1.49 is lower only because DNB's 2026 figure carries a pre-spin element), 2027 is held or trimmed by 0% to 3% and the FactSet composite falls mechanically when Nordea updates its 2026 figure and when the dividend consensus of SEK 0.56 meets a proposal near nil; at the April and July 2027 events, 2027 estimates are more likely raised than cut (about 45% raised by 0% to 5%, 35% held, 20% cut) as the easy comparisons and the co-location saving land; from the October 2027 event, 2028 estimates are more likely raised than cut (about 50% raised, 30% held, 20% cut) if leverage is below 2.5x and bolt-ons have restarted, since DNB's 2028 carries only about 3% revenue growth. Over the whole 18 months I put the probabilities for 2027 consensus EPS at: upgraded 40%, held within plus or minus 3% 35%, cut 25%; and for 2028: upgraded 45%, held 30%, cut 25%. Net breadth of revisions is not a meaningful measure for a two-broker stock: it will read negative through February 2027 for mechanical reasons whatever the operations do, and it can turn positive only when both brokers move the same way, most plausibly after April 2027. For 18 months to three years out there is no consensus to speak of (FactSet holds no 2029 figures); if the loop flips, the first 2029 numbers will be set by DNB's 5% acquired-growth assumption and will be below our 2029 (SEK 530m of EBITA) only if the restart is slower than our SEK 150m to 200m a year, so the secondary bias is to upgrades, conditional on the restart.

Reasoning.

1. Where consensus sits against our base, line by line (adjusted, continuing).

2026 2027 2028
Revenue: ours / DNB / Nordea (SEK m) 4,721 / 4,824 / 4,783 4,923 / 5,118 / 5,164 5,171 / 5,268 / 5,388
Adjusted EBITA: ours / DNB (post-Q3, approximate) 318 / 311 398 / about 395 to 400 462 / about 455 to 460
Adjusted EPS: ours / DNB / Nordea 1.49 / 1.92 (about 1.42 on DNB's own 2027 method) / 0.62 (after items) 2.24 / 2.32 / 2.02 2.89 / 2.88 / 2.70
EBITDA (IFRS 16): ours / DNB 550 / about 545 644 / 644 721 / 708
Dividend per share: ours / FactSet 0.30 / 0.56 0.55 / 0.675 0.75 / 0.53
Free cash flow after preference dividend: ours / FactSet 85 / 55 167 / 171 213 / 213

The like-for-like gap on the lines that matter (2027 and 2028 EBITA and EPS) is within 3%. The gaps are in revenue (we are lower), in 2026 EPS (a basis problem), in the dividend (we are lower for 2026 and 2027, higher for 2028) and, invisibly, in 2029 and beyond, where our acquisition restart adds EBITA that no published number carries.

2. Event by event.

  • February 2027 (Q4 2026). DNB's Q4 is about SEK 78m to 80m of adjusted EBITA (311 less 233 for nine months); ours about SEK 82m to 85m. The platform risks are Corroventa (DNB needs SEK 101m of sales and a 20.5% margin, which needs rain) and Tornum (no Lantmännen deliveries; the Q4 2025 comparison was its best quarter of the year). The platform supports are S:t Eriks (Q4 2025 had postponed infrastructure deliveries, so the comparison is easy) and Ettiketto (Swedish grocery volumes +4% in Q3; co-location complete). I expect adjusted EBITA within plus or minus 8% of DNB, i.e. "in line" with downside tilt, and no change to 2027 beyond trimming. The composite will move for three reasons that have nothing to do with Q4: Nordea's first post-Q3 update (its 2026 EPS of SEK 0.62 is after items and must absorb Q3's SEK 29m, so it probably falls towards SEK 0.3 to 0.4, pulling the FactSet 2026 mean from 1.30 towards 1.1; its 2027 may hold); the dividend consensus of SEK 0.56 being replaced by a proposal of SEK 0 to 0.50; and any impairment. Net: "cut" on the screens (the dashboard's sell-side momentum F will persist), "held" in substance. Probability that DNB's 2027 adjusted EPS is above SEK 2.25 after the event: 65%.
  • April 2027 (Q1 2027). The first chance of an upgrade: adjusted EBITA of SEK 60m to 70m against SEK 41m is a 50% to 70% rise; if Ettiketto's margin is 15% or more and S:t Eriks and Tornum together lose less than SEK 15m, DNB's "very slow industrial recovery" assumption will look conservative on the cyclical platforms (2.6% and -1.3% for 2027). DNB said itself that if the Ettiketto savings are realised "EBITA margins will expand rapidly" and it "would likely raise 2027 forecasts". Probability of a 2027 upgrade of 2% to 6% at this event: 45%.
  • July 2027 (Q2 2027). Hard comparison at Communication, easy at S:t Eriks (Q2 2026 under-absorption) and Ettiketto (material shortages in Q2 2026). Leverage below 2.5x would let DNB bring forward its acquired growth. Even odds of an upgrade; a bolt-on announcement at a sensible multiple tips it.
  • October 2027 and February 2028. 2028 becomes the focus. DNB's 2028 (revenue +3%, EBITA about SEK 455m to 460m) has almost no cyclical recovery and only a little acquired growth; a Riksbank cut in 2027, or evidence of grain orders (AGI's late-2026 pick-up; Jefferies' cycle bottom), or SEK 150m of bolt-ons would each force a 2028 upgrade of 3% to 8%. The VAT cliff of December 2027 and the CAP cuts from 2028 are the counter-risks.

3. Why "held" is the single most likely outcome at the first event and "upgraded" from the second. DNB's post-Q3 estimates assume a very slow recovery and have been cut five times since 2024. Our base has a 55% chance of landing within 10% of its 2027 EBITA with a downside skew; but DNB's 2027 on the cyclical platforms is now below ours (S:t Eriks 2.6% against 3.4%; Tornum -1.3% against -1.0%), which means the bear ingredients are already partly in the one active estimate. The asymmetry for the next 18 months is therefore mildly favourable: the broker's number is low enough to be beaten by self-help and comparison arithmetic alone.

4. Why breadth is noise. With two contributors, "net breadth" is -100%, 0% or +100%. Over the last six months FactSet shows EPS revisions of -18% for 2027 and -40% for 2026, EBITDA -54%, sales -48%, target price -33% and the buy share -50 points: almost all of this is the separation (estimates halved mechanically) and Nordea's entry on a lower EBITDA basis. The first set's warning stands: the composite signal masks the components. The memo should track one line, DNB's 2027 adjusted EBITA, and treat Nordea's next note as a separate event with its own date risk (its SEK 39 target is 60% above the price and will be cut or the rating lowered when it updates; that is a probable negative headline in the next three months with no information content).

5. The possibility of a third broker. Volati's market value (about SEK 1.9bn), free float (34%) and liquidity (USD 0.3m to 0.4m a day) make commissioned research (Redeye, ABG Sundal Collier sponsored) more likely than a bank initiation. A third contributor would make consensus more robust and the breadth measure meaningful; it would also, on first publication, probably sit nearer DNB than Nordea and so lower the FactSet mean. Probability of a new contributor within 18 months: about 40%.

6. The 18-months-to-three-years horizon. No 2029 consensus exists. When DNB rolls to 2029 (probably in early 2027), its number will embed 2% organic and 5% acquired growth, about SEK 490m to 510m of EBITA; our 2029 is SEK 530m with SEK 150m to 200m of acquisitions a year from 2028 and a slow cyclical recovery. If the loop flips on the second set's timetable, the published 2029 will be upgraded; if the Riksbank keeps rates near 2.4% through 2027, the cyclical component of our 2029 (about SEK 45m at S:t Eriks and Tornum above 2027) is at risk and the two effects offset. Secondary bias: upgrades, conditional on leverage; probability 50% upgraded, 30% held, 20% cut.

7. The gap, and its favourability. Our 2027 and 2028 are in line with the one active broker; our revenue is lower; our capital-structure view (preference dividend, dividend cut, acquisition restart) is partly in DNB's model and partly not. The favourable elements are a low bar at the first two 2027 events, a 2028 with no cyclical recovery in it, and a 2029 that does not yet exist. The unfavourable elements are mechanical: the composite will keep reading as cuts until Nordea updates and the dividend resets, and the dashboard's F for sell-side momentum will not turn before mid-2027. Net: a modest positive gap over 18 months, masked by a negative screen for the first six.

11d

Current multiple

–not yet ratedMemo topic 11, : the Rater's score1 answer
D-0750
D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

C503rd of 8

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. Almost entirely idiosyncratically, and the multiple is company-driven. Over the latest six-month window (4 May to 28 October 2026) the correlation of Volati's daily returns with the equal-weighted peer basket is 0.13, the beta 0.68 and the R-squared 2%: 98% of the share's daily variance is its own. The six-month return is -70.6% against +16.9% for the basket. The two-year series never shows the basket explaining more than 12% (November 2024) and shows 0% to 1% through most of 2025 and 2026. Two caveats cut in opposite directions and roughly cancel. The price series is not adjusted for the Salix distribution of 15 June 2026 (about SEK 69 a share of value left the price), which manufactures idiosyncrasy in the latest window; but the basket omits the six closest Swedish serial-acquirer peers (no price history for Lifco, Sdiptech, Storskogen, Idun, Röko, Beijer Alma), so the measured basket is mostly non-Nordic conglomerates against which a low correlation is expected. Even so, in the windows before the spin and with no distribution effect (March to August 2025) the correlation was 0.06 to 0.09 with a beta of zero, so the result is not an artefact. The consequence for predictability is double-edged. The multiple will not be rescued by a sector re-rating (the dashboard sector's twelve-month returns run from +4% to +70% for the names with ratings, and Volati's is -77.6% unadjusted, about -15% to -18% adjusted since the spin), and it will not be sunk by a sector de-rating either; what moves it is Volati's own prints, its leverage and its post-spin shareholder base. That makes the multiple's drivers identifiable (D-06) but not predictable (D-04).

Reasoning.

1. The readings. Latest window: correlation 0.13, beta 0.68, R-squared 2%; against individual peers the correlation ranges from -0.05 (Rosebank) to 0.19 (Schouw), none above 3% R-squared. Month-end windows: the basket explained 12% in November 2024, 7% to 9% around the turn of 2024, 11% in September 2025 (a brief sector episode), and 0% to 2% in every other month. The beta has been zero in 11 of 24 windows.

2. Why the artefact argument does not overturn the result. The Salix distribution produced a one-day fall of roughly 70% in the unadjusted price series on 10 June 2026; that single day dominates the six-month variance and would alone push the correlation towards zero. But the readings for the windows ending March to August 2025, which contain no distribution and no spin announcement, are 0.06 to 0.09 with beta 0.00; the stock already moved on its own then, on the Q4 2024 miss, the CFO's resignation, the Q1 and Q2 2025 results and the separation process. The pattern since 2025 is of a stock priced on its own news.

3. Why the basket argument does not overturn it either. The six excluded Swedish peers are the names whose multiples DNB says move with the swap-rate gap, and Volati's correlation with them would probably be higher than with Smiths or thyssenkrupp. But the first set's evidence is that the sector's live stories (short-cycle recovery, repaired acquirers buying again, break-ups where the remainder is the better half) bypass Volati: it is "the control case", the one where leverage stopped the engine. Lifco's own twelve-month return is -17.5% and Storskogen's +15.1%, and Volati tracked neither.

4. What drives the multiple, in order. (i) Its own earnings misses and the resulting downgrades (five consecutive EBITA misses to Q2 2026; DNB's target from SEK 132 pre-spin to SEK 25 now); (ii) its capital structure: ordinary equity about 43% of enterprise value, so a given change in the enterprise multiple is magnified about 2.3 times in the equity, and the preference shares, which peers do not have; (iii) post-spin shareholder rotation and a 34% free float with USD 0.3m to 0.4m of daily trading, which makes the price path dependent on a handful of sellers; (iv) its lack of guidance and of a capital markets day; (v) last, the sector: Swedish compounder multiples compressed about 7% for unproven names as swap rates rose (DNB), and Volati trades at 10.7x 2027e EV/EBITA against 13.2x for Tången, 17.1x for Röko and Addtech, 18.1x for Lifco and 8.4x for Storskogen. The sector sets the ceiling for a re-rating (Storskogen's 8.4x to Sdiptech's 10.8x is the realistic range for a repaired-but-unproven acquirer); Volati's own delivery sets where in that range it sits.

5. The multiple's own history. The 24-month-forward P/E is 9.68x on FactSet consensus (3rd percentile of five years; the ten-year "crash" band is 9.7x), current P/E 8.9x at the 6th percentile, with a valuation rating of A pinned by the dashboard. That history is of the pre-spin entity, so the percentiles overstate how cheap the continuing group is against its own past; on the sector's range above, the discount to proven peers is about 30% to 40% and to Sdiptech about 0% to 10%. Against the guideline fair multiple of 11.21x, the market's 9.7x implies the market is pricing the "stalling or turning up" phase, not the "bottoming" phase.

6. The consequence for predictability. A company-driven multiple means the memo can name the triggers that move it (Q4 2026 leverage, Q1 2027 margins, leverage below 2.5x, a bolt-on restart, a capital markets day, a Nordea update) rather than waiting on sector sentiment; that is a positive for the predictability of the catalysts. It also means that the sector tailwind of 2026 (peers up 15% to 70%) has given and will give no support, and that liquidity makes the path noisy around those triggers. On balance neutral for the predictability of the forecasts, mildly positive for the identifiability of what re-rates them, and negative for the smoothness of the path.

Outside the eleven ratings

Answers not placed under any of the eleven ratings

The table the ratings map uses places these nowhere; each is shown with the reason the table records.

Why it is not placed It asks whether the stock fits the sector's historical patterns of share price and financial outperformance, a cross-check across many topics rather than one of the eleven.

C-84

What do historical precedents, rules of thumb and the empirical evidence show about how companies in this sector and industry have typically generated share price, financial and operational outperformance, and underperformance, and to what extent does this company fit those patterns?

F2518th of 19

Layer 1 · Industry and Value Chain

Judgement. In Nordic and European diversified acquirers, outperformance has come from three patterns:

  1. A repaired balance sheet restarting acquisitions, which produces upgrades consensus has not priced, for 12 to 24 months.
  2. Returning organic growth, which re-expands the multiple.
  3. Subtraction where what leaves is worse than what stays.

Underperformance has come from three opposite patterns:

  1. Leverage near the ceiling in a weak cycle, which stops the engine and compresses the multiple.
  2. Acquired growth masking organic decline (the "false friend").
  3. Turnarounds that take longer than planned.

Volati fits the underperformance patterns today on all three counts. Switching to an outperformance pattern needs leverage on the company's definition below about 2.5x and two quarters of positive organic EBITA growth. On base-case evidence that is three to six quarters away, so the switch is a 2027 event, not a 2026 one.

The precedents.

  • Storskogen, the closest analogue. From 2021 to 2022 it did 119 deals at up to 10x profit, and leverage reached 4.35x in Q3 2022. Then came a Moody's downgrade to B1, the share collapsing to around SEK 10, and two to three years of repair (net debt down SEK 4.6bn to 2.3x, nine loss-making units sold). Q2 2026 was its first quarter of EBITA growth since Q4 2024, and the share is up 17.7% over three months. Yet it still trades at about 8.5x 2026e EV/EBITA, a 30% to 50% discount to proven peers. Lesson: the repair took about three years, and the re-rating only began once the deal engine restarted and organic EBITA turned.
  • Sdiptech, the fast repair. Ten divestments in nine months at above 6x EBITA took leverage from 3.31x to 2.81x and its preference shares were redeemed. Organic growth then reached 11%, the share rose 44.6% over twelve months, and sell-side momentum rated A. Lesson: selling weak units at decent prices fixes leverage fast. Volati's weak units (S:t Eriks, Tornum) cannot be sold at decent prices at a trough.
  • Volati's own 2019 to 2021 cycle. Leverage spiked; the Consumer business was divested and Bokusgruppen listed; leverage fell to 0.8x, and the share peaked in 2021. Its pattern is "cyclical leverage spikes and margin dilution followed by major structural asset divestments" (Risks report §7). The Salix separation did not repair RemainCo's leverage, because the preference capital stayed behind.
  • Lifco. Even a top-quality compounder fell 16% on an unreadable short-seller report in January 2026. Sector sentiment can override fundamentals.
  • Indus. A commodity windfall (tungsten) produced a +70% move, but one that is not repeatable.

Rules of thumb from the files.

  • "A serial acquirer's multiple is the market's guess at how long the engine runs, and organic growth is its evidence."
  • "Name the trigger before you buy the turnaround."
  • "Turnarounds take longer than you think."
  • "Worser, odder, longer, further."

All four argue against front-running Volati's recovery before the trigger shows.

Why it is not placed The table holds this question in reserve for Riskiness sub-items that are not yet built, so it is not yet placed.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

F2019th of 19

Layer 1 · Risks

Judgement. To about SEK 13 to 15 in a central trough case, about 37% to 45% below SEK 23.75. To about SEK 19 to 22 in a mild case, about 10% to 20% below. To about SEK 10 or lower in a severe case, more than 55% below. The downside is large for a share already near its post-spin low, because the ordinary equity is only about 43% of EV. A modest fall in enterprise value removes a much larger share of equity value.

Reasoning.

  • Claims ahead of the ordinary shares. Net debt of about SEK 1.5bn to 1.6bn (FactSet Q2 2026 SEK 1.475bn including leases, plus July acquisitions), preference shares of about SEK 1.0bn, and minorities and pensions of about SEK 14m. That is about SEK 2.55bn. EV at SEK 23.75 is about SEK 4.4bn to 4.5bn: about 13.5x 2026e EBITA (DNB SEK 326m) and about 11x 2027e (SEK 408m).
  • A fair trough multiple for a levered, cyclical, low-ROCE conglomerate is about 9x to 10x forward EV/EBITA. Storskogen trades at about 8.5x 2026e (its bear case is 8x). DNB's bear case is 10x, giving SEK 22. The Selector's ten-year 24-month-forward P/E "crash" band is 9.7x, but it was built on the pre-spin entity.
  • Mild. 10x on 2027e EBITA of about SEK 408m gives an EV of about SEK 4.1bn, equity of about SEK 1.5bn, and about SEK 19 a share. DNB's SEK 22 bear uses slightly different inputs.
  • Central. 2027e EBITA cut 10% to 15% (to about SEK 350m to 365m) on a failed H2 2026 recovery and Tornum losses, at 9.5x to 10x, gives an EV of about SEK 3.3bn to 3.65bn, equity of about SEK 0.8bn to 1.1bn, and about SEK 10 to 14 a share. On a P/E basis, 7x to 8x on adjusted EPS cut to about SEK 1.8 to 2.0 gives about SEK 13 to 16. Taking the two methods together: about SEK 13 to 15.
  • Severe. 9x on EBITA of about SEK 330m with leverage pressing the 3.5x maximum gives a theoretical ordinary equity below SEK 10. In practice the preference shares would also fall in value, but their fixed claim would leave ordinary holders exposed to dilution.
  • Context for later sets. On normalised EBITA of about SEK 550m to 600m at 10x, EV would be about SEK 5.5bn to 6.0bn, equity about SEK 3.0bn to 3.5bn, and about SEK 38 to 44 a share. The equity is a leveraged option on cyclical normalisation, and timing decides which tail is realised.

Why it is not placed It rates the staircase of news the market can look forward to, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or the catalysts as such.

Group judgement

Stock market narrative

F3818th of 19

Layer 1 · Stock market narrative

Written last by the Investment Analyst for its group

The narrative is "Volati handed out its best business and kept the leverage", and the near-term staircase does not change it.

The next six months bring risk events (the Q3 detail, the November Riksbank decision, the year-end impairment test) more than positive steps. The best-founded positives arrive in H1 2027: very easy comparisons, cost and integration run-rates, and a smaller dividend that speeds deleveraging. These can produce strong year-on-year EBITA growth and lift leverage off its ceiling. If leverage falls below about 2.5x, Volati could move into the sector's "repaired balance sheet restarting acquisitions" story, which the market has been rewarding (Storskogen, Sdiptech).

The genuinely novel steps (a RemainCo capital markets day, refinancing of the preference shares, a portfolio action, a construction recovery) are unsignalled or depend on the cycle. For timing, the second set should treat the first credible narrative turn as H1 2027 at the earliest, conditional on Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 showing the self-help landing.

Why it is not placed It rates how well the earlier answers agree with each other, which is a check on the analysis rather than on the company.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

–58

Layer 3 · Risks

Judgement. The two sets are highly congruent on the shape of the case and congruent in their numbers; where they differ, the second set resolved the difference with the Q3 evidence rather than contradicting the first set. The one place where they jar is the balance sheet: the first set's risk group treats leverage as the fact that dominates everything, with a trough price of SEK 13 to 15 and a one-in-three to one-in-two chance of a material event within 24 months, while the second set's model has leverage falling to 2.1x by end-2027 even in its bear case and the leverage-and-acquisition loop flipping to reinforcing by mid-2027. Both can be right only if the next two quarters go to plan: the first set describes the state today, the second set describes the state after the Q4 2026 cash release and the dividend cut. That dependence on sequence is the real finding of the congruence test. The incongruent document is the Financial Analyst's model, whose forecasts are not those of either set for the mechanical reasons given in the Preface; once corrected it reproduces the second set.

Reasoning.

Where the sets support each other (the spine of the case).

  • Both describe a cyclical trough in two platforms (S:t Eriks, Tornum) that contribute roughly nothing today but 40% to 50% of normal-year EBITA, a stable-to-improving pair (Ettiketto, Communication) and a weather-random one (Corroventa). Both say no end-market recovery before H2 2027; the second set's platform inputs (S:t Eriks 3.4% in 2027, Tornum -1.0%) obey the first set's instruction.
  • Both say pricing power is cost recovery with a lag at best and negative in the cyclical platforms; the second set's "price adds about 1% to revenue and nothing to margin" is the first set's C-17 and C-45 turned into an input.
  • Both make the 2027 recovery a self-help story (cost programmes, production realignment, integration, Tramex annualisation) and both say the balance sheet is the amplifier (EPS elasticity to EBITA of about 2x; fixed claims of 40% to 46% of EBITA).
  • Both discount the 15% target and the "2023 level in normal markets" statement as anchors without dates.
  • Both conclude that the market's 2026 EPS figures are on inconsistent bases and that DNB's adjusted EBITA is the only usable consensus line.

Where they differed and the Q3 evidence settled it.

  • Central costs: the first set counted SEK 10m to 30m of savings; the second set gave SEK 0m to 5m after head office cost SEK 47m for nine months against SEK 44m. The evidence sides with the second set.
  • The 2026 consensus cut: the first set said 5% to 10%, the second set 0% to 3% on adjusted EBITA. DNB cut 3%. The second set's number was right, the first set's direction was right.
  • Tornum 2027: the first set accepted breakeven; the second set used SEK -8m; DNB has since moved to -1.3% (about SEK -11m). The sets converged in the right direction.
  • Leverage below 2.5x: the first set said three to six quarters away; the second set says Q1 to Q2 2027, the short end of that range. Not a contradiction, a narrowing.

Where they jar, and what it means.

  1. Balance-sheet fragility now against balance-sheet repair later. The first set measures headroom against the 3.5x maximum from the LTM at Q2 (a 12% to 17% EBITDA fall breaches). On Q3 figures the breach needs only an 11% fall in adjusted EBITDA with net debt flat. The second set's "about 25% below" is measured from its own end-2026 forecast, after a Q4 cash release of about SEK 110m after the preference dividend and EBITDA growth. Both are true; the jar is in the time stamp. The memo must say that the headroom is thin until the FY2026 report and wide only if that report shows net debt near SEK 1.2bn and leverage near 2.7x.
  2. Management's acquisition appetite against the model's restraint. The second set models SEK 50m of acquisitions in 2027. Management bought Tramex and NicEtikett at 2.9x to 3.1x leverage, said all five platforms would do bolt-ons within twelve months (29 April 2026), and told the Q3 call it sees attractive opportunities. The first set's C-19 and C-64 said management prioritises acquisitions over the balance sheet. The second set's deleveraging path is therefore faster than management's revealed preference, and the risk is a slower path with more acquired EBITA, which is roughly neutral for 2028 EBITA and negative for the multiple's trigger (the "false friend" pattern).
  3. The second set's own internal tension. It calls 2027 "the most predictable year in the forecast" and its drivers diffuse, yet gives a 30% bear weight, a bear case 17% below base on EBITA and 32% below on EPS, and a downward skew. The reconciliation is that the sources of improvement are diffuse but the sources of error are concentrated (D-03), so predictability of the direction is high and predictability of the level is moderate. The language should not be read as "high predictability".
  4. The first set's narrative group and the second set's loop. The first set says the first credible narrative turn is H1 2027 at the earliest; the second set's loop flips in H2 2027. Both are conditional on Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 landing. Congruent, but the memo's "lifecycle" judgement (topic #10) must be stated as conditional on two prints that have not happened.

The one incongruent document. The Financial Analyst's model shows FY2026 revenue 44% above the second set's, EBITDA 47% above, EPS three times and free cash flow nine times. None of that is a difference of view. It is the quarterly revenue growth applied to pre-spin quarters, a pre-spin opening working capital, and the absence of the preference dividend and minorities (Preface, section 3). The congruence of the two sets' judgements is therefore not reflected in the forecasts of record until the model is re-run with the changed input and a continuing-operations base.

Why congruence matters for predictability here. Two analysts working from the same evidence reached the same shape with independently built numbers (the second set's 2027 EBITA of SEK 398m is within 3% of DNB and within the first set's central range of SEK 370m to 400m). That makes the central case well founded. But congruence on a conditional case does not make the condition more likely; it concentrates the test on two dates (February and April 2027).

Why it is not placed It rates how well the sell-side and investors already articulate the case, and none of the eleven rates the narrative or how much of the case is known.

I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

A561st of 8

Layer 4 · Stock market narrative

Judgement. The narrative is one house's narrative. DNB Carnegie (analyst Carl Korsheden) is the only active sell-side voice; Nordea's initiation of 2 October 2026 is the only other document and it predates Q3; the AlphaSense search found no investor commentary (no Twitter, no forum, no letter) on Volati at all. That is itself the finding: a SEK 1.9bn company with a 34% free float and USD 0.3m a day of trading has no investor narrative, only a broker's, and the broker's is "Hold, waiting for end markets to improve". Within that narrative the negatives are comprehensively articulated and the positives are not. Well known: that Salix was the better business and the remainder is more volatile; that leverage is 3.1x on the company's definition and 4.5x to 4.8x with the preference shares and leases, and that this blocks acquisitions; that S:t Eriks and Tornum are in cyclical troughs; that Ettiketto's margin is diluted by Interket and Clever and that the German co-location should help from Q1 2027; that the FactSet 2026 EPS composite is distorted (DNB says so itself); that there is no guidance and no capital markets day. Known to DNB but not framed as the market frames them: the dividend cut (DNB's SEK 0.56 implies it; nobody calls it a deleveraging positive); the preference-share redemption (DNB calls it "highly unlikely near term" and models none; the SEK 625 step-down, the Sdiptech precedent and the SEK 0.36 of EPS it would add are not discussed as optionality); the acquisition restart (DNB models 5% acquired growth from 2027 while describing leverage as blocking deals, so the restart is in the model but not in the story). Discussed by no one in the Volati context: the price-cost squeeze at S:t Eriks from Heidelberg's clinker consolidation at Slite and its SEK 5.5bn purchase of NCC's 106 aggregate sites; the grain-storage price war with Ag Growth International and Kepler Weber as a structural rather than cyclical fact; Karl Perlhagen's departure from the board (a press release and a Bernstein ESG monitor, no analysis of what the 39% holder does next); the arithmetic that H1 2027 year-on-year growth is almost assured by the comparison base; that FY2026 reported ordinary EPS will be about zero; the VAT cliff of December 2027 for Ettiketto's Swedish volumes; the working-capital reversal in 2027. For 18 months to three years out, nothing is articulated by anyone beyond DNB's long-run assumption of 2% organic and 5% acquired growth and its 2032 leverage of 2.6x. The gap between our assessment and the narrative is therefore favourable in a specific way: the stock is priced and described as a leveraged, cyclical Hold with no catalysts, and the things that would change that description (two low-bar prints, a dividend reset, leverage below 2.5x, a bolt-on, optionality on the preference shares) are either unarticulated or articulated only as constraints. The unfavourable side is that the unarticulated negatives (Heidelberg, the grain price war, the reported EPS of zero) will surface in the same window, and that with no investor narrative there is no buyer base to respond to good news quickly.

Reasoning.

1. Coverage and its limits. The T-I2 report identifies DNB Carnegie as the only covering house AlphaSense holds research for, cites it for 24 of its sources, and cites Nordea's rating only through FactSet. Across the eight AlphaSense reports, every analytical statement about Volati is DNB's or the company's. DNB's own notes are the narrative: "Still waiting for end markets to improve" (20 October), "In line if adjusting for large one-offs" (26 October), "Appears fairly priced given leverage profile" (25 June), "Not the print we were looking for" (20 July), "BUY on separation anxiety" (4 May). The arc is from a Buy on the separation's value crystallisation to a Hold on the remainder's leverage, which is the first set's "handed out its best business" framing adopted by the one broker.

2. Dynamic by dynamic: who says what, how prominently, since when.

3. What the sector's investors are and are not focused on, and where Volati sits. The sector narrative in October 2026 has three live stories (the first set's C-84): repaired balance sheets restarting acquisitions (Storskogen, Sdiptech, AddLife), break-ups where the remainder re-rated (Smiths, TKH, thyssenkrupp) and compounder multiples tracking the swap-rate gap. Volati is cited in the sector notes as the counter-example to the first two (the engine stopped; the remainder is the worse half) and as a victim of the third. Peer valuations in DNB's table (Storskogen 8.4x 2027e EV/EBITA, Sdiptech 10.8x, Volati 10.7x, Tången 13.2x, Röko and Addtech 17.1x, Lifco 18.1x) place Volati with the repaired-but-unproven names, not with the broken ones: the market already gives it some credit for the Ettiketto half. Investors who own the sector therefore know Volati as "the one that did it backwards" and are not looking at it; those who own Volati (founders 63% of votes; a thinned institutional base after the spin) have no public voice.

4. The dynamics the memo should expect to become articulated within 18 months, and when.

  • February 2027: FY2026 ordinary EPS near zero and a dividend near zero (negative, mechanical); leverage at about 2.7x (positive, if it happens); a possible Tornum impairment (negative, non-cash).
  • April to July 2027: the easy-comparison growth (positive; DNB will describe it as "easy comps", which it already does for H2 2026); the co-location saving; leverage below 2.5x; a first bolt-on. If these land, DNB's language would move from "waiting" to "recovery under way" and a rating change to Buy is plausible (it was a Buy until June 2026).
  • H2 2027: the acquisition restart as a story (currently only a model line); the Heidelberg price-cost effect, if S:t Eriks' margin fails to lift with volume; the VAT cliff ahead of December 2027.
  • 2028 and beyond: the preference-share redemption becomes discussable once leverage is below 2x; nobody will articulate it before then, which makes it the least-known positive with the longest fuse.

5. Why the thin narrative is favourable on balance. A one-broker Hold narrative with no investor commentary means the price carries almost no expectation of the positive sequence our base case describes; the dashboard's ratings are F for technical momentum, F for sell-side momentum and A for valuation, which is the profile of a stock nobody is positioned in. If the February and April 2027 prints land, the narrative has only one voice to change, and that voice has a documented willingness to move (Buy to Not Rated to Hold within 2026). The unfavourable side is twofold: there is no buyer base, so the re-rating would be slow and noisy; and the negatives that nobody has articulated (Heidelberg, the grain price war, the reported EPS) are as likely to surface in the same window and would find an equally thin defence.

6. Secondary, 18 months to three years. Nothing is articulated. DNB's long-run model (acquired growth 5%, leverage 2.6x by 2032) is the only forward view and it is a mechanical extrapolation. Our 2029 to 2030 (EBITA SEK 530m to 600m; a partial preference redemption as the clearest unmodelled upside) has no counterpart in any published narrative. That is favourable if delivered and irrelevant until the 18-month tests are passed.

Each layer's own material

What each layer was asked, what its scores mean, and the Investment Analyst's notes and appendices

Layer 1: Assessing the foundational components of an investment case

Written 25 October 2026, 20:28 to 20:52 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to investigate and research each question, analyse the relevant information and form an insightful judgement regarding it, and to do the same for each group of questions as a whole, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating model and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Volati AB (VOLO-SE): First set of questions, foundational components of the investment case

As at 2026-10-25. Author: Investment Analyst, first set. Reader: Investment Analyst, second set (operating and financial model, 18 months to four years).

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Preface (not an answer): the baseline, the source checks and the traps to avoid

Source check on the latest Earnings Review. The file was flagged because its results date could not be read. It covers the period ending 30 June 2026: it states that Volati published its interim report for Q2 2026 (April to June) and H1 2026 on 17 July 2026, on continuing operations after the Salix Group listing of 15 June 2026. It is safe to rely on.

The Q3 2026 report is the most important missing input. Volati published its Q3 2026 report on 23 October 2026, after almost all of the files were written. Only fragments reached the files, all inside one AI-compiled report (the Q1 2026 Earnings Review, file 06), which cites the Q3 interim report and presentation. Treat them as provisional until checked: (a) net debt to adjusted EBITDA of 3.1x "following recent acquisitions", above the 2.0x to 3.0x target range, with management expecting seasonally stronger cash flows to bring it down; (b) Tornum sales down 20% (one passage says 17%) as customers deferred grain-handling projects; (c) rising energy and shipping costs "prevented any meaningful margin expansion at S:t Eriks and Tornum despite active cost-containment measures"; (d) a total consideration of SEK 494m for Interket. The share fell 4.0% on the results day (SEK 24.75 to SEK 23.75). DNB Carnegie had already trimmed its target to SEK 26 from SEK 27 on 19 October, ahead of the report. The second set's first job is to get the Q3 segment figures. Several judgements below (the H2 2026 recovery, the deleveraging path) depend on them.

What the company now is. "Volati" means the continuing operations (RemainCo) left after the Lex Asea distribution of 100% of Salix Group. That is five platforms: Ettiketto Group (self-adhesive labels and labelling machines), Communication (Scanmast masts and towers, MAFI mounting hardware), Corroventa (water-damage drying, rental and sales, now including Tramex moisture meters), S:t Eriks Group (precast concrete and natural stone for infrastructure and landscaping) and Tornum Group (grain drying, handling and storage).

Platforms. FY2025 net sales and EBITA margin: Ettiketto SEK 1,203m at 17.9%; Communication SEK 667m at 10.1%; Corroventa SEK 268m at 15.2%; S:t Eriks SEK 1,257m at about 4.2%; Tornum SEK 924m at a low single-digit margin.

Q2 2026 net sales and EBITA (margin): Ettiketto SEK 436m and SEK 65m (15%); Communication SEK 202m and SEK 28m (14%); Corroventa SEK 49m and SEK 2m (3%); S:t Eriks SEK 392m and SEK 20m (5%); Tornum SEK 259m and SEK 6m (2%). The five platforms total SEK 121m of EBITA in Q2 against a group figure of SEK 91m, so central costs and eliminations were about SEK 30m in the quarter. That figure includes Salix separation costs, which have not been quantified.

Q1 2026: Ettiketto SEK 401m and SEK 65m; S:t Eriks EBITA SEK -16m; Tornum sales SEK 184m (down 37%) and EBITA SEK -15m.

Estimates now visible (FactSet, 22 October 2026). Two brokers, not one.

  • DNB Carnegie: Hold, target SEK 26 (19 October). Adjusted EPS SEK 1.98 (2026), 2.30 (2027), 2.82 (2028). EBITDA SEK 560m, 668m, 725m. Its July model had adjusted EBITA of SEK 326m (2026e) and SEK 408m (2027e). Its 2026e segment margins: Ettiketto 15.2%, Communication 11.7% (EBITA SEK 83m), Corroventa 15.5%, S:t Eriks 1.8%, Tornum -3.2%. Further out: Tornum EBITA SEK 5m in 2027e, S:t Eriks margin 3.9% in 2028e, Ettiketto 16.3% in 2028e.
  • Nordea: Buy, target SEK 39 (2 October). EPS SEK 0.62, 2.02, 2.70. EBITDA SEK 388m, 525m, 587m.
  • FactSet consensus: revenue SEK 4,804m, 5,141m, 5,328m; EBIT SEK 221m, 329m, 384m; EPS SEK 1.30, 2.16, 2.76; dividend per share SEK 0.56, 0.675, 0.53; free cash flow SEK 55m, 171m, 213m.

Traps the second set must avoid.

  1. EPS basis. Volati's reported EPS per ordinary share deducts the preference dividend: SEK 64m a year, about SEK 0.81 per ordinary share. Q1 2026 is the check: continuing profit of SEK 5m less about SEK 16m of quarterly preference dividend gives about SEK -0.14, against a reported SEK -0.16. DNB's adjusted EPS adds back amortisation of acquired intangibles. Nordea's 2026 figure of SEK 0.62 fits a reported basis (after amortisation and one-offs). So the FactSet "consensus" of SEK 1.30 for 2026 averages two different definitions and means nothing on its own. Build EPS from EBITA down.
  2. Leverage basis. The company-defined net debt to adjusted EBITDA was 2.9x at Q2 2026 and reportedly 3.1x at Q3. DNB's 4.1x to 4.8x includes 100% of the preference shares and lease liabilities. FactSet's enterprise value implies the preference shares are worth about SEK 1.0bn: its EV of SEK 4,484m less market value, debt and cash leaves about SEK 994m. The figure of 7.4x pro-forma leverage, used in the August memo and repeated in one sector note, comes from an uncited source and matches no broker calculation. Do not use it.
  3. The "SEK 1.1bn to 1.5bn EBITA by 2027" figure was stated on the Q3 2025 call (24 October 2025) for the whole group, Salix included. It is not a RemainCo target, and several reports wrongly measure RemainCo's LTM EBITA of SEK 262m against it. The relevant post-spin statement is from the Q1 2026 call: in normal markets the remaining platforms should earn at least their 2023 level, which was SEK 472m of EBITA on the 2023 perimeter.
  4. Price history is not adjusted for the Salix distribution. These readings are therefore largely artefacts: the 52-week high of SEK 116, the 200-day average of SEK 56, the 12-month return of -77.6%, the relative-strength percentile of 0 and the P/E percentile of 6. At listing, Salix was worth about SEK 69 per Volati share (SEK 5.5bn of market value over 79.4m shares). RemainCo first traded at about SEK 27 to 29. The clean readings are -5.2% over three months and about -15% to -18% since the spin.
  5. FactSet's history table is consolidated and pre-spin (revenue SEK 8,419m in FY2025, gross margin of 5% to 9% on FactSet's classification). It cannot be compared with RemainCo consensus.
  6. Q2 2026 organic growth was +2%. One digest says -2%. The +2% matches total growth of 11% less about 9 points from acquisitions.

Rules of thumb used throughout.

  • SEK 10m of EBITA is worth about SEK 0.10 of EPS (after 21% to 23% tax, over 79.4m ordinary shares).
  • Claims ranking ahead of the ordinary equity are net debt of about SEK 1.4bn to 1.5bn plus preference shares of about SEK 1.0bn. Together that is about 57% of an enterprise value of about SEK 4.4bn at SEK 23.75, so ordinary equity is only about 43% of EV.
  • One turn of EV/EBITA on 2027 EBITA of about SEK 400m is worth about SEK 5 a share, roughly a fifth of the share price.

Research requests for the next set

Company: Volati AB (VOLO-SE), a Swedish decentralised industrial acquirer that, after distributing Salix Group to its shareholders on 15 June 2026, owns five platforms: Ettiketto Group (self-adhesive labels and labelling machines, Nordics, Germany, the UK and the Netherlands), Communication (telecom masts, towers and mounting hardware through Scanmast and MAFI), Corroventa (water-damage drying and moisture-measurement equipment, rental and sales, including Tramex), S:t Eriks Group (precast concrete and natural stone for Swedish infrastructure and landscaping) and Tornum Group (grain drying, handling and storage systems, including SIMEZA silos)

Request: T-P1

Peers: Storskogen Group (STOR.B-SE; decentralised Nordic acquirer with construction-exposed Services units, a 2023 to 2026 cost and divestment programme and published margin drop-through); Sdiptech (SDIP.B-SE; decentralised acquirer whose 2025 to 2026 divestments and restructuring show what cost and portfolio action delivered); Indus Holding (INH-DE; German buy-and-hold holding with three segments and documented fixed-cost under-absorption in German downturns); Lifco (LIFCO.B-SE; benchmark for how decentralised subsidiaries cut costs in downturns, including Demolition & Tools in 2009); Indutrade (INDT-SE) and Addtech (ADDT.B-SE; references for margin behaviour of decentralised groups through 2009, 2020 and 2023 to 2025); Benders and Starka (private Swedish concrete-products competitors of S:t Eriks); Heidelberg Materials (HEI-DE; cement supplier and precast producer in the Nordics); Ag Growth International (AFN-CA), AGCO (AGCO-US, owner of Cimbria), Kepler Weber (KEPL3-BR) and PETKUS (private) (grain-handling and storage competitors of Tornum); CCL Industries (CCL.B-CA), Multi-Color Corporation (private) and All4Labels (private) (label converters, references for converting cost structures and integration of acquired converters); Dantherm (private) and Trotec (private) (drying-equipment competitors of Corroventa); Vestum (VESTUM-SE; Swedish construction-exposed acquirer reporting pricing and margin pressure) Inputs: What is the split of fixed and variable costs at S:t Eriks, Tornum, Corroventa, Communication and Ettiketto, and what share of each cost base is plant overhead, personnel and materials? How large was the under-absorption effect of S:t Eriks running its plants below demand in H1 2026 to release inventory (S:t Eriks EBITA SEK 20m in Q2 2026 against SEK 47m a year earlier, Volati Q2 2026 interim report, 17 July 2026), and what margin did comparable precast producers regain once production was realigned with orders? What were Tornum's decremental margins in 2026 (Q1 2026 sales SEK 184m, down 37%, EBITA SEK -15m against SEK 12m; Q2 2026 sales SEK 259m, down 8%, EBITA SEK 6m against SEK 19m, Volati interim reports), and how did the margins of Ag Growth International, AGCO's grain and protein business and Kepler Weber move through the 2015 to 2017 and 2019 to 2020 agricultural downturns and how long they took to recover? What did the cost programmes launched at S:t Eriks and Tornum in Q1 2026 cover (headcount, sites, savings), what run-rate and timing did management give (six to nine months, Q1 2026 call, 29 April 2026), and what did comparable programmes at Storskogen (about 1,000 full-time equivalents removed in Services), Indus and Vestum deliver against what was promised? How large were the Salix separation costs and other one-off central costs inside Volati's 2026 continuing EBITA (the CEO said group EBITDA grew 4% in Q1 2026 when adjusted for separation costs), and how large is the post-separation central cost base against Lifco's, Indutrade's and Storskogen's head-office costs? What margin uplift did Ettiketto achieve on earlier acquired converters, through which cost lines (procurement, press consolidation, capacity utilisation), and over how long, against its 24-month programme for Interket (acquired January 2026, SEK 450m of annual sales) and the two-year programme for Clever Etiketten (acquired 2025, SEK 290m of annual sales)? What incremental margin does Communication earn on project volume (Q2 2026 sales up 25% to SEK 202m and EBITA up 56% to SEK 28m)? Which cost lines proved cuttable at Volati in 2023 to 2025 (restructuring costs SEK 20m in 2025, annual report) and which did not, and what have decentralised peers saved by pooling purchasing without centralising operations (Storskogen's joint electricity and logistics agreements)? Focus: How much EBITA S:t Eriks, Tornum and Corroventa regain on a given volume recovery once the 2026 cost programmes and production realignment are complete, measured against peer precedents in concrete and grain handling. And how much of the margin uplift planned for Interket and Clever, and of any resizing of the post-separation central costs, is achievable and by when.

Request: T-P3

Why requested: Volati's leverage, its capacity to acquire, its cyclical earnings and its valuation multiple are linked, and the forecast for 18 months to four years needs to establish which way those links are running. Peers: Storskogen Group (STOR.B-SE; reduced leverage from a 2022 peak through divestments and cash flow, then restarted acquisitions in 2025 to 2026); Sdiptech (SDIP.B-SE; ten-plus divestments, preference-share redemption and an acquisition restart in 2025 to 2026); AddLife (ALIF.B-SE; cut leverage before resuming deals); Indus Holding (INH-DE; working-capital and leverage strain followed by a planned acquisition restart); Lifco (LIFCO.B-SE), Indutrade (INDT-SE) and Addtech (ADDT.B-SE) (acquirers whose self-funded acquisition loops have run at low leverage); CCL Industries (CCL.B-CA) and All4Labels (private) (label consolidators, for the scale-and-consolidation loop); Ag Growth International (AFN-CA) and Kepler Weber (KEPL3-BR) (grain equipment, for the overcapacity and price-war loop); Salix Group (Volati's former subsidiary, listed June 2026, for comparison of the separated businesses) Inputs: The leverage and acquisition loop: Volati targets net debt to adjusted EBITDA of 2.0x to 3.0x, not exceeding 3.5x, reported 2.9x at 30 June 2026 (Q2 2026 interim report), and retained all its preference shares, which carry an annual dividend of SEK 40 per share. What leverage did it report at 30 September 2026 (Q3 2026 interim report, 23 October 2026)? How does the loop run from leverage to acquisition capacity, acquired EBITDA and back to leverage, and how long did Storskogen, Sdiptech and AddLife take from their leverage peaks to restart acquisitions, at what leverage levels, and what came first (EBITDA recovery, divestments or equity)? The under-absorption loop at S:t Eriks: lower production releases inventory and cash but depresses margins (S:t Eriks Q2 2026 EBITA margin 5% against 12%). Does realigning production reverse this, and how have precast producers' margins and working capital moved after similar episodes? The grain-storage overcapacity loop: does discounting force weaker producers out, how long did capacity take to leave the market in past cycles, and who exits first (Kepler Weber's commentary on capacity added in 2020 to 2023)? The label-consolidation loop: do digital presses, the EU Packaging and Packaging Waste Regulation and recycled-content requirements push small converters to sell, and does each acquisition lift procurement scale and margins in a way that funds the next, as in Ettiketto's history? The valuation loop: how does a lower share price, post-spin shareholder rotation, low liquidity and a rising swap rate (DNB Carnegie, October 2026, on Swedish compounder multiples and swap rates) feed back into the cost and availability of equity and the market's willingness to fund acquisitions? The balancing loop of keeping cyclical units: does retaining low-return cyclical platforms cap group returns, and have Volati's own separations (the Bokusgruppen listing and Besikta sale in 2021, Salix in 2026) acted as a release valve that restored balance-sheet capacity? Focus: How long it typically takes a leveraged Nordic acquirer with cyclical platforms to restart acquisitions after its leverage peaks, what has to happen first, and whether Volati's path more closely resembles Storskogen's slower repair or Sdiptech's faster one. Also, whether the label-consolidation loop at Ettiketto can run while the group balance sheet is constrained.

Request: T-P4

Why requested: The bridge from Volati's EBITA to ordinary earnings per share passes through several large items that move separately (amortisation of acquired intangibles, interest, preference dividends, tax, minorities and one-off costs), and the two covering brokers publish EPS forecasts that appear to be on different bases. Peers: Storskogen Group (STOR.B-SE; brokers' differing treatment of acquired-intangible amortisation, minority put options and items affecting comparability in adjusted EPS); Sdiptech (SDIP.B-SE; preference-share redemption, bond refinancing and earn-out accounting); Indus Holding (INH-DE; tax rate, purchase-price amortisation and financing structure of a buy-and-hold holding); Lifco (LIFCO.B-SE), Indutrade (INDT-SE) and Addtech (ADDT.B-SE) (benchmarks for amortisation, tax, interest cost and minority treatment in decentralised acquirers) Inputs: The preference shares: the number outstanding, the SEK 40 annual dividend per share, the redemption terms and amount, their market value, and whether Volati can or intends to redeem or refinance them, with Sdiptech's redemption of 1,750,000 preference shares for SEK 184m in March 2026 as precedent. Net financial items for continuing operations after the separation: the size, margin and maturity of the new credit facility with Nordea and SEB (June 2026), lease liabilities and lease interest, how much interest was allocated to discontinued operations in the restated figures, and the sensitivity to STIBOR (the 2025 annual report disclosed SEK 17m of post-tax earnings per 100 basis points on pre-separation net debt). The run-rate of amortisation of acquired intangibles after Interket, NicEtikett and Tramex, and the purchase-price allocations of each. The effective tax rate for the continuing group and its non-deductible items. Minority interests from platform co-ownership, put options and earn-outs. The amounts of Salix separation costs, restructuring costs (S:t Eriks and Tornum, Q2 2026) and other items affecting comparability within 2026 continuing results. Goodwill by platform and its impairment sensitivity (post-tax discount rates of 10.1% to 12.8%, annual report 2025). The definitions behind DNB Carnegie's EPS (SEK 1.98, 2.30 and 2.82 for 2026 to 2028, 19 October 2026) and Nordea's (SEK 0.62, 2.02 and 2.70, 2 October 2026): which items each adds back, whether each deducts the preference dividend and minorities, and how FactSet compiles its consensus from them. Normal levels of working capital, capex and lease payments for the continuing group. Focus: A line-by-line reconciliation from Volati's adjusted EBITA to reported and adjusted ordinary EPS and to free cash flow after preference dividends for 2026 to 2028, with each broker's assumptions set against company disclosures. That shows why DNB Carnegie's and Nordea's 2026 EPS forecasts are so far apart and which lines carry the forecast risk.

Layer 2: Translating the foundational components into an operating and financial model for the next 18 months, and 18 months to 3 years out

Written 26 October 2026, 08:59 to 09:19 UK, Opus, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a well-reasoned, coherent, deeply understood and accurate operating and financial model for the company for the next 18 months to four years, based on the first set's answers, a base of the company's historical financial and operating performance, and how businesses in the sector and industry outperform or underperform, so as to best help the Consumer achieve their objective, which is to produce a trustworthy judgement regarding the predictability of delivery of the financial forecasts over the next 18 months.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each group judgement with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For this set of questions the score says how positive the likely effect on the company's financial forecasts is for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Volati AB (VOLO-SE): Second set of questions, the operating and financial model

As at 2026-10-26. Author: Investment Analyst, second set. Reader: Investment Analyst, third and fourth sets (predictability of the forecasts; guidance, consensus and narrative).

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Preface (not an answer): what changed since the first set, the basis used, and the base case in one table

1. The Q3 2026 segment figures are now visible. The first set's most important missing input has arrived, through the AlphaSense reports of 26 October 2026, which cite Volati's Q3 interim report and presentation of 23 October 2026. Segment figures for Q3 2026 (Q3 2025 in brackets):

Platform Net sales EBITA EBITA margin Note
Ettiketto 448 (297), +51% 64 (about 52), +22% 14% (18%) Interket is 26% of sales; legacy business about +8% to +9% organic
Communication 161 (150), +7% 18 (18) 11% (11%) Telecom cautious; masts for transport and energy
Corroventa 95 (66), +44% 17 (about 10), +68% 18% (16%) Tramex is about 32% of sales; dry weather
S:t Eriks 291 (320), -9% about 15 (about 16) 5% (5%) Production raised again; cost programmes held the margin
Tornum 184 (222), -17% -9 (-4) -5% (-2%) Lantmännen deliveries not replaced
Head office -16 (-15) Nine months SEK -47m against SEK -44m

My derived group totals (not yet checked against the report itself; the third set should verify them): net sales about SEK 1,175m (+11% reported, about -3% organic, about +15% acquired); adjusted EBITA about SEK 89m, which matches the company's statement that underlying EBITA grew 15%; items affecting comparability SEK -29m (SEK 8m transaction costs, SEK 19m restructuring for Ettiketto's co-location of Interket and NicEtikett, about SEK 2m other), so reported EBITA about SEK 60m. Leverage on the company's definition was 3.1x. Net debt was SEK 1,331m (against SEK 2,081m for the pre-spin group at end-2025). Rolling twelve-month cash conversion was 93%.

2. The company's leverage definition, reverse-engineered. SEK 1,331m divided by 3.1x implies about SEK 430m of EBITDA. That is only consistent with net debt that excludes lease liabilities and an adjusted, pro-forma EBITDA after lease costs (that is, before the IFRS 16 adjustment). IFRS 16 EBITDA over the last twelve months is about SEK 520m on an adjusted, pro-forma basis. I use the company's definition for the covenant and policy tests, and the IFRS 16 EBITDA for the model.

3. The basis used throughout. "Adjusted EBITA" is EBITA before items affecting comparability (separation, transaction and integration restructuring costs) and before amortisation of acquired intangibles. EBITDA is on the IFRS 16 basis, before items affecting comparability. "Adjusted EPS" is (adjusted EBITA less net financial items) after tax, less minorities, less the SEK 64m preference dividend, over 79.4m ordinary shares. "Reported EPS" also deducts amortisation of acquired intangibles and items affecting comparability. Continuing operations only. SEK m unless stated.

4. The base case in one table.

Line FY2023 FY2025 FY2026e FY2027e FY2028e FY2029e FY2030e

Against the two brokers (FactSet, 22 October 2026): DNB Carnegie adjusted EBITA SEK 321m, 409m and 454m for 2026 to 2028, adjusted EPS SEK 1.98, 2.30 and 2.82; Nordea EPS SEK 0.62, 2.02 and 2.70. My base case is in line with DNB on adjusted EBITA in 2026 and 2028 and 2.5% below it in 2027. It is 2% to 4% below consensus revenue in every year. It is close to the two brokers' 2027 EPS on a like-for-like basis (SEK 2.24 against SEK 2.02 and 2.30). It is materially different from the published 2026 EPS of both brokers, because neither 2026 figure is on a consistent basis (P-08 and Part 4 of the forecast inputs).

5. Where I disagree with the first set.

  • The 2026 cut. The first set expected a 5% to 10% cut to 2026 consensus because the H2 "hockey stick" lacked support. With Q3 visible, adjusted EBITA for 2026 lands at about SEK 318m, 1% below DNB's SEK 321m. The cut risk is real but sits elsewhere: in revenue (about 2% below consensus), in reported EBITA (about SEK 47m of items affecting comparability, so reported EBITA is about SEK 270m) and in the EPS definitions. On the adjusted EBITA line that DNB models, the cut is likely 0% to 3%, not 5% to 10%.
  • Central costs. The first set counted SEK 10m to 30m of central-cost rightsizing in its self-help. The evidence runs the other way: head office costs were SEK 47m for nine months against SEK 44m a year earlier, and SEK 16m in Q3 against SEK 15m. I give it SEK 0m to 5m, and only from 2027.
  • Ettiketto's price lag. The first set treated Ettiketto's H2 2026 price increases as a further lagged margin recovery to come. The Q3 arithmetic says the lag is largely over: the legacy business (excluding Interket and NicEtikett) made about 17.5% in Q3 against about 17.7% a year earlier. Further margin gains at Ettiketto must come from integrating Interket and Clever, not from price.
  • Tornum in 2027. The first set modelled Tornum near breakeven in 2027 (DNB: SEK +5m). Q3 (sales -17%, EBITA SEK -9m) and Ag Growth International's stated policy of pricing commercial business aggressively to absorb plant costs (Q2 2026 call, 30 July 2026) point lower. I use SEK -8m.
  • DNB's 2026 EPS. The first set read DNB's SEK 1.98 as an adjusted figure that adds back amortisation. It is that, but it is also internally inconsistent. DNB's own bridge (as reproduced by AlphaSense from the 20 October 2026 note) gives continuing net profit of SEK 87m; deducting the SEK 64m preference dividend and adding back SEK 79m of post-tax amortisation and SEK 15m of post-tax items gives about SEK 1.47, not SEK 1.98. DNB's 2027 and 2028 figures do reconcile on that basis (SEK 2.31 and SEK 2.82). The SEK 1.98 is therefore about SEK 0.50 too high against DNB's own method, probably because it carries a pre-spin element. On a consistent basis DNB's 2026 is about SEK 1.47 and mine is SEK 1.49.

The Investment Analyst's group headings, as written Group: Financial forecasts

Part 4. The reasoning behind the inputs

Segments: revenue and margin by segment, where the company reports them

Volati reports five platforms plus head office (continuing operations, from 21 April 2026). SEK m; EBITA margin before items affecting comparability. FY2025 from the segment-reporting presentation of 21 April 2026; 2026e includes reported Q1 to Q3 2026 (Q3 from the AlphaSense reports citing the Q3 report of 23 October 2026) plus my Q4 estimate. Platform figures for 2027 onwards exclude bolt-ons not yet made, which are shown on their own line.

Group sales include eliminations of about SEK -10m a year. FY2025 reported EBITA was SEK 318m (7.4%) after SEK 14m of items affecting comparability. 2026e reported EBITA is about SEK 271m after about SEK 47m of items.

The operating drivers behind the inputs

  • Revenue. Organic growth about -2% (2026), +2% (2027), +2.6% (2028) and +3.5% (2029 and 2030); acquired growth about +11.5% in 2026 (Interket from January, NicEtikett and Tramex from July), about +2% in 2027 (carry-over), then about +2.5% to +3.5% a year from new bolt-ons. Price adds about 1% a year, as cost recovery only.
  • EBITDA margin. The 2027 step (+1.4 points) is self-help and mechanical: S:t Eriks no longer under-absorbing (Q2 2026 EBITA SEK 20m against SEK 47m), full-year cost programmes at S:t Eriks and Tornum, Ettiketto's co-location savings from Q1 2027 and Interket and Clever margins rising from about 6% to 7% towards 9% to 10%, Tramex for a full year. 2028 to 2030 adds cyclical recovery at S:t Eriks and Tornum, Interket and Clever at 11% to 13%, and bolt-ons at about 12%. No head-office savings.
  • Depreciation and amortisation excludes amortisation of acquired intangibles (SEK 100m, 108m, 112m, 117m and 121m), which the model should not include if it builds adjusted EPS. It includes right-of-use depreciation (IFRS 16). About 4.9% to 5.0% of sales.
  • Net financial items: interest at about 4.5% to 4.6% on net debt excluding leases (SEK 1,331m at Q3 2026), about SEK 12m of lease interest and about SEK 5m of other items. H1 2026 was flattered by interest allocated to discontinued operations.
  • Tax: 22.0% for 2026, 22.5% thereafter (more profit in Germany, the UK, the Netherlands and Ireland).
  • Dividend: SEK 0.30 for 2026 (policy 10% to 30% of net profit; the SEK 2.00 for 2025 was paid on the pre-spin group in 2026), rising with reported net profit.
  • Working capital about 19.5% of sales at each year end (continuing Q2 2026 about 20.5% of last-twelve-months sales after S:t Eriks' inventory release; Q4 is seasonally low).
  • Lease payments are the repayment of lease liabilities (principal) only; lease interest is in net financial items.
  • Acquisitions: 2026 is Interket (total consideration SEK 494m), Tramex and NicEtikett, plus earn-outs; from 2027, bolt-ons resume as the company's leverage falls below 2.5x (about Q2 2027).
  • Share count: 79.4m ordinary shares throughout; warrants (1.34% if all exercised) are immaterial. The model must also deduct the SEK 64m preference dividend (1,603,774 shares at SEK 40) and minorities of SEK 10m, 10m, 11m, 12m and 13m below net income to reach EPS for ordinary shareholders. If it cannot, its EPS will be about SEK 0.93 to 0.97 higher than my cross-check every year, and its free cash flow about SEK 64m higher; that gap is the preference dividend, not a disagreement.
  • Leverage (company definition: net debt excluding leases over adjusted EBITDA after lease costs): about 2.7x (end-2026), 2.1x, 1.8x, 1.6x and 1.4x. Including the preference shares at SEK 1.0bn and leases: about 4.5x, 3.7x, 3.3x, 3.0x and 2.7x.

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

Volati gives no quarterly or annual guidance and has held no capital markets day for the continuing group. Its standing targets and statements, against my inputs:

  • EBITA per ordinary share growth of at least 15% a year over a cycle. My adjusted EBITA per share: -4% (2026, against the 2025 adjusted base; 0% against the reported base), +25% (2027), +16% (2028), +15% (2029), +13% (2030); about 12% a year from 2025 to 2030. Below target over the period, at target from 2027 to 2029 only because of the low starting point.
  • "At 2023 earnings levels or higher in normal markets" (Q1 2026 call; SEK 472m of EBITA on the 2023 perimeter). My base reaches SEK 462m in 2028 and SEK 530m in 2029, but with S:t Eriks and Tornum still well below normal margins; on the existing perimeter (excluding new bolt-ons) I reach about SEK 540m only in 2030.
  • Net debt to adjusted EBITDA of 2.0x to 3.0x, not above 3.5x. 3.1x at Q3 2026; management expects seasonal cash flow to reduce it. My path is about 2.7x at end-2026 and below 2.0x from 2028, which leaves room for more acquisitions or a partial preference redemption than I assume.
  • Platform margin targets (Ettiketto 20%, Corroventa at least 22%, Communication at least 14%, S:t Eriks and Tornum 10%): my 2030 margins are below all five (16.7%, 16.9%, 12.2%, 5.5%, 3.0%).
  • Qualitative 2026 commentary ("tough conditions to persist through the rest of 2026"; no immediate market recovery at Tornum; cost measures to take effect within six to nine months): consistent with my 2026 and H1 2027 inputs.

Against the two brokers (FactSet, 22 October 2026): revenue 2% to 4% below both in every year; adjusted EBITA in line with DNB Carnegie in 2026 (SEK 318m against SEK 321m) and 2028 (SEK 462m against SEK 454m), 2.5% below it in 2027 (SEK 398m against SEK 409m), and above Nordea's apparent EBITA (about SEK 360m in 2027, see below).

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

  1. Company reported to my adjusted basis. Reported EBITA plus items affecting comparability (2026: about SEK 47m, of which SEK 29m in Q3) gives adjusted EBITA. Reported EBIT plus amortisation of acquired intangibles (about SEK 100m in 2026) plus those items gives adjusted EBITA. Reported EPS for ordinary shares already deducts the preference dividend (Q1 2026: profit SEK 5m, EPS SEK -0.16). Adjusted EPS adds back amortisation and items after tax.
  2. DNB Carnegie (EPS SEK 1.98, 2.30, 2.82; 19 October 2026). Adds back amortisation of acquired intangibles (SEK 100m, 112m, 112m) and items affecting comparability, deducts minorities and, in 2027 and 2028, the preference dividend: SEK 160m - 64m + 87m = SEK 183m, or SEK 2.31 (2027). Its 2026 figure does not reconcile on the same method: continuing net profit of SEK 87m - 64m + 79m + 15m = SEK 117m, or about SEK 1.47, not SEK 1.98. DNB's EBITDA (SEK 560m, 668m, 725m) is on the IFRS 16 basis, like mine.
  3. Nordea (EPS SEK 0.62, 2.02, 2.70; 2 October 2026). FactSet's EBIT and EBITDA consensus are the means of the two brokers, so Nordea's lines can be backed out: EBIT about SEK 239m, 360m and 426m and EBITDA SEK 388m, 525m and 587m. Its EBITDA is about SEK 160m to 170m below DNB's, which fits EBITDA after lease costs (not IFRS 16), and its "EBIT" looks like EBITA. Its 2027 EPS fits adding back amortisation and deducting the preference dividend on an EBITA of about SEK 360m; its 2026 EPS looks to be after items affecting comparability.
  4. FactSet consensus. EPS SEK 1.30 (2026) averages two inconsistent 2026 figures and should not be used. EBIT (SEK 221m, 329m, 384m) mixes DNB's reported EBIT (after amortisation) with Nordea's EBITA-like figure. EBITDA (SEK 474m, 596m, 656m) mixes an IFRS 16 figure with one after lease costs. On my basis the like-for-like 2027 range is adjusted EBITA SEK 360m (Nordea) to SEK 409m (DNB) and adjusted EPS SEK 2.02 to 2.30; mine are SEK 398m and SEK 2.24.
  5. Leverage. The company's 3.1x excludes leases and preference shares and uses pro-forma EBITDA after lease costs. DNB's 4.5x to 4.8x adds leases, put options and the preference shares at their redemption value (about SEK 1.0bn). The 7.4x in the August memo matches neither and should not be used.

Your cross-check: your own earnings per share and free cash flow for each of the five fiscal years

Earnings per share is adjusted EPS for ordinary shareholders: (adjusted EBITA less net financial items) after tax, less minorities, less the SEK 64m preference dividend, over 79.4m shares; before amortisation of acquired intangibles and items affecting comparability. Reported EPS on the same assumptions is SEK 0.05, 1.13, 1.75, 2.38 and 2.99. Free cash flow is after tax, interest, lease principal, capital spending, working capital, items affecting comparability and the preference dividend; before ordinary dividends and acquisitions (before the preference dividend: SEK 149m, 231m, 278m, 312m and 359m). 2026 is continuing operations for a full year.
