Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Volati AB (VOLO-SE), a Swedish decentralised industrial acquirer which, after distributing Salix Group to its shareholders on 15 June 2026, owns five platforms: Ettiketto Group (self-adhesive labels and labelling machines in the Nordics, Germany, the UK and the Netherlands, including Interket and Clever Etiketten), Communication (telecom masts, towers and mounting hardware through Scanmast and MAFI), Corroventa (water-damage drying and moisture-measurement equipment, rental and sales, including Tramex), S:t Eriks Group (precast concrete and natural stone for Swedish infrastructure and landscaping) and Tornum Group (grain drying, handling and storage systems, including SIMEZA silos); it also has listed preference shares with a fixed SEK 40 annual dividend.

Request: T-D1

Peers: Storskogen Group (STOR.B-SE; leveraged Nordic acquirer that repaired its balance sheet from 2022 and restarted acquisitions); Sdiptech (SDIP.B-SE; divestment-led repair and preference-share redemption in March 2026); AddLife (ALIF.B-SE; cut leverage from about 3.5x to 2.2x through cash flow before resuming deals); Indus Holding (INH-DE; buy-and-hold group with fixed-cost under-absorption in German downturns); Lifco (LIFCO.B-SE), Indutrade (INDT-SE) and Addtech (ADDT.B-SE) (benchmarks for integrating acquired companies and for downturn cost behaviour); CCL Industries (CCL.B-CA), Multi-Color Corporation (private) and All4Labels (private, Triton-backed) (label consolidators, for the record of lifting acquired converters' margins); Ag Growth International (AFN-CA), Kepler Weber (KEPL3-BR), AGCO (AGCO-US, owner of GSI and Cimbria) and PETKUS (private) (grain-storage competitors of Tornum, for margin recovery after downturns); Benders and Starka (private Swedish concrete-products competitors of S:t Eriks); Heidelberg Materials (HEI-DE; Sweden's only cement producer and a precast competitor); Vestum (VESTUM-SE; construction-exposed Swedish acquirer that restructured in 2025 to 2026). Operating conditions: a prolonged Swedish residential construction downturn with Riksbank rates at 1.75% and a hiking bias in late 2026; Swedish public infrastructure investment (national transport plan) against slowing municipal water and wastewater spending; a European farm capital-spending freeze with low grain prices, delayed EU grant payments and proposed post-2027 cuts to the Common Agricultural Policy; overcapacity and price discounting in grain storage equipment after the 2020 to 2023 boom; input-cost pressure from EU ETS-driven cement prices, the EU steel safeguard from 1 July 2026 and label-stock and freight shocks; dry versus wet years for water-damage restoration; consolidation of European label converting under the EU Packaging and Packaging Waste Regulation (applying since 12 August 2026). Inputs: (1) How long have Ettiketto's earlier acquisitions of label converters taken to reach the platform's margin level, through which cost lines (procurement, press consolidation, co-location), and how does that compare with the 24-month programme for Interket (acquired January 2026, about SEK 450m of annual sales, total consideration SEK 494m per the Q3 2026 interim report) and the two-year programme for Clever Etiketten (acquired 2025, about SEK 290m of sales)? What did Volati say on 23 October 2026 about the SEK 19m of Q3 2026 restructuring costs for co-location and the savings expected from Q1 2027? (2) What evidence is there that the cost programmes launched at S:t Eriks and Tornum in Q1 2026, which management said would take effect within six to nine months (Q1 2026 call, 29 April 2026), have delivered, given S:t Eriks' Q3 2026 EBITA margin of 5% on sales down 9% and Tornum's Q3 2026 EBITA of SEK -9m on sales down 17% (Q3 2026 interim report)? (3) How have precast concrete producers' margins recovered after deliberate production cuts to release inventory, and how have grain-storage suppliers' margins recovered after downturns (AGI 2015 to 2017 and 2019 to 2020, Kepler Weber 2015 to 2016, AGCO grain and protein 2019 to 2020), and how long did each take? (4) How did leveraged Nordic acquirers (Storskogen, Sdiptech, AddLife) move from their leverage peaks back to acquiring, what came first (EBITDA recovery, divestments, dividend cuts or equity), and how long did it take? (5) How has Volati's EBITA compared with sell-side estimates in each year from 2021 to 2025 and in each quarter of 2026, and in which platforms did the differences arise? (6) Has Volati, or any of its platforms, ever lifted margins in a downturn by moving product mix (for example S:t Eriks' shift from standard landscaping products to specified infrastructure and low-carbon products such as evoZero and the Cemvision partnership)? Focus: Whether the earnings recovery the market now expects for 2027 rests on things Volati has demonstrably done before (integrating acquired label converters, cutting costs locally, deleveraging through cash flow), or on things it would need to happen to it (a Swedish construction recovery, an end to the grain-storage price war). And how long comparable integrations and balance-sheet repairs have actually taken.

Request: T-D2

Peers: Storskogen Group (STOR.B-SE; construction-exposed Services units and published margin drop-through); Sdiptech (SDIP.B-SE; low-volatility infrastructure niches, a reference for narrow forecast errors); Indus Holding (INH-DE; segment-level volatility in German downturns); Lifco (LIFCO.B-SE) and Indutrade (INDT-SE) (reference dispersion for diversified acquirers); Idun Industrier (IDUN.B-SE) and Vestum (VESTUM-SE) (small Swedish acquirers with construction exposure); Ag Growth International (AFN-CA) and Kepler Weber (KEPL3-BR) (dispersion of grain-equipment earnings); CCL Industries (CCL.B-CA) (label earnings stability); Heidelberg Materials (HEI-DE) and Benders (private) (Nordic building-materials volatility). Operating conditions: a Riksbank decision in November 2026 with a hiking bias, and the alternative of rate cuts in 2027; Swedish housing starts at a fraction of their 2021 level against civil engineering investment forecast to grow (Veidekke market update, 21 October 2026); European farm investment frozen by low grain prices and policy uncertainty; random autumn and winter flooding; lumpy telecom, defence and infrastructure project deliveries for masts; the Swedish food VAT cut of April 2026 and the return of food inflation expected by year-end 2026 (Danske Bank, 18 October 2026). Inputs: (1) Which drivers have historically explained most of the difference between Volati's forecast and actual EBITA: S:t Eriks' volume and production rates, Tornum's project deliveries and pricing, Corroventa's weather, Communication's project timing, Ettiketto's input costs, or head-office and one-off costs? (2) How wide has the quarter-to-quarter and year-to-year dispersion of EBITA been for each of the five platforms since 2019, including S:t Eriks' EBITA falling from SEK 47m to SEK 20m in Q2 2026 on sales up 1%, Tornum's EBITA margin falling from 9.1% (2023) to 0.2% (2024), and Corroventa's Q2 2026 margin of 3%? (3) How sensitive are group EPS to interest rates and to the fixed SEK 64m preference dividend, and how have comparable fixed claims amplified EPS dispersion at other leveraged acquirers? (4) What does the historical dispersion of outcomes look like for diversified acquirers whose forecasts rested on one or two cyclical units recovering, and how often were such forecasts met? (5) How do acquisitions restarting after a leverage peak change the spread of outcomes, and how did consensus treat them at Storskogen and Sdiptech? (6) Which events widened or narrowed the spread of outcomes for Nordic building-materials and grain-equipment suppliers in past cycles (rate turns, subsidy changes, capacity exits)? Focus: Whether the change in Volati's earnings over the next 18 months is spread across several independent drivers or rests on one or two cyclical ones, and how wide and how skewed the range of outcomes has been for comparable companies when their forecasts depended on the same drivers.

Request: T-I1

Peers: Storskogen Group (STOR.B-SE; Q3 2026 report 23 October 2026); Sdiptech (SDIP.B-SE; Q3 2026 report 23 October 2026); Lifco (LIFCO.B-SE), Indutrade (INDT-SE) and Addtech (ADDT.B-SE) (Q3 2026 reports and guidance language); Indus Holding (INH-DE; Q3 report 12 November 2026); Salix Group (Volati's former subsidiary, listed June 2026); Ag Growth International (AFN-CA) and Kepler Weber (KEPL3-BR) (latest grain-equipment results and outlook); Heidelberg Materials (HEI-DE; Q3 2026 pricing); CCL Industries (CCL.B-CA) (label pass-through). Operating conditions: the Riksbank decision in November 2026; Swedish construction and civil engineering demand into 2027; European farm investment and grant payments; Middle East shipping costs and oil prices; the return of Swedish food inflation after the April 2026 VAT cut; the year-end 2026 goodwill impairment test; Volati's first full year as a separate continuing group. Inputs: (1) Volati gives no quarterly or annual guidance; what did management say on 23 October 2026 about Q4 2026, 2027, leverage (3.1x at Q3 2026 against a 2.0x to 3.0x target and a 3.5x maximum), the dividend under the 10% to 30% payout policy against the SEK 2.00 paid for 2025, acquisitions, and any plan for a capital markets day? (2) How did DNB Carnegie (Hold, target SEK 26, 19 October 2026; EPS SEK 1.98, 2.30 and 2.82; EBITDA SEK 560m, 668m and 725m) and Nordea Equity Research (Buy, target SEK 39, 2 October 2026; EPS SEK 0.62, 2.02 and 2.70; EBITDA SEK 388m, 525m and 587m) change their estimates, ratings and targets after the Q3 2026 report, and on what definitions (amortisation, items affecting comparability, the preference dividend, IFRS 16)? (3) Has any other broker initiated coverage since October 2026? (4) Do the brokers' models include future acquisitions or a redemption of the preference shares (redeemable at SEK 625 a share), and what does each assume for Tornum and S:t Eriks margins in 2027 and 2028? (5) What do peers' Q3 2026 results and outlooks imply for Volati's platforms: Storskogen's construction-exposed units, AGI's farm order intake (30 September 2026) and pricing, Kepler Weber's margins, Heidelberg's Swedish cement pricing, CCL's label pass-through? (6) What is Volati's record of informal outlook statements against outcomes (for example its expectation that construction would return to organic growth during 2025 to 2026)? Focus: Which way the two brokers' 2026 and 2027 estimates moved after the Q3 2026 report and why, and whether anything in management's commentary or the peers' latest results points to consensus being raised, held or cut at the Q4 2026 report in February 2027.

Request: T-I2

Peers: Storskogen Group (STOR.B-SE; the "repaired balance sheet buying again" story); Sdiptech (SDIP.B-SE; the clean reset and preference redemption); Indus Holding (INH-DE; reset plus a commodity windfall); Salix Group (Volati's former subsidiary, for the "best business handed out" comparison); Lifco (LIFCO.B-SE) and Addtech (ADDT.B-SE) (how investors frame proven compounders against leveraged ones); Smiths Group (SMIN-GB), TKH Group (TWEKA-NL) and thyssenkrupp (TKA-DE) (break-ups where the remainder re-rated); Tången (young Swedish serial acquirer) and Röko (ROKO.B-SE) (new acquirers competing for Nordic targets). Operating conditions: a risk-off market favouring defensive and low-leverage stocks; Swedish compounder multiples tracking the gap between earnings yields and swap rates (DNB Carnegie, October 2026); post-spin shareholder rotation and low liquidity (average daily value traded about USD 0.3m to 0.4m); a sector that has rewarded break-ups where the remainder is the better half and balance-sheet repairs followed by renewed acquisitions. Inputs: the dynamics to test are: that Volati "handed out its best business" (Salix) and kept the cyclical platforms and the preference shares; leverage of 3.1x on the company's definition and about 4.5x to 4.8x including preference shares and leases (DNB Carnegie); the self-help recovery at S:t Eriks and Tornum (cost programmes, production realignment) and its timing; the integration of Interket and Clever at Ettiketto and the co-location savings from 2027; the Tornum price war with AGI and Kepler Weber; S:t Eriks' price-cost squeeze from cement and aggregates and Heidelberg Materials' acquisition of NCC's aggregates and asphalt business (7 October 2026); the possibility that falling leverage restarts acquisitions; the possibility of refinancing or redeeming the preference shares; the dividend cut; the different EPS definitions used by DNB Carnegie and Nordea and the resulting FactSet consensus; the largest shareholder's (Karl Perlhagen, about 39% of votes) departure from the board in November 2025; the absence of a capital markets day for the continuing group. Focus: Which of these dynamics investors in Volati and in Nordic serial acquirers are discussing, and which no one is: in particular whether anyone discusses a restart of acquisitions or a preference-share refinancing as falling leverage allows, and whether the market has noticed that the two brokers' 2026 EPS figures are on different bases.

Request: OWN-1

Question: What exactly did Volati AB (VOLO-SE) report in its Q3 2026 interim report and presentation of 23 October 2026: group net sales, organic and acquired growth, EBITA before and after items affecting comparability, EBITDA, amortisation of acquired intangibles, net financial items, tax, minorities, profit and EPS for ordinary shareholders, operating cash flow, net debt and its definition (including or excluding leases), the leverage calculation behind the reported 3.1x, lease liabilities, platform return on capital employed, and management's statements on Q4 2026, 2027, cost programmes, integration savings, acquisitions and the dividend?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Volati AB (VOLO-SE), what are the full terms of the 1,603,774 listed preference shares (SEK 40 annual dividend, redemption price of SEK 625 after May 2025, voting rights, any step-ups or conversion rights), how have their price and yield moved since the Salix distribution on 15 June 2026, what have management and the board said about redeeming or refinancing them, what are the size, maturity, margin and covenants of the credit facility with Nordea and SEB effective 15 June 2026, and what precedents exist of Swedish listed companies redeeming or refinancing preference shares (including Sdiptech's redemption of 1,750,000 preference shares for SEK 184m in March 2026), how they financed it and what it did to their EPS and valuation?

Request: OWN-3

Question: What is the outlook for 2027 and 2028 for the Swedish markets served by Volati AB's (VOLO-SE) S:t Eriks Group: housing starts (Boverket, SCB and industry forecasts), civil engineering and transport infrastructure investment (Trafikverket, Veidekke's market update of 21 October 2026), municipal water and wastewater investment, landscaping and builders' merchant demand; and how are Swedish cement, aggregate and ready-mix concrete prices developing after Heidelberg Materials' clinker consolidation at Slite (announced 19 March 2026), its 30-year Slite quarry permit (8 September 2026) and its agreement to buy NCC's aggregates and asphalt business (7 October 2026), and how are Benders and Starka pricing and adding capacity?

Request: OWN-4

Question: What is the outlook for European grain handling, drying and storage investment in 2027 and 2028 as it bears on Volati AB's (VOLO-SE) Tornum Group: order intake and pricing at Ag Growth International, Kepler Weber, AGCO's grain and protein business (GSI, Cimbria), PETKUS and other European suppliers; which competitors are discounting and by how much; whether capacity is leaving the market; the timing and content of the post-2027 Common Agricultural Policy and Nordic nitrogen rules; delays in EU and Swedish investment grant payments; Lantmännen's and other Nordic cooperatives' investment plans; and any Ukraine reconstruction demand for grain storage?

Request: OWN-5

Question: For Volati AB's (VOLO-SE) Ettiketto Group, what is the evidence on the integration of Interket Group (UK and the Netherlands, acquired January 2026) and Clever Etiketten (Germany, acquired 2025), the price sensitivity of their customers compared with Ettiketto's Nordic base, label demand and pricing in Germany, the UK and the Netherlands, the effect of the EU Packaging and Packaging Waste Regulation on small label converters' willingness to sell, label demand from nicotine-pouch makers and the regulatory and tax outlook for nicotine pouches in Sweden and the EU, and Swedish food retail volumes after the April 2026 VAT cut (Danske Bank, 18 October 2026)?

Request: OWN-6

Question: For Volati AB's (VOLO-SE) Corroventa platform, how do water-damage insurance claims and restoration activity in the Nordics, the UK, North America and Australia vary between dry and wet years (2022 to 2026), how have rental day rates and equipment prices behaved in those years at Corroventa and at Dantherm and Trotec, how large and how profitable is the professional moisture-meter market that Tramex (acquired July 2026) serves, and what did Corroventa report for Q3 2026 and say about Q4 2026 demand?

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 30 October 2026, 09:11 to 09:31 UK, Fable, richie login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Volati AB (VOLO-SE): Third and fourth sets of questions, predictability of the forecasts; guidance, consensus and narrative

As at 2026-10-29. Author: Investment Analyst, third and fourth sets. Reader: the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo for Richard.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Preface (not an answer): verification of the Q3 2026 figures, the Financial Analyst's model gaps, and where I differ from the earlier sets

  1. The second set's Q3 2026 group totals, verified against the AlphaSense reports that cite the interim report of 23 October 2026

The second set derived its Q3 group figures from segment data and asked for them to be checked. They hold, with small differences that change nothing: net sales SEK 1,176m (the second set used 1,175m), up 12%, organic -3%, acquired growth SEK 149m; EBITA before items affecting comparability SEK 87m at 7.4% (the second set derived 89m), up 14% to 15% on SEK 76m; items affecting comparability SEK -29m (SEK 19m for the Ettiketto co-location in Germany, SEK 8m transaction costs, about SEK 2m other); reported EBITA SEK 58m, down 23%; reported EBITDA SEK 115m; amortisation of acquired intangibles SEK 25m; EBIT SEK 33m; net financial items SEK -18m; tax SEK -5m; net profit from continuing operations SEK 11m; minorities nil; preference dividend SEK 16m; profit to ordinary shareholders SEK -5m, EPS SEK -0.07; nine-month ordinary EPS SEK 0.02 (down 97%). S:t Eriks' EBITA was SEK 14m (the second set used about 15m). Operating cash flow SEK 55m (SEK 40m a year earlier); rolling twelve-month operating cash flow SEK 321m; cash conversion 93%. Net debt SEK 1,331m, up SEK 185m in the quarter on Tramex and NicEtikett, divided by adjusted EBITDA of SEK 426m (the second set reverse-engineered about 430m) gives the reported 3.1x. The company's net debt includes SEK 57m of finance leases and excludes SEK 339m of operating lease liabilities (total lease liabilities SEK 396m: SEK 281m non-current, SEK 115m current), which confirms the second set's reading of the definition. Platform return on capital employed excluding goodwill, last twelve months: Communication 67% (44%), Ettiketto 39% (56%), Corroventa 33% (44%), S:t Eriks 3%, Tornum -14%.

  1. What has changed since the second set wrote (26 October 2026)

  • DNB Carnegie's Q3 review of 26 October 2026 ("In line if adjusting for large one-offs"): adjusted EPS cut 3% for 2026 to SEK 1.92, raised 1% for 2027 to SEK 2.32 and 2% for 2028 to SEK 2.88; target cut to SEK 25 from SEK 26; Hold kept. 2026 EBITA SEK 311m (6.5% margin) against SEK 321m before. Adjusted EBITDA SEK 644m (2027) and SEK 708m (2028), from 668m and 725m. Tornum's 2027 EBITA margin cut to -1.3% from 0.0% (2028 to 0.7% from 1.0%); S:t Eriks' 2027 margin cut to 2.6% from 3.5% and 2028 to 3.3% from 4.1%. Reported EBITA was 29% below DNB's forecast because of the SEK 29m of one-offs. DNB's long-run model assumes 5% acquired growth and 2% organic growth a year from 2027 to 2032, a 4.5% interest rate, and no redemption of the preference shares; it treats the preference shares as debt (leverage 3.9x for 2027 on that basis; 4.5x for 2026).
  • Nordea Equity Research (Buy, SEK 39, 2 October 2026) has published nothing after the Q3 report that AlphaSense holds, and no third broker has initiated. DNB is the only active voice.
  • Macro: DNB expects the Riksbank to raise its policy rate from 1.75% towards 2.4% by Q3 2027; Morgan Stanley expects 25bp in November 2026 to 2.00%. Swedish consumer confidence fell from 102.3 to 94.3 in October (Nordea). Veidekke (21 October 2026) forecasts the Swedish civil engineering market up 6% in 2027 and 6% in 2028 by value, railways up 9% and 8%; Morgan Stanley (12 October 2026) says European civil engineering activity has turned negative. Swedish cement prices rose 3.8% year on year in August 2026 and aggregates 7.9% (Morgan Stanley). Heidelberg Materials agreed on 7 October 2026 to buy NCC's aggregates and asphalt business (106 sites, 44 plants) for SEK 5.5bn. Ag Growth International's order book was down 22% at June 2026 but it reported an order pick-up in late 2026 (30 September 2026); Jefferies (7 April 2026) puts the agricultural equipment cycle bottom in late 2026 or early 2027. The post-2027 Common Agricultural Policy will not be adopted until Q1 2027. The Swedish food VAT cut runs to December 2027.

  1. The Financial Analyst's model: the flagged gaps explained, and the input that must change

The summary flags that every year's EPS and free cash flow differ materially from the second set's cross-check (for example FY2027 EPS SEK 6.11 against SEK 2.24; free cash flow SEK 448.6m against SEK 167m). The gaps have three causes. Two are mechanical features of the model's base and one is a forecast input that is wrong against the base the model holds. Each is explained in full because the model's total shareholder return figures (+272% to the 18-month column) are an artefact of them and must not reach the memo.

Cause 1, the forecast input that is wrong: the Part 3 quarterly revenue growth for Q3 and Q4 2026 is written against the continuing-operations base, but the model holds the pre-spin consolidated quarters as the base. The model's FY2026 revenue is SEK 6,900m against the second set's SEK 4,721m. The model's H1 2026 is the reported continuing SEK 2,355m (1,020 + 1,335), so its H2 2026 is SEK 4,545m, against the second set's H2 of about SEK 2,366m (Q3 reported 1,176; Q4 about 1,190). The reason is visible in the financials block: FactSet has restated Q1 and Q2 2025 to continuing operations (993 and 1,201) but still holds Q3 2025 at SEK 2,082m and Q4 2025 at SEK 2,020m, the consolidated figures including Salix. The second set's growth inputs of +11.4% and +12.4% were written against the continuing comparatives (about 1,051 and 1,064); applied to the consolidated quarters they roughly double H2 2026, and every later year inherits the error through the annual growth inputs (FY2027 7,197 = 6,900 x 1.043). This one error inflates the model's FY2026 EBITDA by about SEK 257m (807 against 550), its EBITA by the same amount (the depreciation input is an absolute figure), its pre-tax profit by the same, and so every year's EPS and cash flow by roughly 45% to 50% of the intended level. The fix is in inputs-changed.md: Q3 2026 growth -43.5% and Q4 2026 growth -41.1% against the bases the model holds, which land the model on Q3 2026 SEK 1,176m, Q4 2026 SEK 1,190m and FY2026 SEK 4,721m; the 2027 quarterly inputs then produce SEK 4,924m, which is the second set's FY2027. Two cautions for the Financial Analyst. First, if the model's FactSet history is refreshed after the Q3 2026 report, FactSet will restate Q3 2025 to about SEK 1,051m and later Q4 2025 to about SEK 1,064m; the inputs must then revert to +11.9% and +11.8%. Second, the Part 1 FY2026 annual growth of +9.6% is correct on the continuing basis (4,721 against 4,309) and is not what sets the FY2026 level in the model (the model displays -18.0% because it builds FY2026 from the quarters); it must only ever be read against the continuing FY2025 base. The cleanest permanent fix is to restate the model's FY2025 and H2 2025 history to continuing operations (FY2025 revenue SEK 4,309m, EBITDA SEK 512m), so that all growth inputs sit on one basis. The target levels are the check: FY2026 SEK 4,721m, EBITDA SEK 550m, EBITA SEK 318m; FY2027 SEK 4,923m, EBITDA SEK 644m, EBITA SEK 398m.

Cause 2, a model-base matter, not an input: the opening working capital and the FY2025 history are pre-spin. The model's FY2026 free cash flow of SEK 767.8m reconciles exactly as EBITDA 807.3 less tax 110.9, interest 71, capital spending 127 and lease payments 80 (= 418.4) plus a working-capital release of SEK 349m. That release is the difference between the model's closing working capital (19.5% of 6,900 = 1,346) and an opening balance of SEK 1,695m, which is FactSet's consolidated end-2025 net working capital, Salix included. After Cause 1 is fixed the closing balance becomes about SEK 921m and the spurious release would grow to about SEK 770m. The Financial Analyst must rebase the opening working capital (and opening net debt) to the continuing balance sheet: net working capital SEK 916m at Q2 2026 (SEK 1,331m net debt excluding operating leases at Q3 2026, or SEK 1,727m including the SEK 396m of lease liabilities). The second set's FY2026 cash flow assumes a working-capital absorption of SEK 16m for the year, so on a correct base the model's FY2026 free cash flow should land near the second set's SEK 149m before the preference dividend. From FY2027 the model's free cash flow has no working-capital artefact: SEK 448.6m is exactly the inflated FY2027 EBITDA less tax, interest, capital spending, leases and the 19.5% working-capital build on the inflated revenue growth; with Cause 1 fixed it falls to about SEK 231m before the preference dividend, which is the second set's figure.

Cause 3, a model-scope matter, flagged by the second set and by Richard's note of 16 October 2026: the model deducts neither the SEK 64m preference dividend nor minorities (SEK 10m to 13m). After Cause 1 is fixed, the model's FY2027 EPS would be about SEK 3.17 ((398 - 73) x 0.775 / 79.4) against the second set's SEK 2.24; the residual SEK 0.93 is (64 + 10) / 79.4. The same SEK 0.93 to 0.97 gap recurs every year and SEK 64m to 77m a year in free cash flow. This is not a disagreement about the forecast. The memo must deduct SEK 0.93 to 0.97 from any model EPS and SEK 64m from any model free cash flow, or treat the model's EPS as "before preference dividend and minorities" wherever it is quoted.

What this does to the return at the guideline fair multiple. The model's +272% to 31 December 2027 applies the 11.21x guideline multiple to roughly its FY2029 EPS of SEK 8.15, which carries all three errors. Indicatively, on the second set's cross-check: at the 18-month column the two-year-forward EPS is FY2029 adjusted EPS SEK 3.57 (after the preference dividend, before amortisation of acquired intangibles); at 11.21x that is SEK 40.0, plus the SEK 0.30 dividend paid in 2027, about SEK 40.3 against SEK 24.75, a total return of about +63% (about +47% annualised over the 14 months to the column date). Of that, earnings growth contributes about +29% (two-year-forward EPS from about SEK 2.76 today to SEK 3.57), the multiple about +25% (from about 9.0x on our EPS to 11.21x) and dividends about +1%. At the three-year column the model extrapolates beyond FY2030; on FY2030 adjusted EPS of SEK 4.22 at 11.21x plus SEK 1.60 of dividends, the value is about SEK 48.9, a return of about +98% (about +29% a year). On reported EPS (after amortisation; FY2029 SEK 2.38) the 18-month value would be only about SEK 27, a +9% return: the basis of the multiple decides the answer. The guideline multiple's history was built on FactSet consensus EPS, which for DNB adds back amortisation, so the adjusted basis is the consistent one, but the memo must say so. The model must be re-run before any of these figures is used; they are given so the memo analyst knows the order of magnitude and that the model's +272% is wrong by a factor of four.

  1. Where I differ from the first and second sets

  1. Consensus does model some acquisitions. The second set treats the acquisition restart as unmodelled by consensus. DNB's model assumes 5% acquired growth a year from 2027 to 2032 alongside 2% organic. The restart is therefore partly in the one active broker's long-term model, although its 2027 and 2028 revenue (SEK 5,118m and 5,268m, growth of 6% and 3%) carry little of it. The stream of "unpriced upgrades" from acquisitions is smaller than the second set implied; the upside sits in the pace, not the existence, of the restart.
  2. Covenant headroom is thin today and wide only later. The second set says the 3.5x maximum needs EBITDA about 25% below its end-2026 figure. On the Q3 2026 balance sheet the headroom is an 11% fall in adjusted EBITDA (426 to 380) with net debt flat at SEK 1,331m. The comfort depends on the Q4 2026 cash release arriving before any shock, and on no further acquisitions before it does. I treat the next four months as the window of maximum balance-sheet fragility.
  3. The ordinary dividend is more likely to be near zero than the second set's SEK 0.30. The policy is 10% to 30% of net profit attributable to ordinary shareholders; reported 2026 profit to ordinary holders is about SEK 4m (nine-month EPS SEK 0.02). The board exceeded the policy for 2025 (53% payout) and may again, but FactSet's SEK 0.56 is unlikely, and a token or nil dividend is a live negative headline for February 2027. I keep SEK 0.30 as the central input because it is immaterial to the cash path (SEK 24m) and the range is SEK 0 to 0.50.
  4. Ettiketto's price lag is mostly, not entirely, behind it. The second set reads Q3 as showing the legacy margin back at 17.5%. The Q3 report also says higher material and label-stock costs were not fully offset by price. I treat cost recovery as largely done, with residual risk from freight (8x pre-conflict levels, Jefferies 29 October 2026) and European gas (EUR 85/MWh).
  5. The first set's 5% to 10% cut to 2026 consensus has partly happened (DNB -3% on EPS and EBITA after Q3); the second set's "0% to 3% on adjusted EBITA" was the better call on size. Both were right that the cut comes in the cyclical platforms' margins.

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Volati AB (VOLO-SE), a Swedish decentralised industrial acquirer which, after distributing Salix Group to its shareholders on 15 June 2026, owns five platforms: Ettiketto Group (self-adhesive labels and labelling machines in the Nordics, Germany, the UK and the Netherlands), Communication (telecom masts, towers and mounting hardware through Scanmast and MAFI), Corroventa (water-damage drying equipment and moisture meters, including Tramex), S:t Eriks Group (precast concrete and natural stone for Swedish infrastructure and landscaping) and Tornum Group (grain drying, handling and storage systems, including SIMEZA silos); it also has 1,603,774 listed preference shares with a fixed SEK 40 annual dividend.

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Volati AB (VOLO-SE), what were the Q4 2025 net sales, EBITA and EBITA margin of each continuing platform (Ettiketto Group, Communication, Corroventa, S:t Eriks Group, Tornum Group) and of head office, as restated in Volati's segment-reporting presentation of 21 April 2026 and its Q4 2025 year-end report of 12 February 2026 (including which S:t Eriks infrastructure deliveries were postponed from Q4 2025 and whether they were later delivered), and what do the earliest available indicators for Q4 2026 show for each platform: Swedish grocery retail volumes for October and November 2026 (Statistics Sweden, Axfood, ICA), Haypp Group's nicotine-pouch volumes for Q3 2026, Nordic and UK rainfall and flood events in October and November 2026 (SMHI, the Met Office, Nordic insurers' weather-claims commentary), Byggfakta's construction-starts indicator and SCB housing starts for Q3 and October 2026, Trafikverket tender awards in H2 2026, the CEMA business barometer for October and November 2026, Ag Growth International's and Kepler Weber's Q3 2026 results and order commentary, and any Tornum order announcements since 23 October 2026?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Volati AB's (VOLO-SE) listed preference share (Nasdaq Stockholm, SEK 40 annual dividend paid quarterly, redeemable at the company's option at SEK 625 after May 2025), what has its daily closing price, implied dividend yield and trading volume been from 1 January 2026 to date, and in particular from 15 June 2026 (the Salix Group distribution) and around the Q3 2026 report of 23 October 2026; how does its implied yield compare with other Swedish listed preference shares, hybrid bonds and SEK corporate bonds of similar size over the same period; what did Volati disclose in its 2025 annual report (12 February 2026), its Q2 2026 report (16 July 2026) and its Q3 2026 report (23 October 2026) about the maturity, size, margin grid, sustainability-linked terms and financial covenants of the SEK 2.5 billion credit facility with Nordea and SEB effective 15 June 2026; and has any holder, broker or governance adviser publicly commented on the preference shares' terms, safety or redemption since June 2026?

Request: OWN-3

Question: Who are the 20 largest holders of Volati AB's (VOLO-SE) ordinary shares and of its preference shares as at 30 September 2026 (Holdings, Modular Finance, Euroclear Sweden and the company's own shareholder list), how have the holdings of each changed since 31 March 2026 and since the Salix Group distribution of 15 June 2026 (including Karl Perlhagen, who resigned from the board on 19 November 2025, and chairman Patrik Wahlén), which institutional and index holders have reduced or exited since the distribution, what insider transactions have been reported to Finansinspektionen since 15 June 2026, what is the current free float and average daily turnover, and are there any public statements by Karl Perlhagen or his companies about his intentions for his holding since November 2025?
